# DECISIONS — ADR-lite log

One entry per decision. Only the Director (tactical) or the Owner (scope, contracts, model, evaluation validity) writes here. Reference the id from STATE.md, task reports and commit messages.

Format:

```
## DEC-<nnn> · <date> · <title>
Scope: tactical | contract | scope-cut | model | evaluation
Decided by: Director | Owner
Drafted by: <the agent or person who actually wrote this entry, when that is not the decider>
Propagated to: <every artifact edited in THIS commit because of this decision, or "— none needed">
Context: two sentences
Decision: one sentence
Consequences: what changes, what tests/contract files are updated
Supersedes: DEC-<nnn> | —
```

---

Decided by: Owner
Context: Architecture v3 (`docs/kien-truc-v3-14-ngay.html`) and `docs/chot-v3-14-ngay.md` incl. section F are the accepted baseline for the 14-day build.
Decision: Build exactly the D1–D20 + F1–F8 scope; cuts C1–C10 stand; cut order per context pack §10.
Consequences: All later decisions reference this baseline.

## DEC-001 · 2026-09-05 · Indexer environment corrected to the real IA1803 host
Scope: contract
Decided by: Owner
Context: The docs assumed the stock Wazuh layout (a `wazuh-indexer` service, CA at `/etc/wazuh-indexer/certs/root-ca.pem`). Verified on IA1803: `wazuh-indexer.service` is `inactive`/`disabled`; the alert store is a plain OpenSearch listening on `https://127.0.0.1:9400`; the manager's `alerts.json` is shipped by **Logstash** (`/etc/logstash/conf.d/wazuh-opensearch.conf`) into `wazuh-alerts-4.x-YYYY.MM.dd`; the root CA is `/etc/logstash/opensearch-certs/root-ca.pem` (world-readable, OpenSearch's bundled demo CA `CN = Example Com Inc. Root CA`, valid to 2034-02-17); `/var/ossec/logs/alerts/alerts.json` is not readable without root.
Decision: Correct the environment assumptions in place; no config key, index pattern, invariant or D-item changes.
Consequences: Context pack §6.3 gains a verified-environment note and `INDEXER_CA` now points at `conf/root-ca.pem`; §11 and `HUONG-DAN-VAN-HANH.md` §0.5 name the real CA path and OpenSearch instead of `wazuh-indexer`; `prompts/P2.md` calls it the OpenSearch root CA; `kien-truc-v3-14-ngay.html` §3 and §5 carry the same correction. `INDEXER_INDEX="wazuh-alerts-*"` is unchanged and still matches. Two follow-ups: the demo CA is a limitation to record in `docs/limitations.md` during P8, and the D3/F2 `alerts.json` file fallback is unavailable to the app user, so the puller is the only path to history. **Qualified by DEC-014 (05/09):** "unavailable" holds for the *application*, which runs as `user1`; it does **not** hold for the *Owner*, who has root and has since read the archive. R2 is dead and G1 can be backfilled without `soc_ro`. The `Final-Project` reuse QUESTION still open in INBOX becomes DEC-002.
Supersedes: —

## DEC-002 · 2026-09-05 · `Final-Project` reuse: port the two modules that measured clean, rewrite the two that did not
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Owner
Context: The Owner ran `Final-Project`'s `wazuh_ingest`, `category_resolver` and `prompt_guard` against the context-pack §8 document (Python 3.12, pydantic 2.13, no DB) and measured each one. Two carry reusable substance — `category_resolver`'s technique/group/decoder/port mapping tables, and `prompt_guard.detect_injection` (detection-only, stdlib, never mutates) — while the parser returns 12 of the 20 §8 fields with `alert_time` taken from the wrong source, and `wrap_untrusted` misses G6′ on six counts.
Decision: Option A — port the mapping tables into `ingest/category.py` and `detect_injection` into `security/detector.py`, and write `ingest/wazuh_parser.py` and `security/wrap.py` fresh against §7.1 and §8.
Consequences: P2's parser task is authored as new code against the §8 expected-parse table, not as a patch, and must produce all 20 fields with `alert_time` from `fields.timestamp[0]` (`2026-08-16T17:56:56.130Z`), not `_source.timestamp`. The ported category tables need the explicit priority table of phase-1 §Block 5 added on top, because `Final-Project` resolves in `rule.mitre.id` order and therefore yields `suspicious_login` where C5 requires `ssh_brute_force` (T1110 outranks T1078) — the canonical fixture is the regression test. `security/wrap.py` is written to §7.1 as specified: `<untrusted_data nonce=… source=…>` delimiters, `secrets.token_hex(8)`, NFKC then HTML-escape, nonce stripped from content, `[truncated]` inside the block, one typed builder — none of it inherited. `Final-Project` stays a read-only reference; nothing imports from it, and it is not vendored into the repo. Closes P0 exit gate item 5. Resolves INBOX 2026-09-05 · P0 · QUESTION.
Supersedes: —

## DEC-003 · 2026-09-05 · Three database connection keys enter §6.3; Compose variables stay out
Scope: contract
Decided by: Director
Context: Context pack §6.3 listed 50 configuration keys and none of them said how to reach PostgreSQL, while §6.1 already fixes that the application connects as `app_rw` and migrations run as the owner role. `.env.example`, `docker-compose.yml`, `scripts/migrate.sh` and `backend/tests/conftest.py` all need a DSN, and P0-T02 could not ship without inventing key names.
Decision: Add exactly three keys to §6.3 — `DATABASE_URL` (app runtime, role `app_rw`), `DATABASE_URL_OWNER` (migration execution, owner role), `TEST_DATABASE_URL` (tests only) — and keep `POSTGRES_USER`, `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`, `POSTGRES_DB`, `DB_PORT` out of the contract as Compose-only variables.
Consequences: Context pack §6.3 gains the three keys and a note stating that the four Compose variables live only in `docker-compose.yml` and `.env.example` and are never read by `backend/app/infra/config.py`. `.env.example` (P0-T02) therefore carries 53 contract keys plus 4 Compose-only ones, and its acceptance check expects exactly those four as "extra". `backend/tests/conftest.py` (P0-T03) enforces the `_test` suffix on `TEST_DATABASE_URL` as a safety rail. P1's `infra/config.py` reads the three keys and none of the four. Resolves INBOX 2026-09-05 · P0-T02 · DECISION_REQUEST (option A, narrowed).
Supersedes: —

## DEC-004 · 2026-09-05 · The database moves onto §6.2's criticality vocabulary; `assets` gains `owner` and `role`
Scope: contract
Decided by: Director
Context: `assets.criticality` was CHECKed against `crown_jewel|high|normal|low` while §6.2 `structured_basis.asset_criticality` is `high|medium|low|unknown`. Gate step 2 (§7.3) compares `structured_basis` against DB facts field by field, so two vocabularies mean a permanent mismatch on that field and every verdict degrading to `needs_review`. Separately, §7.1 names "inventory lookup values (owner, role text)" as text that must be wrapped, but no column held them.
Decision: Change the database to match §6.2 — never the reverse. In P1: alter `assets.criticality` to `CHECK (criticality IN ('high','medium','low','unknown'))` and add `owner text NULL`, `role text NULL`.
Carried by: `013_assets_enrichment.sql` — settled by the Owner on 2026-09-05, closing the open point that `P0-tasks.md` §Hand-off to P1 item 1 had left to the P1 Planner. 013 becomes the single enrichment-tables migration and carries **both** this ruling **and** §6.1's `source`, `loaded_at`, `active` on `assets`, `identities` and `iocs`; one table's DDL is therefore never split across two migrations. The four previously planned migrations each shift up by one: `014_intake_cursor_heartbeat.sql`, `015_labels_reviews_notes_eval_health.sql`, `016_alter_alerts_jobs_llm_runs_users.sql` (which loses its `assets/identities/iocs` line to 013), `017_append_only_and_roles.sql`. P1's exit gate reads "migrations 013–017 apply on a clean DB". Nothing is built against the old numbering yet, so the renumbering costs only the plan documents listed below.
Consequences:
  - **The DDL being replaced**, verbatim from `docs/Schema/001_bang_nen.sql:23-29` (identical at `schema.sql:42-48`):
    ```sql
    CREATE TABLE assets (
      hostname     text        PRIMARY KEY,
      criticality  text        NOT NULL,
      updated_at   timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
      CONSTRAINT ck_assets_criticality
        CHECK (criticality IN ('crown_jewel','high','normal','low'))
    );
    ```
    The P1 migration drops `ck_assets_criticality`, re-adds it over `('high','medium','low','unknown')`, and adds the two nullable text columns. `001_bang_nen.sql` is an applied migration and is **not** edited; `schema.sql` is regenerated by `build_schema.py` after the new migration lands.
  - `conf/inventory.yaml.example` and `enrichment/inventory.validate()` (P0-T05) use `high|medium|low|unknown`; no mapping table is written, because there is nothing left to map.
  - G8′ "asset.criticality = high" now reads literally as `high`, and "asset not in inventory" stays a separate hard block. A stored `unknown` and an absent row both resolve to `unknown` for the model.
  - `owner` and `role` are read only inside an `<untrusted_data nonce=…>` block (§7.1) and never appear outside one; the linter (§7.2) enforces it.
  - **Two follow-ups this creates, to be settled before the code that depends on them is written:** the risk-score formula `docs/phase-4-enrichment.md:170` weights four tiers (`crown_jewel: 30, high: 20, normal: 5, low: 0`) and must be re-weighted onto three plus `unknown` before `soar/risk.py` is written in P2; and `kb/playbooks/malware.md:36` and `kb/playbooks/ssh_brute_force.md:34` both branch on `asset_context.criticality = crown_jewel` and must be rewritten when the Owner and the advisor review the playbooks in P3.
  Resolves INBOX 2026-09-05 · P0-T05 / P1 · DECISION_REQUEST (neither option as filed: the ruling inverts option C's direction of change and keeps option A's columns).
Supersedes: —

## DEC-005 · 2026-09-05 · Two tactical calls from the Planner pinned: migration directory and pytest invocation
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Context: `01-plan.md` and `prompts/P1.md` both left the migration directory open ("under `docs/Schema/migrations/` (or `backend/migrations/`)", "or wherever P0 decided"), and the P0 task cards had already committed to one answer in `scripts/migrate.sh` and the `Makefile`. Separately, `backend/pyproject.toml` is a tool-config file with no build backend, so pytest only resolves `app.*` when it is pointed at that file explicitly.
Decision: Migrations live flat in `docs/Schema/` next to `001`–`012`, and pytest is always invoked as `python3 -m pytest -c backend/pyproject.toml`.
Consequences: There is no `docs/Schema/migrations/` and no `backend/migrations/`; `MIGRATIONS_DIR ?= docs/Schema` in the `Makefile`, `MIG_DIR="${MIGRATIONS_DIR:-docs/Schema}"` in `scripts/migrate.sh`, and `build_schema.py`'s `^(\d{3})_.*\.sql$` glob picks up every new migration in that directory with no edit — which is also why `docs/Schema/schema.sql` stays regenerable and its 001–012 line numbers stay stable, since a new migration only ever appends. `PYTEST ?= $(PY) -m pytest -c backend/pyproject.toml` in the `Makefile`; every acceptance command in a task card, every CI step and every report uses that exact form, run from the repository root. `pythonpath = ["."]` resolves against rootdir `backend/`, so `backend/pyproject.toml` must not move to the repository root. `01-plan.md` line 46 and `prompts/P1.md` line 17 lose their "or" and name `docs/Schema/`.
Supersedes: —

## DEC-006 · 2026-09-05 · An acceptance command that cannot execute is the Director's defect, not the Coder's
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Context: P0-T01 shipped a correct `.gitignore` and was still returned CHANGES because acceptance #5, `git check-ignore -q .env backups eval/results conf/inventory.yaml conf/root-ca.pem && echo IGNORED`, exits 128 with `fatal: --quiet is only valid with a single pathname` on any git — `-q` has never accepted more than one pathname. The Coder measured it before and after their change, reported it accurately, and could not fix it, because no `.gitignore` content can change a git CLI constraint.
Decision: Acceptance commands are part of the card and are the Director's to get right; a card whose acceptance line fails on its own syntax is corrected in the card, and the Coder neither works around it nor is held to it.
Consequences: Acceptance #5 is rewritten as a per-path loop in both `P0-T01.prompt.md` and `P0-tasks.md`. The rewrite keeps `backups/` and `eval/results/` **with** trailing slashes — measured: both `.gitignore` patterns are directory-only and neither directory exists on disk, so the bare names the Reviewer proposed in INBOX option A report *not ignored* and that loop still fails. Two further corrections ride along, both measured on a clean `git worktree` at `1bb33ba`: the P0-T01 design note that said to verify the negation with `git check-ignore -v` is wrong, because `-v` prints `!conf/*.example` and exits **0** for a path that is not ignored — only `-q`'s exit code is semantically correct, and `P0-T05.prompt.md:94` already had it right; and acceptance #8 moves from `git log --oneline -3` to `git log --oneline main..HEAD`, because the fixed `-3` window is unsatisfiable by construction for a small task — it only becomes all-yours once you happen to have made three commits, so P0-T01 failed it honestly at two commits, then chased its own tail to pass it (`4a94b1f` → `1bb33ba`, and the report is still one commit stale at HEAD). Measured at `1bb33ba`, the merge-base form returns all four branch commits, every one prefixed `P0-T01:`, and stays true no matter how many commits a task needs. Going forward every acceptance line in a card is executed once by its author before the card is dispatched. Resolves INBOX 2026-09-05 · P0-T01 · QUESTION (option A, corrected: the loop is right, its pathnames were not).
Supersedes: —

## DEC-007 · 2026-09-05 · The seven design questions the pre-dispatch audit left open
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Context: The DEC-006 audit of P0-T03…T06 executed 37 acceptance commands and separated real defects (fixed under DEC-006) from seven questions that are design choices rather than errors. `README.md` §Conventions puts "minor design inside the frozen contracts" in the Director's sole rights, and none of the seven touches §6 — no schema, no `output_schemas.json`, no config **key**, no API route, no import rule, no job type, no event type.
Decision: Rule all seven now so the four cards dispatch without a Coder having to guess.
Consequences:
  1. **`conf/iocs.csv` comments are legal.** The loader skips blank lines and lines whose first non-space character is `#`, so the card's required note lives in the `.example` as a real comment. One condition in the reader, and it removes the footgun where an Owner comments their real `conf/iocs.csv` and silently gets a phantom IoC. A `#` inside a quoted field is not a comment; both cases are tested.
  2. **`INVENTORY_PATHS` is parsed with `json.loads`.** §6.3 and `.env.example` both store it as a JSON array, so it is read as one and must yield a list of `str`. Bad JSON, or JSON that is not a list of `str`, is a configuration error naming the key — never a silent fallback to the `conf/` defaults; only unset-or-empty falls back. This fixes how an existing §6.3 **value** is read; the key and its value are untouched, so §6.3 is not reopened.
  3. **`validate()` routes by content.** `.csv` → `validate_iocs`; a YAML file → `validate_assets` or `validate_identities` by its top-level key. Never by position (which mis-validates the moment `INVENTORY_PATHS` is reordered) and never by basename (which breaks on a rename). A file matching none returns one problem naming the path and the keys looked for.
  4. **`--save-samples` does not breach P0-T04's file scope.** "Do not touch any other file" governs files a Coder *authors*; `indexer_sample_rule<id>.json` is runtime output of the delivered tool, written only with that flag, only once credentials exist, and committed by the Owner. The Coder still authors exactly the four paths in "Files — create".
  5. **`--json` is defined**: the same three sections as the text report, as one JSON object on stdout and nothing else there. Exit codes unchanged (`0` ok, `2` config error), diagnostics stay on stderr, `INDEXER_PASSWORD` never appears in either form. It exists so P2's puller work and the Owner's retention answer can be scripted rather than retyped.
  6. **P0-T06 depends only on P0-T02.** The wave diagram in `P0-tasks.md` is scheduling preference, not dependency; T06's acceptance #1 already carries an escape hatch for P0-T03 being absent. Where the diagram and a card's `Depends on:` field disagree, the field wins. T06 may run in wave 2.
  7. **`<TASK_ID>.prompt.md` is the authoritative card.** DEC-006 makes the acceptance set the Director's deliverable, so it gets one home. `P0-tasks.md` is the Planner's index; where it restates an acceptance command that copy matches the prompt verbatim or is dropped. Coder executes the prompt, Reviewer verifies against the prompt, and a divergence is a Director defect, not a Coder's judgment call.
  Also corrected here: `STATE.md`'s status legend had dropped `approved`, contradicting `README.md` §Conventions (`approved` = Reviewer APPROVE, awaiting merge; `done` = merged by Director) and `prompts/reviewer.md`, which mandates it. The legend was the stale artefact, not the rows — and `approved` is what makes the approved-but-unmerged queue visible, which is exactly the state that let P0-T02 sit on a tree predating DEC-002…DEC-006. Legend restored; P0-T01 and P0-T02 stay `done` because they are merged.
Supersedes: —

## DEC-008 · 2026-09-05 · `CREATEDB` granted: the test database is real, and `make migrate` is broken
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Owner (grant) · Director (consequences)
Context: The Owner granted `CREATEDB` to `user1` and verified it end to end. Re-measured here before recording: `rolcreatedb = t`, and createdb → CREATE TABLE → INSERT → SELECT → dropdb all pass on PostgreSQL 16.15 with no docker. This closes the last blocker standing between P0-T03 and a real database.
Decision: Close INBOX `2026-09-05 · P0 / P1 · BLOCKER` as **option A**. P0-T03's acceptance becomes a real DB run; a skip is now a failure.
Consequences:
  - **The DSN in the grant report does not work as written.** `postgresql://user1@127.0.0.1:5432/soc_test` fails with `fe_sendauth: no password supplied` — TCP to localhost requires a password on this host. The socket form does not: `postgresql:///soc_test` connects as `user1` passwordless, as does `postgresql://user1@/soc_test?host=/var/run/postgresql`. **`TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql:///soc_test` is the recorded value.** `.env.example` keeps its compose default (still correct when docker is available) and gains one comment line naming the no-docker form; no key is added or renamed, so §6.3 stays closed.
  - **`make migrate` exits 3 on a clean database** — found within minutes of the grant, and invisible before it. `schema.sql` is migrations 001–012 concatenated, but only `001`–`007` carry an `INSERT INTO schema_migrations`; `008`–`012` never record themselves. The loop therefore re-applies `008` and PostgreSQL rejects it (`constraint "ck_audit_event_type" ... already exists`). This breaks P1's exit gate and P0-T03's `_test_database` fixture, which shells out to the script and asserts its return code. `scripts/migrate.sh` is not at fault; the missing `INSERT`s are a v1 artefact.
  - **The fix goes in `scripts/migrate.sh`, never in `docs/Schema/*.sql`.** Those files carry hundreds of line-number citations from the phase specs, and `schema.sql` is regenerated from them, so adding the missing `INSERT`s would shift every citation after migration 008. The script instead records the whole base set from `schema.sql`'s own `NGUỒN: migrations/<file>` banners. The exact block is in P0-T03's design note 7 and was run end to end before dispatch: clean DB → exit 0, 12 recorded, 14 tables; re-run → exit 0, `0 applied, 12 already present`. The pattern is `[0-9]{3}_[a-z0-9_]+` — a letters-only stem misses `005_index_lop_bao_ve_2`. P1's `013`–`017` are unaffected; they record themselves like `001`–`007`.
  - `scripts/migrate.sh` and `.env.example` join P0-T03's "Files — modify"; its acceptance gains item 9 (clean-DB migrate + idempotent re-run) and item 3 now requires DB tests to **pass**, with a second invocation proving the skip path still works where no database exists.
  - **Dispatch, at the standing cap of 3 concurrent coders** (`01-plan.md` cross-phase rules, `HUONG-DAN` §1 step B, `director.md` decision rights — ordering is the Director's, the cap is the Owner's and is not raised): **P0-T03, P0-T05, P0-T06** now; **P0-T04** takes the first slot that frees. The Owner's reasoning is recorded as the rationale: T03's acceptance is real for the first time today and it produces the canonical fixture every later phase builds on, while T04 accepts on recorded fixtures whenever it runs.
Supersedes: —

## DEC-009 · 2026-09-05 · DEC-007 item 7 propagated to the role prompts that agents actually read
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Context: DEC-007 item 7 made `<TASK_ID>.prompt.md` the authoritative card and `<PHASE>-tasks.md` the Planner's index — but the ruling was recorded only in `DECISIONS.md` and the P0 card files. Every role prompt that tells an agent where to find the card still pointed at the index. The Owner caught it on `reviewer.md:7`; measuring it turned up three more sites, and the near-miss was live: P0-T03's prompt carries **9** acceptance items, the index restates **7**, and the two missing ones are exactly the pair added by DEC-008 — the real-database run and item 9, which is the only thing that exercises the `scripts/migrate.sh` fix. A Reviewer following `reviewer.md` today would have approved P0-T03 without ever running the fix that task exists to land.
Decision: A rule about which file is authoritative is worthless until the prompts that send agents to a file say so. Correct all four sites and treat "propagate to the role prompts" as part of any future ruling that changes where an agent looks.
Consequences:
  - `prompts/reviewer.md` §Read now names `<TASK_ID>.prompt.md` as the card, requires every acceptance command **in the prompt** to be run in order with real output pasted, and demotes the index to a cross-check whose shorter list must never define what was exercised. A divergence is a note to the Director, not something the Reviewer reconciles.
  - `prompts/coder-template.md` — the generator — no longer calls the index "your card". Since it is the template, this stops the error at source for P1–P8.
  - All six already-generated `P0-T0*.prompt.md` files carried the same line, including the three dispatched this afternoon (T03, T05, T06). Fixed in place; the coders now hold prompts that name themselves as the card.
  - `HUONG-DAN-VAN-HANH.md` §checklist told the Planner to prove no two tasks touch the same file by grepping `P<n>-tasks.md`. File scope lives in the prompt, so that check could pass while two prompts collide. It now greps `P<n>-T*.prompt.md`. (The T03/T05/T06 disjointness check run before dispatch was already done against the prompts, so the dispatch stands.)
  - Nothing about the acceptance sets themselves changed; this is a pointer fix. The underlying divergence between each prompt and its index entry remains, and stays a Director defect to reconcile per DEC-007 item 7.
Supersedes: — (extends DEC-007 item 7)

## DEC-010 · 2026-09-05 · One dedicated git worktree per task, from the moment it is dispatched
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Context: The single shared checkout has now caused four incidents in one day, each costing real work. P0-T01's Coder measured `git status --porcelain` against a tree carrying the Director's uncommitted edits and honestly recorded acceptance 3 as FAIL when it passes cleanly. DEC-002…DEC-006 sat uncommitted in that same tree, invisible to any fresh clone, while P0-T02 was built and approved against a tree that predated them. HEAD then moved under the Director mid-edit, landing the DEC-009 planning commit on `task/P0-T05` and sweeping the T05 reviewer's verdict in with it — which would have failed T05 on the Reviewer's own checklist item 8 for a scope violation the Director introduced. The Reviewer independently reached the same recommendation while approving P0-T03, and the T03 and T04 sessions, which did use their own worktrees, hit none of it.
Decision: Every dispatched task gets its own `git worktree` at dispatch, and the Director's plan edits stay in the primary checkout on `main`. No agent shares a checkout with another.
Consequences:
  - `HUONG-DAN-VAN-HANH.md` §1 step B gains the worktree creation as part of dispatch, and step C removes it after merge. The Coder prompt and the Reviewer prompt both say to work only inside the worktree they were given.
  - A Reviewer already verifies in an isolated checkout; this makes the Coder's tree isolated too, so `git status --porcelain` is meaningful for the first time and acceptance items that depend on a clean tree stop producing false failures.
  - The Director never commits from a task worktree. Before any `git add`, confirm `git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` is `main` in the primary checkout — HEAD moving under a long edit is what caused the DEC-009 misplacement, and a one-line check catches it.
  - This is process only: no card, contract or acceptance command changes.
Supersedes: —

## DEC-011 · 2026-09-05 · `make test-db` hard-requires docker; fixed as P0-T07 before P1 dispatch
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Context: The Owner measured `make test-db` on `main`: exit 1, "the docker daemon is unreachable", citing an INBOX blocker DEC-008 already closed. The tests underneath are fine — `TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql:///soc_test python3 -m pytest … -m db -q` gives 3 passed, and `scripts/migrate.sh` on a clean database gives 12 recorded, 14 tables, exit 0, idempotent. The target alone is at fault: it gates on `docker compose ps` before anything else and offers no path for an already-reachable `TEST_DATABASE_URL`. It survived P0-T02's review and P0-T03's because T03 acceptance #3 invokes pytest directly and never the target — a gap DEC-006's execute-every-acceptance-line rule does not close, because the defect is in a command no acceptance line runs. I had also called P1's exit gate "measurable"; with this target broken it is not.
Decision: Fix the target so a reachable `TEST_DATABASE_URL` is used directly and compose is the fallback, not the precondition. It ships as **P0-T07**, a P0 follow-up rather than a P1 task, and must land before P1 is dispatched.
Consequences:
  - **Why P0 and not P1.** `Makefile` is a P0-T02 deliverable and this is P0 scaffold debt; P1 is smoke test and schema, not repairing P0's scaffold. Two coder slots are free now (T03/T04/T05 merged, only T06 running), so it costs a slot today instead of delaying P1 tomorrow. It is **not** a P0 exit-gate item and does not hold T06 or P0 closure — but `coder-template.md` rule 3 and `reviewer.md` checklist item 1 both order every agent to run `make test-db`, and P1's gate reads "DB tests green", so P1 cannot dispatch over it.
  - **The Planner writes the card, not the Director** (`prompts/director.md`: the Director does not create task cards). This entry is the re-plan order and carries the verified fix so the Planner does not re-derive it.
  - **Verified fix** — prototyped and run before this was recorded, all three paths measured on this host:
    ```
    test-db:
    	@dsn="$${TEST_DATABASE_URL:-}"; \
    	if [ -z "$$dsn" ] && [ -f .env ]; then \
    	  dsn=$$(sed -n 's/^[[:space:]]*TEST_DATABASE_URL[[:space:]]*=[[:space:]]*//p' .env \
    	         | tail -1 | sed 's/[[:space:]][[:space:]]*#.*$$//'); \
    	fi; \
    	if [ -n "$$dsn" ] && psql "$$dsn" -tAc 'SELECT 1' >/dev/null 2>&1; then \
    	  echo "test-db: TEST_DATABASE_URL is reachable — running directly, no docker needed"; \
    	  TEST_DATABASE_URL="$$dsn" $(PYTEST) -m "db and not live" backend/tests; \
    	elif $(COMPOSE) ps >/dev/null 2>&1; then \
    	  <the existing compose-up + healthcheck-wait + pytest block, unchanged>; \
    	else \
    	  echo "test-db: no reachable TEST_DATABASE_URL and no docker daemon." >&2; \
    	  echo "  Set TEST_DATABASE_URL to a reachable database whose name ends in _test" >&2; \
    	  echo "  (on this host: postgresql:///soc_test — see DEC-008), or start docker." >&2; \
    	  exit 1; \
    	fi
    ```
    Path A, `TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql:///soc_test`: `3 passed, 77 deselected`, exit 0, no docker. Path B, unreachable DSN and no docker: the three-line message above, exit 1. Path C, variable unset: falls back to `.env`, and since the Owner's `.env` has no `TEST_DATABASE_URL` it lands on Path B's message rather than a stack trace. `make test`, `make lint` and `make -n migrate run-app run-worker backup` are unchanged. The comment strip requires whitespace before `#` so a `#` inside a DSN survives; the last assignment wins, matching the ten-line parser P0-T02 already uses.
  - **CORRECTION, 05/09 (DEC-021):** every "exit 1" in this entry is wrong. GNU Make remaps any failing recipe to **exit 2** regardless of the code the recipe used — measured across 1, 2, 3, 7 and 99. The recipe's own `exit 1` is real and appears in Make's `Error 1` message, but `$?` from `make` is always 2. The acceptance criterion is "non-zero, with the recipe's message", never "exit 1". The Reviewer caught this on P0-T07; the error was mine, in this entry's reference snippet, not in the delivery.
  - **The stale message goes too.** The current text cites `docs/plan/INBOX.md (2026-09-05 · P0 / P1 · BLOCKER)`, closed by DEC-008. Pointing an agent at a closed blocker is its own defect.
  - **Acceptance must exercise the target itself**, not just the tests beneath it: `make test-db` with a reachable DSN → exit 0; with an unreachable DSN and no docker → exit 1 and a message naming `TEST_DATABASE_URL`; and `make test`/`make lint` unchanged. The lesson generalises — where a card ships a wrapper, at least one acceptance line runs the wrapper.
  - **Owner action:** the `.env` on this host predates DEC-003 and carries none of `DATABASE_URL`, `DATABASE_URL_OWNER`, `TEST_DATABASE_URL`. Add `TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql:///soc_test` so the target's `.env` fallback works without an exported variable.
Supersedes: —

## DEC-012 · 2026-09-05 · Real inventory landed; `user1` recorded as non-privileged, and what that gates
Scope: evaluation
Decided by: Owner
Context: The Owner wrote `conf/inventory.yaml`, `conf/identities.yaml` and `conf/iocs.csv` from real host data; `validate()` returns OK. Two things the `.example` files had implied are wrong and are now corrected: `agent_control -l` shows **two** agents, so `IA1803` (agent 000, the manager itself) was missing entirely; and `admin` does not exist on this host — the only human account is `user1`, with `root` as the real privileged identity. The Owner also made an explicit judgement call: `user1` is in group `sudo` but is recorded `is_privileged: false`, with `root` carrying the privileged case, and asked for it to be recorded either way. Under `README.md` §Conventions this touches evaluation validity, so it is the Owner's to decide and the Director's to record — which is how it happened.
Decision: Keep `user1` as `is_privileged: false`. The inventory stands as written.
Consequences:
  - **Adding `IA1803` swapped which G8′ clause fires; it did not unblock anything.** Before, agent 000 was blocked by "asset not in inventory"; now it is blocked by `asset.criticality = high`. Auto-close behaviour for agent 000 is unchanged and deliberately so — the file says as much. The real gain is different and worth naming: the model now receives `criticality`, `owner` and `role` facts for that host, so gate step 2 has something to compare `structured_basis` against instead of `unknown`.
  - **The whole auto-close path therefore runs through one agent and one flag.** Agent 000 is permanently blocked, so every auto-close, every D8 digest row and every digest-sourced label comes from agent 001 (`user1-IA1803`, `medium`) — and `user1` is named in almost every alert it produces. Had `is_privileged` been `true`, the privileged-identity clause would have blocked that path too, leaving auto-close dead estate-wide, the digest empty for the pilot, and B1 without the class it exists to separate. The Owner's reasoning holds; it is load-bearing rather than incidental, which is exactly why it is recorded here.
  - **What still blocks auto-close with `user1` non-privileged**, and this is what makes the call defensible: `severity = critical`, any `malicious`/`suspicious` IoC hit, `asset.criticality = high`, and any host absent from the inventory. The residual set is non-critical alerts, on the workstation, with no bad IoC.
  - **Auto-close is not terminal.** Per D8 (`docs/chot-v3-14-ngay.md:74`), ① runs on **100 %** of auto-closed alerts and the 08:00 digest puts every one in front of a human for a one-click verdict into `autoclose_reviews`, with "wrong" reopening the alert. So this flag does not create unreviewed closure; it creates closure reviewed the next morning. That distinction belongs in the write-up.
  - **Disclosure is mandatory, and is the only thing that makes this safe.** `docs/limitations.md` (P8) must carry it as a named limitation, and the evaluation chapter must state that auto-close volume and the B1 comparison depend on reading a `sudo`-capable account as non-privileged. A disclosed engineering trade-off is defensible; an undisclosed one would put the auto-close numbers in question the moment an examiner reads `conf/identities.yaml`.
  - **P6 sizing changes.** Digest-sourced labelling can only be counted on from agent 001. The Planner must not size P6's 400 clusters assuming both agents contribute, and should say in the P6 card where the shortfall is made up from (lab scenarios, G2).
  - **Reversal is one line plus `POST /api/admin/reload-inventory`**, no migration. Reverse if P6 finds digest labels dominated by alerts where `user1`'s sudo rights were actually material to the verdict — that is the signal the strict reading was right.
  - `admin` never existed; nothing in the repo depended on it. Note for readers: the `admin` in §6.4's role list (`tier1`, `tier2`, `admin`) is an application role, unrelated to any OS account.
Supersedes: —

## DEC-013 · 2026-09-05 · P1 migrates a new `soc_dev`; migration 017 needs `CREATEROLE`, which does not exist
Scope: contract
Decided by: Director (database choice) · Owner (the `CREATEROLE` grant, and any §6.1 cut)
Context: The Owner declined to add `DATABASE_URL` / `DATABASE_URL_OWNER` to `.env` and gave the measurement: `soc` is owned by `postgres` and `has_schema_privilege('user1','public','CREATE')` is false, so `DATABASE_URL_OWNER=postgresql:///soc` dies on migration 013's first DDL. Verified independently, all of it. Verifying it turned up a **third** hole the report did not name: `user1` has `rolcreatedb = t` but `rolcreaterole = f`, and `CREATE ROLE app_rw` fails even in `soc_test`, which `user1` owns — database ownership does not confer `CREATEROLE`. Migration **017** (`CREATE ROLE app_rw` and the owner role, `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE`, `raise_immutable()` triggers) therefore cannot run on any database on this host as things stand. Measured on a throwaway `user1`-owned database: the base schema applies cleanly (exit 0, 14 tables), and `CREATE ROLE` in that same database fails.
Decision: **P1 migrates a new `soc_dev`, owned by `user1`.** `DATABASE_URL_OWNER=postgresql:///soc_dev`; `DATABASE_URL=postgresql://app_rw@/soc_dev` once 017 lands. Migrations 013–016 are unblocked by this and can be written now; **017 is blocked pending an Owner decision** between the three routes below.
Consequences:
  - **Why not `soc`:** owned by `postgres`, no `CREATE` on `public` for `user1` — measured false. It also holds the v1 data and is not something a pilot should migrate in place. It stays untouched. If the Owner later wants P1 to run against the real v1 data, that is a separate ownership question, not a default.
  - **Why not `soc_test`:** the P0-T03 conftest **drops and recreates** it every session. Pointing `DATABASE_URL_OWNER` at it would put `make migrate` and the test suite in a fight over one database. Three databases, three jobs: `soc` legacy and untouched, `soc_dev` the dev/app database P1 migrates, `soc_test` the fixture's alone.
  - **`createdb soc_dev` is one command and needs nothing new** — `CREATEDB` came with DEC-008. It is P1-T02's first step, not a manual prerequisite.
  - **Migration 017 — three routes, Owner's call:**
    **A (recommended)** `sudo -u postgres psql -c 'ALTER ROLE user1 CREATEROLE'`. One command, same shape as DEC-008's grant, preserves §6.1 whole and keeps migrations self-contained so "migrations 013–017 apply on a clean DB" stays a real, repeatable gate.
    **B** A superuser pre-creates `app_rw` and the owner role once; 017 shrinks to `GRANT`/`REVOKE`, which a database owner may do on their own objects. Preserves the contract but makes the migration set non-self-contained — P1's gate then needs a documented manual pre-step, which is exactly the kind of human step DEC-006's discipline is trying to remove.
    **C** Drop the role split for the pilot. This is a **§6.1 frozen-contract change and needs Owner sign-off**, not a Director ruling. Worth knowing before rejecting it: the `BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE` trigger is the real append-only enforcement and survives intact, so G2 and G11 still hold; what is lost is the `REVOKE ... FROM app_rw` defence-in-depth and the app/owner separation. It would become a named limitation in P8.
  - **Until 017 is settled, P1's exit gate reads "013–016 apply on a clean `soc_dev`".** 017 is carved out and tracked separately rather than silently failing the gate.
  - **Folded into P0-T07** (`.env.example` and `.gitignore` are P0 scaffold files, and `HUONG-DAN-VAN-HANH.md:183` bans direct commits to `main` outside `docs/plan/`, so neither may be a loose edit here): add `*.sw?` to `.gitignore`; make the no-docker DSN at `.env.example:20` the documented default for this host rather than a footnote comment; and add `DATABASE_URL` / `DATABASE_URL_OWNER` defaults pointing at `soc_dev`, with the compose forms kept as the docker alternative. No key is added or renamed — all three are already §6.3 keys under DEC-003.
  - **Folded into the P1 plan:** `soc_dev` as the migration target, `user1` as the owner role, and 017's dependency on the Owner's choice above.
Supersedes: —

## DEC-014 · 2026-09-05 · The real alert volume is 60× the planning assumption; four consequences
Scope: evaluation
Decided by: Owner (measurement) · Director (consequences)
Context: The Owner read the manager's own alert archive as root — 28 daily files 08/08–04/09 plus today, 84,376 raw alerts. Median 592/day, mean 3,013/day, peak 47,917 on 16/08. Per agent, anchored on the `agent` object: `user1-IA1803` 60,743, `IA1803` 22,766. **I could not verify this independently — `/var/ossec/logs/archives` and `/var/ossec/logs/alerts` are both permission-denied to `user1` — so these are the Owner's figures, not mine.** What I did verify is the arithmetic, and every figure reconciles: 60,743 + 22,766 = 83,509 anchored, leaving 867 (1.03%) with no `agent` object; 47,917 is 56.8% of the archive; 84,376 − 47,917 = 36,459; 84,376 / 28 = 3,013.
Decision: Correct the volume assumption at source, record the four consequences, and order the one measurement that unblocks the rest.
Consequences:
  - **Context pack §1 carried "≈ 50 alerts/day" as fact and is corrected.** That is the #1 source of truth, so the wrong number was propagating into every downstream judgement. **Precision the report understates:** ~12× is against the *median* (592/50 = 11.8×); against the *mean* it is **60×** (3,013/50). For a monthly figure like `LLM_MONTHLY_USD_CAP=30` the mean is the sizing statistic, so the budget exposure is 60×, not 12× — if ① ran per alert.
  - **It does not, and that is the whole question.** ① runs per cluster. The dedup ratio has never been measured, so the LLM budget is **unknown**, not merely larger — exactly as the Owner puts it. **Ordered: a P2 measurement task, dedup compression on one real day**, run before any budget claim is made. Until it reports, no document should assert the cap is comfortable.
  - **A cut is now resting on a void justification.** C8 (keyset pagination, `enrich_cache`, circuit breaker, rate limit, `sweeper sealed_at`) was cut with the recorded reason "Vô nghĩa ở 50/ngày" — meaningless at 50/day. At a 3,013 mean and a 47,917 peak that reason no longer stands on its face. **C8 stays cut for now** — un-cutting is the Owner's call, not the Director's — but its stated justification is void, and it must be re-examined once the dedup ratio is known. Keyset pagination and a rate limit are not obviously meaningless at 47,917 alerts in a day.
  - **16/08 becomes the dedup fixture.** 47,917 alerts, 57% of the archive, the canonical sample alert's own day, rule 40112 SSH brute force. It exercises `MAX_CLUSTER_SIZE=1000` and `MAX_CLUSTER_AGE_HOURS=4` for real. **Ordered into P2 as the dedup test fixture in place of a synthetic burst** — a real storm beats an invented one, and this one is already the fixture's provenance.
  - **R2 is dead; DEC-001 is qualified in place.** R2 ("no archive → thin G1", mitigated by a 300-cluster floor and more benign lab data) assumed the history was unreachable. It is reachable by the Owner as root: 84,376 alerts over 29 days, or 36,459 excluding the storm day. G1 can be backfilled **today, without `soc_ro`**. The 300-cluster floor can relax and P6's sizing should be revisited against real history rather than lab padding. `soc_ro` still gates the live puller path, so the INBOX blocker stays open — it is simply no longer the only road to a gold set.
  - **The 1% with no `agent` object must not be rejected — ruled now, before P2 codes it.** `alerts.agent_name` is `NOT NULL` (`schema.sql:163`) and is one of the four dedup cluster-key columns, so "make it nullable" is not available without a §6.1 change. It does not need one: **when the `agent` object is absent, `agent_name` falls back to `manager.name`** (the same value that populates `manager_id`), `agent_id` stays NULL as the schema already permits, and the alert proceeds normally. `rejected_alerts` is reserved for payloads that cannot be identified at all — no alert id, no rule id, unparseable JSON — never for a structurally valid manager-level alert. Silently dropping 1% of real traffic into `rejected_alerts` would be a data-loss defect, not validation. Pleasant side effect: those alerts then resolve to host `IA1803`, which is in the inventory at `criticality: high`, so G8′ blocks auto-close on them — the safe outcome, reached without a special case.
  - **DEC-012 is strengthened, not weakened.** `IA1803`'s permanent auto-close block covers 27% of historical alerts, not a majority; 73% come from `user1-IA1803`, the agent where auto-close *can* fire. The digest-volume argument behind reading `user1` as non-privileged holds with real numbers behind it. Today's file runs the other way and is not representative.
  - **Amended 2026-09-06 by DEC-032 — the ratio is measured; this entry's "unknown" is retired.** The Owner ran the dedup predicates over the full 29-day archive with `IDLE_GAP`, `MAX_AGE` and `MAX_SIZE` applied: **30.2 : 1 — 2,778 clusters, ≈ 96 clusters/day.** *(Input-specific, noted 07/09: 2,778 is the 29-day / 84,376-line export. The 30-day / 92,030-line export of 07/09 gives **3,070** clusters under the same predicates — both figures are correct for their input and neither supersedes the other. Quote the denominator with the number.)* Against the 12.5 break-even that follows from the smoke test's measured cost ($0.00415/call, `LLM_MONTHLY_USD_CAP=30`, 3,013 alerts/day mean) it clears by **2.4×**. Recorded here because the number lived only in chat for a day — the DEC-026 defect, this time with the Owner's own data. Arithmetic note, **corrected 06/09 after the Owner's answer:** the clustering run processed **83,957 lines** over a slightly different file set — it included today's `alerts.json` — while 84,376 came from another glob; 83,957 ÷ 2,778 = 30.2 exactly. A line-counting artifact, nothing more. The first version of this note explained the gap by "the ≈ 1 % lacking an `agent` object" — a figure from this entry's own bullet above that the Owner had **withdrawn**: parsing every archive line as JSON measured **zero** alerts without an `agent` object; the ≈ 1 % / ≈ 870 lines was itself a line-counting artifact. The fallback rule (`agent_name ← manager.name`) stays as defensive behaviour for a shape the manager can emit, not as a data-loss fix, and no document may cite the frequency. The Director reasoned from a retracted fact in a note about precision — recorded because that is exactly what the last two days have been about. The P2 task ordered above becomes a *verification* of the coded dedup against 2,778, not the first measurement.
Supersedes: — (qualifies DEC-001)

## DEC-015 · 2026-09-05 · `soc_ro` closed; worktree rule propagated; P0 exit gate item 2 met; `soc_dev` handed to P1
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Context: Five of the Owner's ten intake items are settled by measurement rather than judgement. Items 4, 5 and 6 of that intake were already recorded in **DEC-014** yesterday evening — the S1 volume correction with the dedup-ratio order and 16/08 as the P2 fixture, the ruling that `agent_name` falls back to `manager.name` so the 1% with no `agent` object never reaches `rejected_alerts`, and the DEC-001 qualifier. They are not re-recorded here.
Decision: Close the `soc_ro` blocker, propagate DEC-010 into the cards that predate it, re-issue the P0-T07 order, record the probe run, and hand P1 a working `soc_dev`.
Consequences:
  - **`soc_ro` (item 1) — INBOX `2026-09-05 · P0 / P2 · BLOCKER` closed.** Verified independently, not taken on report: `authinfo` returns user `soc_ro` with role `soc_reader`; `_count` on `wazuh-alerts-*` = **7,452** (the Owner counted 7,451 — one document arrived between the two reads, which is what a live index should do); `POST _doc` = **403**. `INDEXER_URL` stays `https://127.0.0.1:9400` as DEC-001 recorded: the certificate SAN carries only `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` and `::1`, and §6.3 forbids an insecure mode, so `79.79.79.11:9400` is unusable. That is now on the record rather than folklore.
  - **The worktree rule (item 7) was systemic, not one card.** `P0-T06` line 65 was the reported case, but **all six P0 cards and `coder-template.md` itself** carried `git checkout main && git checkout -b task/…` with zero mentions of a worktree — following any of them verbatim breaks DEC-010. Same shape as DEC-009: the generator was the source, so it is fixed first and the six instances with it. Working rule 1 now reads `git worktree add ../AI_Support_SOC_1_2-<TASK_ID> -b task/<TASK_ID> main`, with an explicit "do not `git checkout` in the primary checkout — another agent is working there". No dispatch-time override is needed; the card is correct now. `prompts/director.md:42` keeps its `git checkout main` because the Director genuinely does work on `main` in the primary checkout.
  - **Recorded deviation:** `director.md:42` prescribes `git merge --no-ff`; I have been rebasing each approved branch onto `main` and merging `--ff-only`. That was deliberate — every branch needed rebasing anyway so its acceptance could be re-run against the current cards — but it is a deviation from my own role prompt and belongs on the record rather than passing silently.
  - **P0 exit gate item 2 (item 9) is MET.** `python3 eval/indexer_probe.py --save-samples 5` ran for real, exit 0: 02/09 6,395 · 03/09 371 · 04/09 354 · 05/09 332 · **total 7,452**, earliest index date **2026-09-02**, `search_after` page returned 500 hits with `first_sort [1788340214689]`, and five distinct-rule fixtures were written (rules 591, 5503, 5760, 92601, 521). The gate item was "indexer query returns documents" and it does. The retention answer it produces is the bad news that drives DEC-016.
  - **`soc_dev` exists (item 10).** Created and migrated rather than left to P1-T02: `createdb soc_dev` then `scripts/migrate.sh` → exit 0, **14 tables, 12 migrations recorded, owner `user1`**. P1 now starts against a working target instead of an environment step, and DEC-013's choice of `soc_dev` is confirmed in practice. `soc_test` and `soc` are untouched.
  - **P0-T07 (item 8) re-issued.** The card still does not exist and DEC-011's re-plan order went unanswered. It is re-issued with its full scope in one place — the `make test-db` fix from DEC-011, plus `*.sw?` in `.gitignore` and the `.env.example` DSN defaults from DEC-013 — so the Planner formats rather than derives. P1 cannot dispatch over it and it is the only P0 item now standing between here and P1 planning.
Supersedes: —

## DEC-016 · 2026-09-05 · Migration 017 takes route D, plus a TRUNCATE trigger it cannot ship without
Scope: contract
Decided by: Director
Context: The Owner supplied route D with measurements, and corrected my route A: PostgreSQL 16 restricts `CREATEROLE` to roles the grantee created, so granting `user1` `CREATEROLE` would still not let it manage `app_rw`, which `postgres` created. Verified verbatim — `ALTER ROLE app_rw` as `user1` gives "Only roles with the CREATEROLE attribute and the ADMIN option on role app_rw may alter this role". Route A is withdrawn. Everything else the Owner measured also reproduces: `app_rw` exists NOLOGIN with no attributes, `user1` is a member, `SET ROLE app_rw` succeeds, and as database owner `user1` can run the whole of 017's body — conditional `CREATE ROLE`, `GRANT`, `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE`, `CREATE FUNCTION` plpgsql and `CREATE TRIGGER` — with no superuser.
Decision: **Adopt route D**, with one addition that is not optional.
Consequences:
  - **Both enforcement layers verified biting, on a real database.** As `app_rw`: `UPDATE`, `DELETE` and `TRUNCATE` on `audit_events` all return `permission denied for table audit_events`, while `INSERT` succeeds. As the owner, who the `REVOKE` does not constrain: the row trigger fires — `append-only table: audit_events is immutable` — and the row survives. §6.1 holds in substance; the `REVOKE` genuinely bites.
  - **A hole the proposal did not name, found by testing rather than reasoning: `TRUNCATE`.** A `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE ... FOR EACH ROW` trigger **does not fire on TRUNCATE**. Measured: a session that does `RESET ROLE` back to `user1` truncated `audit_events` and wiped both rows with no error. The disclosed weakening is therefore larger than "a session can RESET ROLE back to user1" — as owner it could erase the audit trail outright.
  - **Required addition, verified to close it:** 017 must also create a **statement-level** trigger, `BEFORE TRUNCATE ... FOR EACH STATEMENT EXECUTE FUNCTION raise_immutable()`, on each of `audit_events`, `llm_runs` and `intake`. Measured with it in place: `TRUNCATE` as the owner raises `append-only table: audit_events is immutable` and the row survives. Without this, route D's append-only guarantee is defeated by one statement. 017 does not ship without it.
  - **Residual, irreducible weakening — one line in `docs/limitations.md`:** the application session can `RESET ROLE` to `user1`, which owns the database and can therefore `DROP TRIGGER` (measured: it succeeds). No trigger can prevent that while the app's session can reach the owner; only a separate `app_rw` login could, and creating one needs a privilege this host will not give. This is strictly less loss than route C, which would have given up the `REVOKE` as well.
  - **P1's gate reads "013–017 apply on a clean `soc_dev`", nothing carved out.** DEC-013's carve-out is withdrawn. No superuser command is needed and no Owner action remains on this item.
  - `CREATE ROLE` in 017 stays conditional (a `DO` block testing `pg_roles`) because `app_rw` already exists here and would otherwise abort the migration; on a clean host it still creates the role.
Supersedes: DEC-013 (route A withdrawn; 017's carve-out removed)

## DEC-017 · 2026-09-05 · F2 is false; G1's source recommended as both, escalated to the Owner
Scope: evaluation
Decided by: Director (recommendation) · **Owner (decision — escalated)**
Context: `docs/chot-v3-14-ngay.md:172` (F2) claims the daily indices reach back to 16/08 and used that to replace D3's `alerts.json` reading with puller-based replay from `PULL_START=2026-08-01`. Measured with `soc_ro`: the indexer holds **four** days, 02/09–05/09, 7,452 documents; earliest index created 2026-09-02T09:10:15Z (16:10 local), matching the Logstash keystore built 02/09; `_plugins/_ism/policies` returns none, so nothing is pruning — Logstash simply started on 02/09. `PULL_START=2026-08-01` will find nothing before 02/09. The five weeks exist only in the manager archive (84,376 alerts, 08/08–04/09 plus today, root-readable, unpruned).
Decision: **Recommend (c) both** — the indexer stays the live intake path; the manager archive supplies the history backfill for G1. **Escalated to the Owner**, because reversing F2's replacement of D3 is a D-item decision and the choice determines whether the 300-cluster floor is reachable, both of which `README.md` §Conventions reserves to the Owner.
Consequences:
  - **Why not live-only.** By the time P6 runs there would be roughly 12 days of live data. Measured over the three post-backfill days (1,066 alerts): the top rules are `2904`/`2902` (package installation, 328) and `550`/`553`/`554` (file-integrity, 242), with 38 alerts mentioning `sudo` and 11 mentioning `tmp`. That is the signature of **development activity on the host** — our own build. A gold set drawn only from live data would over-represent a class the thesis created, which is an evaluation-representativeness problem an examiner would find.
  - **Headroom, expressed against the unknown.** Clusters ≈ alerts ÷ compression C, and C is unmeasured (DEC-014 ordered it). Live-only ≈ 4,000–4,500 alerts by P6, so 300 clusters needs C ≤ ~14. The archive excluding the storm day gives 36,459, needing C ≤ ~120 — roughly **eight times the headroom**. Neither number can be asserted until the dedup measurement lands; both are stated as functions on purpose.
  - **What the archive costs.** The archive line and the indexer `_source` document are not the same shape — the canonical fixture in §8 is an indexer document with `_source`, `fields.timestamp[0]` and `sort`, and the parser reads `fields.timestamp[0]` for `alert_time`. A backfill path must either reconstruct that envelope or the parser must accept both shapes, and G9's byte-identical `raw_payload` has to mean the archive line for backfilled rows. This is real work and belongs in P2's estimate, not hidden in P6.
  - **F2's amendment, for the Owner to approve with the choice:** keep "the indexer is the live intake path, `source='wazuh'`"; strike the claim that indices reach back to 16/08; state the measured retention (02/09 onward, no ISM policy, Logstash start date); and record that history before 02/09 is reachable **only** from the manager archive, read by the Owner as root, ingested as `source='replay'`. Nothing here reinstates D3 as an application code path — the app still cannot read `/var/ossec/logs/`.
  - **`soc_ro` did not solve the retention question; it answered it, and the answer was bad.** That is worth stating plainly because the P0 exit gate treats "indexer query returns documents" as met while the thing the gate was proxying for — enough history for G1 — is not.
Supersedes: — (amends F2; qualifies DEC-014's R2 note)

## DEC-018 · 2026-09-05 · Context pack §3 drops the container runtime; P0-T07's missing card, and what reviews it
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Context: P0-T07 landed on its own branch with no card ever written — DEC-011 ordered a re-plan twice, the Planner was never run, and the Owner asked for the fix directly. Its scope was DEC-011, plus DEC-013's additions, plus the Owner's 05/09 decision that no `Makefile` target may require docker. That last one goes further than my DEC-011 specification, which kept compose as a fallback; the delivered target drops compose entirely. Verified on all four paths in the T07 worktree: unset and no `.env` → exit 1 naming the fix; `TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql:///soc_test` → **"test-db: using postgresql:///soc_test", 3 passed, exit 0 — the first time that target has ever completed**; set but unreachable → exit 1 with `createdb nope_test` as remediation; `make test`, `make lint` and the dry-run targets unchanged. `grep -cE 'COMPOSE|docker compose' Makefile` returns 0.
Decision: Amend §3 to describe the runtime that exists; treat DEC-011 + DEC-013 as P0-T07's acceptance authority in the absence of a card; dispatch it for review with the `.env` caveat stated.
Consequences:
  - **§3 is not a frozen contract and this amendment is the Director's.** §6 is the frozen set (`00-context-pack.md:73`) and `README.md:49` enumerates what needs the Owner — schema, output schemas, config keys, API list, import rules. §3 "Runtime shape" is none of those, and the underlying no-docker decision was already the Owner's. §3 now reads local processes plus native PostgreSQL 16, with `docker-compose.yml` retained as optional tooling for a host that has a daemon. The old line was not merely stale, it was false on the only host this is built on.
  - **The card gap is real and is mine, not the Planner's.** DEC-009 makes `<TASK_ID>.prompt.md` the authoritative card, and P0-T07 has none — so a Reviewer following `reviewer.md` has nothing to verify against. For this one task, **DEC-011 and DEC-013 stand in as the acceptance authority**; they carry the full scope and a pre-verified fix, which is why the work was possible without a card at all. This is a one-off, recorded rather than quietly tolerated: the next phase does not get to skip carding, and the Planner still owes P1 its cards.
  - **Dispatch note the Reviewer needs.** `reviewer.md` checklist item 1 says to run `make test-db`, but a worktree has no `.env` — it is git-ignored and therefore absent from every fresh checkout. The Reviewer must run `TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql:///soc_test make test-db`. Without it the target correctly exits 1, and a Reviewer could read a working target as broken. Confirmed by running it in the T07 worktree, which has no `.env`.
  - **P0's exit gate is MET**, checked item by item on `main` after P0-T06 merged: `make test` runs (77 passed); the indexer returns documents (7,452, DEC-015); `.env.example` is complete (the §6.3 comparison reports no missing keys); `conf/*.yaml` are present; the `Final-Project` decision is recorded (DEC-002). P0-T07 is not an exit-gate item and does not hold closure — but it does hold P1 dispatch, so P0 closes with one task in review.
Supersedes: —

## DEC-019 · 2026-09-05 · G1 draws from both sources; F2 amended
Scope: evaluation
Decided by: Owner
Context: DEC-017 established that F2 was false — the indexer holds four days, not five weeks — and recommended drawing G1 from both the live indexer and the root-readable manager archive, escalating the choice because reversing F2's replacement of D3 is a D-item call and it determines whether the 300-cluster floor is reachable.
Decision: **Approved — "both".** The indexer remains the live intake path; the manager archive supplies history before 02/09.
Consequences:
  - **F2 is amended in place** at `docs/chot-v3-14-ngay.md:172`, as a single table row so the file's line count is unchanged and the two line-number citations into it (`:74` for D8, `:172` for F2) stay valid. It now keeps the live puller with `source='wazuh'`, strikes the "indices exist at least from 16/08" claim with the 05/09 measurement that disproves it, and records that pre-02/09 history comes from the manager archive as `source='replay'`.
  - **D3 stays cut as an application code path.** The app runs as `user1` and cannot read `/var/ossec/logs/`. Backfill is an Owner-run root-side operation, not app code — that distinction is what lets "both" be adopted without reinstating a cut D-item.
  - **No frozen contract changes.** `alerts.source ∈ wazuh|lab|replay` and `intake.sort_key bigint` already exist in §6.1; "both" consumes contract surface that was already provisioned rather than widening it.
  - **F4 already covers the shape difference, and P2 must not "fix" it away.** An archive line is the manager's alert object — what Logstash later becomes `_source` — with no `_index`/`_id`/`fields`/`sort` envelope, so `alert_time` cannot come from `fields.timestamp[0]` for replay rows. F4 already specifies the fallback: `alert_time ← fields.timestamp[0]` (UTC), **`dự phòng _source.timestamp` (+0700)**. That fallback *is* the replay case. Note the trap: DEC-002 records that `Final-Project`'s parser wrongly read `_source.timestamp` for **live** documents, and P2 is told to correct it. Correcting it must not delete the fallback — it is a bug on the live path and the specified behaviour on the replay path. One parser, two documented inputs.
  - **`sort_key` needs the one rule the archive cannot supply.** The indexer's `sort` value is epoch-millis of `fields.timestamp[0]` (P0-T04 verified `1786903016130` against the canonical fixture). An archive line has no `sort`, so for replay rows `sort_key` is computed as epoch-millis of the alert's own timestamp. P2 owns this; it is the only genuinely new derivation "both" introduces.
  - **G9 for replay rows means the archive line, byte-identical.** `raw_payload` stores what was read, not a reconstructed indexer envelope. Do not synthesise an `_source` wrapper to make the shapes match — that would violate G9 to save a branch in the parser.
  - **P6 sizing (DEC-014, DEC-017).** The floor is now backed by 36,459 alerts excluding the storm day rather than ~4,000 live ones, so 300 clusters needs compression ≤ ~120 instead of ≤ ~14 — roughly eight times the headroom. Still a function of the unmeasured dedup ratio; the P2 measurement ordered in DEC-014 remains the gate on asserting any of it. R2 is formally dead and its 300-cluster floor plus benign-lab padding can be revisited.
  - **One thing to check before P6 labels anything:** `alerts.source` must not be visible on the labelling page. Blind labelling is an evaluation-validity rule that is never relaxed (§0 precedence, `01-plan.md` cross-phase rules), and a labeller who can see `replay` versus `wazuh` can infer the storm day. Flagged for the P4/P6 cards rather than assumed.
Supersedes: — (implements DEC-017; amends F2)

## DEC-020 · 2026-09-05 · DEC-016 was wrong: migration 017 goes back to the Owner
Scope: contract
Decided by: Director (withdrawal) · **Owner (both questions below — escalated)**
Context: An adversarial verification run I commissioned before ruling on route D returned after DEC-016 was already committed, and rejected it. I re-tested every claim myself rather than take it on trust. Four hold, and together they mean DEC-016 accepted route D on a verification that was too narrow — I tested `audit_events` and never `intake`, and I tested this host and called the result portable.
Decision: **Withdraw DEC-016's acceptance.** Migration 017 is blocked again, on two Owner questions, not one.
Consequences:
  - **1. Fatal, and route-independent — §6.1 contradicts G12.** §6.1 specifies `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE ON audit_events, llm_runs, intake FROM app_rw` plus a BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE trigger. But G12 requires "every `intake` row gets `processed_at` or `error` within 60 s", and `intake.outcome` is only knowable once the pipeline job has run — the row is INSERTed on arrival and UPDATEd on completion. As written, the contract makes its own pipeline's commit step impossible **under every route**, D included. This has nothing to do with roles and would have failed P1-T05 on the first integration test. I missed it because I verified `audit_events` and stopped.
    **Verified fix, measured on a stand-in table with `intake`'s shape:** `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE ON intake FROM app_rw, PUBLIC` then `GRANT UPDATE (processed_at, outcome, error) ON intake TO app_rw`, plus a trigger pinning the receipt columns. Result as `app_rw`: `INSERT` → 1 row; `UPDATE … processed_at, outcome` → 1 row (**G12 satisfied**); `UPDATE … raw_payload` → `permission denied` (**G9 protected at the privilege layer, before the trigger**); `DELETE` → `permission denied`. This is a §6.1 wording change and therefore the Owner's.
  - **2. §6.1 says, verbatim, "The application connects as `app_rw`".** Route D has it connect as the owner and `SET ROLE`. That is a change to frozen text — the same standard under which I said route C needed sign-off. I applied it to C and not to D, which was inconsistent, and the verification was right to call it. Escalated.
  - **3. The escape surface is wider than I disclosed.** I recorded "a session can `RESET ROLE`". Measured, all four return the pooled connection to the owner: `RESET ROLE`, `SET ROLE NONE`, `SET ROLE user1`, `SET SESSION AUTHORIZATION user1` — and `DISCARD ALL`, which is pgbouncer's default reset query, does the same outside a transaction block. The limitations line must say "the application's own connection can return to the owner by several ordinary means", not name one.
  - **4. P1's gate wording was false off this host.** DEC-016 said "013–017 apply on a clean `soc_dev`, nothing carved out". Roles are **cluster-global**, and `user1` cannot `CREATE ROLE` at all — measured, the conditional `DO` block fails with `permission denied to create role` when the role is genuinely absent. It passes here only because a superuser created `app_rw` before any of this. That is route B with its manual pre-step hidden inside an `IF NOT EXISTS`, and presenting it as self-contained would have been the exact "never report a gate as met on a path you did not run" failure `director.md` warns about. The gate must read "013–017 apply on a clean `soc_dev` **on a cluster where `app_rw` already exists**", or the Owner grants `CREATEROLE` and it needs no qualifier.
  - **What stands from DEC-016:** the TRUNCATE finding and its statement-level trigger — that was right, and the verification independently reproduced it. Whatever route wins, 017 carries `BEFORE TRUNCATE … FOR EACH STATEMENT` on all three tables.
  - **Routes converge more than either the Owner or I said.** 017 needs `CREATE ROLE` only on a cluster lacking `app_rw`; it never needs `ALTER ROLE`, which is the thing PG16 blocks. So on **this** host every route's DDL already works and the live question is only §6.1's text; on a **fresh** host every route needs one superuser command or `CREATEROLE`. Route A's `ALTER ROLE user1 CREATEROLE` remains the one command that removes the qualifier from the gate.
  - Process note: this is the second time a verification run has changed a ruling after I committed it. The lesson is not to commission fewer, it is not to commit a contract ruling while its verification is still in flight.
Supersedes: DEC-016 (acceptance withdrawn; the TRUNCATE requirement retained)

## DEC-021 · 2026-09-05 · No target may require docker (logged); Make's exit code; four record-keeping corrections
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Owner (the no-docker ruling) · Director (the rest)
Context: P0-T07's Reviewer approved the delivery and filed four record-keeping asks, one of which is a measurement error of mine. All four checked; two were already done, two were not.
Consequences:
  - **The no-docker ruling is now logged, not just implied.** The Owner ruled on 05/09 that **no `Makefile` target may require docker**. It reached P0-T07 as dispatch scope and shaped the delivery, but lived nowhere in `DECISIONS.md` — it was visible only as context inside DEC-018. Recorded here as a decision in its own right: the build must run end to end on a host with no container runtime, `docker-compose.yml` is retained as optional tooling for a host that has one, and `grep -cE 'COMPOSE|docker compose' Makefile` returning `0` is the standing check. Verified on `main` after the merge: it returns 0, and `make test-db` completes against `TEST_DATABASE_URL`.
  - **`make` never exits 1, and DEC-011 said it did.** GNU Make remaps **any** failing recipe to **exit 2**, whatever code the recipe used — measured across recipes exiting 1, 2, 3, 7 and 99, all of which give `make` exit 2. The recipe's own code survives only inside Make's `Error N` text. DEC-011's reference snippet promised "exit 1" for the unreachable and unset paths, so that criterion was **never achievable by any delivery**. P0-T07's report inherited the claim from my snippet and its Reviewer measured the truth. DEC-011 is corrected in place with a visible marker rather than quietly edited. The general form: an acceptance line may require "non-zero, with the recipe's message", never a specific exit code from a `make` target.
  - **`reviewer.md` now carries the `TEST_DATABASE_URL` line — and that omission is DEC-009's lesson repeating.** DEC-018 recorded the worktree/`.env` caveat as a "dispatch note" and never put it in the prompt the Reviewer actually reads, which is exactly the failure DEC-009 was written about three decisions earlier: a rule is worthless until the prompt that sends the agent says so. `reviewer.md` gains both the export line and the Make exit-code rule, so the next Reviewer needs neither a dispatch note nor this entry.
  - **Already done, no action:** context pack §3's false docker line was amended under DEC-018 (`grep -c "docker compose\`: \`app\`"` → 0). The Reviewer filed it before that landed.
  - **P0-T07 is merged** at `0bdbede`, rebased and re-verified first: `TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql:///soc_test make test-db` → 3 passed; unset → non-zero with the remediation message; `make test` 77 passed; `make lint` clean; zero docker references; `*.sw?` ignored; `.env.example`'s `TEST_DATABASE_URL` default promoted to `postgresql:///soc_test`. **Every P0 task is now merged.**
Supersedes: — (corrects DEC-011's exit-code claim)

## DEC-022 · 2026-09-05 · The Planner's three P1 questions, resolved
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Context: The P1 Planner filed three items, each with a measurement. All three measurements reproduce.
Consequences:
  - **`sampled_for_control` — the clause is KEPT, not struck.** Verified: `alerts` has 51 columns on the migrated `soc_dev` and none is named `sampled_for_control`; it exists only as a key inside a `jsonb_build_object(...)` audit payload at `docs/phase-3-auto-close.md:166`. §6.1 orders a column dropped that never existed. The Planner's option A struck the clause from §6.1; **I am taking the first half and declining the second.** Migration 016 carries `ALTER TABLE alerts DROP COLUMN IF EXISTS sampled_for_control` — measured a clean idempotent no-op (`NOTICE … skipping`, exit 0, same on re-run) — and §6.1 gains an annotation saying so. Striking text from §6.1 would be a frozen-contract edit needing the Owner; annotating it is not, achieves the same thing for the next reader, and needs no sign-off. DEC-020 caught me being inconsistent about exactly this, so the disciplined choice is the one that does not touch frozen text at all.
  - **`enrich_cache` — confirmed in 013.** Verified: the table exists on `soc_dev` and no foreign key references it, so a plain `DROP TABLE` succeeds. §6.1 already orders the drop; only the file placement was open, and "013 is the enrichment-tables migration" is the reading DEC-004 established when it made 013 `assets_enrichment`. It is already written into P1-T02's card, so confirming costs nothing and moving it would cost a card rewrite for no gain.
  - **Smoke-test responses — option A, and the phase brief is amended to match.** The remote is a real public repository (`github.com/Kh4nh709/AI_Support_SOC_1_2`), and the raw responses quote real hostnames, usernames and internal IPs from live alerts. `docs/smoke-test-D1.md` carries the summary tables, percentiles, pass/fail and 300-character excerpts; the **full** responses go to `eval/results/smoke/`, verified git-ignored, with the report naming the path and file count. Reproducibility is preserved by the files existing — publication is not what makes evidence reproducible, and an examiner with repo access can be handed them. `prompts/P1.md` said "the raw responses appended" and is amended, because leaving the phase brief and the card contradicting each other is the DEC-009 defect all over again.
  - The Planner's discipline here is worth recording: three genuine contradictions found by measuring the migrated database rather than reading the spec, each filed with the measurement attached and a recommendation. That is the behaviour DEC-006 was trying to produce.
Supersedes: —

## DEC-023 · 2026-09-05 · Migration 017 unblocked — §6.1 amended on four points, G9 re-worded, two silent-green harness defects closed
Scope: contract
Drafted by: Fable (attributed the acceptance rule and the card corrections to the Director, who had not written them — see DEC-024's governance note)
Decided by: Owner (the four contract rulings, the exit-gate wording, the hotfix exception) · Director (the acceptance rule and the card corrections)
Context: DEC-020 left 017 on two Owner questions. Working them through found two more contract defects entangled with the same migration — G9 cannot hold on `jsonb`, which reorders keys and collapses whitespace, and no route lets the application connect as a `NOLOGIN` role — and two confirmed harness defects that would have turned `main` red on the first migration merge and let a migration card report "acceptance PASS" with zero database assertions executed. Every claim below was measured on this host today as `user1`, on scratch databases created with `createdb` and dropped afterwards; the full 017 draft was applied once end-to-end (`psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -f`) on a database built by `scripts/migrate.sh` plus 014's `intake` shape, exit 0, and every acceptance line now in P1-T06's card was run against it.
Decision: **Routes 1a + 2b + 4d**, in the terms of the four questions put to the Owner; TRUNCATE trigger confirmed. Migration 017 is dispatchable after 014 merges. P1's exit gate stays one repeatable command on this cluster.
Consequences:
  - **1 · `intake` stays in the append-only set; the completion write is a column-level grant (1a).** Layer 1: `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE ON audit_events, llm_runs, intake FROM app_rw, PUBLIC`, then `GRANT UPDATE (processed_at, outcome, error) ON intake TO app_rw`. Layer 2: on `intake` a `BEFORE UPDATE` row trigger (`intake_pin_receipt()`) that rejects any change to a column other than those three and any change to one of them once it is non-null, plus `BEFORE DELETE` and `BEFORE TRUNCATE` through `raise_immutable()`. Measured as `app_rw`: `INSERT` → `INSERT 0 1`; `UPDATE … processed_at, outcome` → `UPDATE 1` (G12 holds); `UPDATE … raw_text` → `permission denied for table intake` (G9 at the privilege layer); `DELETE` and `TRUNCATE` → `permission denied`; a second `UPDATE … outcome` → `receipt columns are pinned once set`. As the owner: `UPDATE … via` → `payload columns are immutable`; `DELETE`/`TRUNCATE` → `intake is immutable`; the row survives. **Order is load-bearing:** a table-level `REVOKE UPDATE` also removes column-level UPDATE grants (`has_column_privilege` → `f` when the column GRANT came first), so the column GRANT follows the REVOKE. Consequence for P2: a retried job may still set `processed_at`/`outcome` after an `error`, but cannot overwrite `error`; a second failure is recorded in `jobs`/`audit_events`, not by rewriting the receipt. 1b would give up the privilege layer on exactly the column G9 protects; 1c adds a table, a join and a second write for nothing.
  - **2 · The application connects as `app_rw`, which becomes a LOGIN role (2b) — one-off superuser prerequisite, per cluster.** 2a does nothing on this host (E5: `user1` cannot `ALTER ROLE app_rw` even with `CREATEROLE`). 2c keeps the REVOKE only nominally — measured, `SET ROLE app_rw; DISCARD ALL; SELECT current_user` → `user1`, so a pooler reset or any bug in the application reaches the owner and `DROP TRIGGER`. 2d is 2c without pretending. Under 2b §6.1's sentence holds verbatim and the REVOKE constrains the thing it names. The step the run-as account cannot perform: `sudo -u postgres psql -c "ALTER ROLE app_rw LOGIN PASSWORD '<generated>'"` on this host; `CREATE ROLE app_rw LOGIN PASSWORD '…' ROLE user1` on a fresh cluster (`ROLE user1` keeps the membership that lets tests `SET ROLE app_rw`). Neither could be run here; the acceptance for the step is `psql "postgresql://app_rw:<pw>@127.0.0.1:5432/soc_dev" -Atc "select session_user, current_user"` → `app_rw|app_rw`, after which `RESET ROLE` has nowhere to go. **It is a P2 prerequisite, not a P1 one:** 017 only grants and revokes, and its tests use `SET ROLE app_rw` from `user1`, which works today (E2). `has_database_privilege('app_rw','soc_dev','CONNECT')` → `t` already. DSN shapes (§6.3's keys unchanged): `DATABASE_URL_OWNER=postgresql:///soc_dev` (socket, peer); `DATABASE_URL=postgresql://app_rw:<pw>@127.0.0.1:5432/soc_dev` (TCP, scram — E6); `TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql:///soc_p1t0N_test`, one per task. The password lives in `.env` beside the two secrets already there; `.env` is git-ignored (`.gitignore:23`).
  - **3 · 017 never runs `CREATE ROLE`.** E4 says it can never succeed when it matters. A `DO` block checks `pg_roles` and raises `migration 017: role app_rw does not exist on this cluster` with a HINT naming HUONG-DAN §0 (measured on a stand-in role name). Failing loudly on the missing prerequisite is the honest form of DEC-020's point 4.
  - **4 · TRUNCATE — confirmed.** `has_table_privilege('app_rw', t, 'TRUNCATE')` → `f` right after `GRANT SELECT, INSERT` and before any REVOKE: an explicit privilege list never grants it, so the `REVOKE … TRUNCATE` is a no-op kept for intent. The statement-level trigger is what stops the owner: `TRUNCATE intake` as `user1` → `append-only table: intake is immutable`, count unchanged. 017 also sets `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES … GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON TABLES TO app_rw` — measured on a table created after 017: `INSERT` → `t`, `TRUNCATE` → `f` — so P2's tables need no grants migration of their own.
  - **5 · G9 — `raw_text text` holds the bytes; `raw_payload jsonb` is generated from it (4d).** `intake` does not exist yet, so 014's shape is free; nothing in `backend/app` reads inside `raw_payload` (grep: zero hits); `alerts.raw_payload` (002) and `rejected_alerts.raw_payload` (001) stay `jsonb`, derived, with no byte claim of their own. Measured: `'{"b":1,"a":2,  "c":3}'` round-trips through `raw_text` byte-identical while `raw_payload::text` is `{"a": 2, "b": 1, "c": 3}`; `raw_payload->>'a'` → `2`; a GIN index on the generated column builds; inserting `'not json'` fails with `invalid input syntax for type json`, so the generated column doubles as the well-formedness check; `server_encoding` is `UTF8` and JSON text is UTF-8 by RFC 8259, so `text` preserves every valid input byte and rejects invalid UTF-8 loudly rather than mangling it. Cost: the payload is stored twice, TOASTed and compressed — tens of MB at pilot volume; `GENERATED … VIRTUAL` would avoid it but is PG18. G9 in §2 and the `intake(…)` line in §6.1 are re-worded. **The byte-compare test** inserts a fixture's bytes as `raw_text`, asserts `raw_text.encode("utf-8") == bytes`, and asserts the control `raw_payload::text != raw_text` — without the control a canonical-form fixture passes against plain `jsonb` too. **For P2:** `json.loads` → `json.dumps` is not "the received bytes".
