The pull path slices the hit object out of the response body (position-tracking decode), the webhook path stores the request body, replay stores the archive line.
  - **6 · P1's exit gate** now reads: "migrations 013–017 apply on a clean `soc_dev` with one command, `scripts/migrate.sh`, on a cluster where role `app_rw` exists; any other cluster needs the one-off superuser step in HUONG-DAN §0 first, and 017 stops with a message naming it." No superuser action is needed on this cluster for P1. The P8 restore drill has the same shape and one unmeasured expectation to test in the drill: a `pg_dump --format=custom` carries `GRANT … TO app_rw` lines, and `pg_restore` into a cluster without the role reports them as errors.
  - **7 · Harness defect A — `backend/tests/test_conftest_helpers.py:55` hard-coded `count == 12`.** Every migration card ends in `make test-db → exit 0`, which became unachievable the moment 013 existed. Fixed to `len(glob("docs/Schema/[0-9][0-9][0-9]_*.sql"))` — 12 today, 13 when 013 merges. Applied directly on `main` by the Owner as a hotfix, together with the `Makefile` `TESTS` hook (item 8) and `.env.example`'s `DATABASE_URL` shape (item 2): **a recorded exception to HUONG-DAN §6**, taken because the fix must precede the first migration merge and the five migration cards are parallel — giving it to one card would serialise the other four behind it. Measured after the change: `make test` 77 passed, `make lint` clean, `TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql:///soc_test make test-db` 3 passed.
  - **8 · Harness defect B — five bare `pytest` acceptance lines skip silently.** Reproduced: with `TEST_DATABASE_URL` unset, `pytest -m db` prints `sss`, `3 skipped`, exit 0; with the cards' extra `-q` (= `-qq`, `addopts` already carries `-q`) the summary line disappears too; `-rs` prints the skip reason. **Rule, extending DEC-006:** a db-marked pytest invocation in an acceptance line (1) sets `TEST_DATABASE_URL` inline to a database whose name ends in `_test` and is private to the task — `soc_p1t0N_test`; the conftest drops, recreates and migrates whatever name it is given, measured `3 passed` on `soc_p1t99_test` — (2) passes `-rs` and never adds `-q`, (3) states its expected result as a numeric `N passed` with `skipped` absent; the Reviewer greps the pasted output for `skipped`, and a hit fails the acceptance regardless of exit code. Whole-suite runs go through `make test-db`, which refuses an unset DSN (measured: exit 1 with no `.env`) and an unreachable one (exit 1), and now accepts `TESTS=<path>` so a single file can go through the wrapper as well. The private database also ends five parallel coders sharing one `soc_test` that the fixture drops and recreates.
  - **9 · Card corrections applied in the same pass**, each confirmed by reading the file or running the statement: T01 — acceptance 6 passes `INDEXER_CA` as an absolute path to the primary checkout (`conf/root-ca.pem` is git-ignored, `.gitignore:26`, and §6.3 forbids an insecure mode), acceptance 8 gains its heredoc terminator, the §6.2 transcription instruction names the enums instead of counting them. T02 — the DDL block gains `BEGIN;`/`COMMIT;`/its `schema_migrations` row. T03 — `raw_text` + generated `raw_payload`, 11 columns, the byte-compare test as in item 5. T04 — six foreign keys, not five (`autoclose_reviews` has two: its PK column is also an FK), every PK and FK named per the card's own rule, acceptances 5 and 8 agree. T05 — acceptance 5 inserts one distinct `subject_id` per value, because 012's partial unique index `ux_jobs_mot_job_song_moi_subject` rejects a repeated `(job_type, subject_id)` pair with `duplicate key value` (measured) before the CHECK is reached; `suggestion_visible NOT NULL` kept and §6.1 annotated. T06 — rewritten in full: no `CREATE ROLE`, `intake` seeded, seven triggers, the re-run line no longer claims to prove conditionality, the schema-wide GRANT it silently required is written. T07 — preflight text updated. "Report and `STATE.md` are not in Files — modify" is the P0 convention and stays.
  - **Not measured, stated plainly:** the two superuser statements in item 2, and `pg_restore` behaviour with the role absent (item 6).
Supersedes: DEC-020 (both Owner questions answered; its gate qualifier kept in item 6's form). DEC-016's residual-weakening line and P1-tasks hand-off item 5 are withdrawn: the `RESET ROLE`/`DISCARD ALL` escape surface exists only when the application session is the owner, which 2b removes. The surviving limitations for `docs/limitations.md` (P8) are (a) `app_rw`'s pre-existing grants on the legacy `soc`, which making it LOGIN exposes to the same password and which only `postgres` can revoke, and (b) the owner's socket session, which is passwordless and can `DROP TRIGGER` — append-only is enforced against the application, not against whoever holds the `user1` OS account.

## DEC-024 · 2026-09-05 · The superuser grant: gate qualifier partly dropped, append-only limitation corrected, four cluster facts settled
Scope: contract
Decided by: Director (all six)
Drafted by: Director (Claude) — stated explicitly because item (f) below makes it the rule · **left uncommitted — the commit is the Owner's, with DEC-023 and Fable's fix set**
Context: The Owner completed DEC-023's superuser prerequisite and went past item 6: `app_rw` is now a LOGIN role and `user1` is a superuser. Re-measured here without sudo — `app_rw` login=t super=f; `user1` super=t; `psql "$DATABASE_URL" -Atc "select session_user, current_user"` → `app_rw|app_rw`; `pg_authid` 18 rows; `CREATE ROLE`/`DROP ROLE` succeed as `user1`. `user1` shows `rolcreaterole=f`, which is not a gap — superuser bypasses the attribute, as the Owner says.
Consequences:
  - **(a) The gate qualifier drops for the SCHEMA path only — the proposal misses a credential.** `CREATE ROLE` now succeeds as `user1` (verified), so DEC-023 item 3's "017 never runs `CREATE ROLE`" is superseded: 017 may create `app_rw` when absent, and migrations 013–017 become one repeatable command on any cluster whose migrator can create roles. **But a role 017 creates cannot be connected to.** `app_rw` authenticates over TCP with a **SCRAM-SHA-256 password** (`DATABASE_URL=postgresql://app_rw:…@127.0.0.1:5432/soc_dev`), and a LOGIN role created without one is unusable: measured, a passwordless LOGIN role fails peer authentication on the socket, and TCP has nothing to offer. A password cannot go in the migration — DEC-022 established the remote is a public GitHub repository. **Therefore: 017 creates `app_rw` as `NOLOGIN`.** That is structural and sufficient — `GRANT`/`REVOKE` and the append-only enforcement are all meaningful against a NOLOGIN role. Granting it `LOGIN` and a password stays a one-off out-of-band step per cluster, in `HUONG-DAN` §0, never in the repo. So the exit gate reads: **"migrations 013–017 apply on a clean `soc_dev` with one command on any cluster whose migrator can create roles"** — the "where role `app_rw` exists" qualifier is gone. The **P8 restore drill** runs that same one command for the schema; if the drill also asserts the application *connects*, it needs the credential step, and that belongs in the drill's script rather than being discovered during it.
  - **(b) The limitation is real, but narrower than I first wrote — corrected by the Owner, re-measured.** My first measurement was malformed: I ran the `REVOKE` on one table and the trigger on a **different** table, then combined the two results into a claim about a configuration neither table had. That is the inverse of the failure `director.md` warns about — reporting a breach observed on a path the system does not have. Re-measured on **one** table carrying both layers exactly as 017 builds them (`GRANT SELECT, INSERT` + `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE` + `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE FOR EACH ROW` + `BEFORE TRUNCATE FOR EACH STATEMENT`), three deletes:
      1. as `app_rw` → `permission denied for table t`
      2. as `user1`, **superuser, no trick** → `append-only table: t is immutable (DELETE)` — **the trigger fires**
      3. as `user1` with an explicit `SET session_replication_role = 'replica'` → `DELETE 3`, 0 rows left
    So it is **not** both layers off. The **privilege layer no longer binds the owner; the trigger still does**, and defeating it takes one deliberately named statement that exists for logical replication and has no other reason to appear in this codebase. That distinction is the whole substance of the limitation: **the owner cannot breach append-only by accident.** "Both layers off" would have implied they could, and would have overstated the risk in a thesis limitation — the opposite error from hiding it. `docs/limitations.md` (P8) carries the three-way result above verbatim, not a summary of it. §6.1's wording is unaffected. In a multi-operator deployment the owner and application roles sit on two OS accounts, which is what makes statement 3 auditable rather than routine.

  - **(c) The five probe fixtures ride with the Owner's commit, and P1-T01 uses them.** `backend/tests/fixtures/indexer_sample_rule{521,591,5503,5760,92601}.json` are my P0 exit-gate item 2 output (DEC-015): five real alerts, five distinct rules, verified free of the indexer password. They are **P0 evidence**, so they are committed as such — folded into the same Owner commit that carries DEC-023's item 7 hotfix, rather than inventing a second exception to `HUONG-DAN` §6. **P1-T01 then cites them** instead of synthesising adversarial samples: real documents beat invented ones for a smoke test, and DEC-007 item 4 already settled that `--save-samples` output is runtime output the Owner commits, not a Coder's file.
  - **(d) `domain_rw` is out of scope, and now measurably so.** It is NOLOGIN, not superuser, and holds **30 privileges on the legacy `soc` database and 0 on `soc_dev`** — so 017, which runs on `soc_dev`, cannot touch it whatever it does. 017 names `app_rw` only. One line in the card so no Coder "tidies" a v1 role it does not own; a v1 artefact on a database v3 never migrates is not v3's business.
  - **(e) `soc_p1t07a` — fix the pattern, not the instance.** It holds 14 tables and 12 migrations: a v1-schema dry run, nothing precious. It is also a name **P1-T07's own card uses**, so it is a genuine collision, not just litter. The durable fix is the convention, not a one-off cleanup: **every card that creates a scratch database must `DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS <name>` first.** That is self-healing — T07's first run clears the leftover — and it protects every later re-run, which a manual drop today would not. I am deliberately not dropping it: a destructive action that the card will perform correctly anyway is not one I should take by hand.
  - **Result-intake on the corrected cards.** The two blocking defects I reported are closed and I re-verified both rather than take the report: harness A now binds to `len(glob("docs/Schema/[0-9][0-9][0-9]_*.sql"))` instead of the literal 12, and `TEST_DATABASE_URL=… make test-db` gives `3 passed`; harness B is closed with **zero bare `pytest` acceptance lines across T02–T06**, each carrying an inline private `soc_p1t0N_test` DSN. Spot-checked the card corrections too: T01's `INDEXER_CA` is now the absolute path into the primary checkout, T04's foreign-key count reads `6`, T03 carries `raw_text` throughout (DEC-023 item 5), and T05 now names migration 012's partial unique index. `make test` 77 passed, `make lint` clean.
  - **What is still NOT verified, stated plainly:** the adversarial pass over these cards never ran — all 31 verify agents died on a session limit — and **P1-T07 was never audited at all**. On the P0 audit the adversarial pass refuted 2 of 3 claimed defects, and it later overturned my whole route-D ruling, so the remaining card-level findings are leads rather than facts. T07 dispatches without the pre-dispatch audit DEC-006 requires; that is a knowing exception, recorded here, and it is the weakest link in this dispatch.
  - **(f) Governance: a DEC records who DRAFTED it, not only who decided it.** I opened this intake saying I did not recall writing DEC-023 — which is recorded partly in my name, with its acceptance rule and card corrections attributed to me. Fable drafted it. The content is right and I have verified the parts I rely on, so nothing is being reversed; but reading a decision in your own name that you did not write is a governance smell regardless of whether the content holds, because the next reader cannot tell whose judgement they are trusting, and an error would be attributed to someone who never had the chance to make it. The `DECISIONS.md` format block gains a **`Drafted by:`** line, to be filled whenever the drafter is not the decider, and DEC-023 is retro-attributed to Fable. This is the same class as DEC-009 and DEC-021: a rule is worthless until the template people actually copy carries it.
Supersedes: DEC-023 item 3 (017 may now create the role, as NOLOGIN) · DEC-023 item 6's gate qualifier

## DEC-025 · 2026-09-05 · Every acceptance needs a demonstrated failing case; DEC-024(a) propagated to the artifacts
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Owner (the rule) · Director (the propagation)
Drafted by: Director (Claude)
Context: The Owner corrected two things at once. First, a record correction in my favour: their handed-in "defect 1" against P1-T07 was wrong — they grepped for the SQL spelling `DROP DATABASE IF EXISTS`, while every card uses the shell spelling, and `P1-T07:109` already read `dropdb --if-exists soc_p1t07a && createdb soc_p1t07a`. DEC-024(e)'s pattern had landed; the card was runnable and was asserted otherwise without running the one command that would have shown it. Second, and the one to act on: DEC-024(a) had not reached a single artifact a Coder reads.
Consequences:
  - **The propagation gap, third occurrence.** `P1-T06.prompt.md` design note 3 still read "There is no `CREATE ROLE`, and there must not be one", justified by "`user1` has `rolcreaterole = f`" — false since the superuser grant. Under DEC-009 the Coder transcribes the **card**, not the DEC, so T06 would have shipped a migration refusing to create the role my own ruling permits, and every acceptance would have passed. Fixed in five places: T06 design note 3 (rewritten, with the NOLOGIN reasoning and the loud `insufficient_privilege` failure path kept), `P1-tasks.md:136` (gate qualifier), `P1-tasks.md:214` (the `rolcreaterole` rationale), `01-plan.md:8` (gate qualifier), and two adjacent claims the sweep for it exposed — `P1-tasks.md` still described `app_rw` as NOLOGIN in two rows, which the Owner's own DEC-023 route 2b had made false.
    One of those two mattered more than the qualifier: the row governing whether `conftest.py` connects as `app_rw`. Its old reasoning ("`app_rw` is NOLOGIN — nothing can connect as it") is spent, but the **conclusion is unchanged for a better reason**, now recorded: the password lives only in `.env`, which is git-ignored and absent from every worktree (verified). A test connecting as `app_rw` directly would pass for the Owner and fail for every Coder. Right answer, dead rationale — exactly the kind of thing that rots into a wrong answer the next time someone reasons from it.
    This is the same shape as DEC-009 (reviewer.md after DEC-007) and DEC-021 (reviewer.md after DEC-018): the governing document moved, the artifact people read did not. DEC-024(f) added `Drafted by:` for a related governance smell; this one wants a habit, not a field. **When a DEC changes a fact a card states, the propagation is part of the DEC, not a follow-up.**
  - **The rule: every acceptance must have a demonstrated failing case, not only a passing one.** The Owner's diagnosis, which I accept in full — four defects in this project have been one shape, *an acceptance that goes green while proving nothing*:
      1. `make test-db` gated on docker and exited before ever reaching pytest;
      2. `assert count == 12` would have gone red only once a migration existed, so it passed for the whole window in which it was wrong;
      3. `grep 'GRANT ALL'` matched the DDL's own explanatory comment;
      4. `diff a b && echo IDENTICAL` passes on two empty files, and `build_schema.py --check` run straight after writing the file only proves the file equals itself.
    Add the five bare `pytest` lines that skipped silently (DEC-023 item 8) and it is six. The cure is identical each time and cheap: **break the thing, watch it go red, put it back.** An acceptance that cannot be made to fail is not evidence, and is a card defect to report rather than a pass to claim.
  - **Propagated in the same pass, per the lesson above** — into the artifacts, not only here: `prompts/coder-template.md` gains it as working rule 7 (the generator, so every future card carries it), `prompts/reviewer.md` gains it as a checklist line with the four instances named, and `HUONG-DAN-VAN-HANH.md`'s Planner checklist gains it in Vietnamese next to the existing "acceptance must be a runnable command" line. `P1-T06`'s acceptance 15 is fixed in place as the worked example: `grep -inE '^[[:space:]]*GRANT ALL'`, anchored, with the demonstration spelled out.
  - **Not claimed:** the propagation sweep I launched across the other artifact classes had not returned when this was written. These five fixes are the ones the Owner named plus the two the same grep exposed; there may be more.
Supersedes: — (propagates DEC-024(a); adopts the Owner's acceptance rule)

## DEC-026 · 2026-09-05 · The propagation gap is systemic: 52 sites, and a mechanism instead of another promise
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Drafted by: Director (Claude)
Context: DEC-025 fixed the five DEC-024(a) sites the Owner named and I said there might be more. There were. A four-way sweep of the artifacts agents actually read found **52 gaps, 20 of them high**, across role prompts (15), the context pack and plan (10), the runbook and board (12) and the P1 cards (15). Three of the four areas contained a *frozen contract or an exit gate* stating something a decision had already falsified.
Consequences:
  - **A correction I owe the Owner.** They said P1-T06's acceptance 1 greps for zero `CREATE ROLE` occurrences; I checked, saw only design-note hits, and told them no such grep existed. It is there, at the end of a long line my 160-character output truncated. **They were right and I was wrong**, and the wrongness was the same shape as the error I had just corrected them on: a conclusion drawn from a proxy — truncated output — instead of the thing. Fixed: the acceptance now expects `1`, with the failing case spelled out per DEC-025.
  - **An error of mine the sweep caught.** Inserting DEC-025's rule into `coder-template.md` created **two rules numbered 7**. Chasing that exposed worse: rule 6 still read bare `pytest`, so **DEC-005 had never reached the generator** — I fixed the six P0 cards and not the file that produces every future card. And rule 3 told the Coder to run bare `make test-db`, which is DEC-018/DEC-021 repeated in the one prompt that never got the line: `reviewer.md` was fixed twice, `coder-template.md` never once. All three fixed; the rules renumber 1–11.
  - **Fixed in this pass (the highest-severity cluster and three gates):** §6.1 itself still said "Migration 017 never creates the role" — the frozen contract contradicting the ruling that governs it. §9 still sent people to the compose `db`. §6.3 still said the puller is the *only* path to history, which DEC-014 qualified. DEC-024(a) also reached `P1.md:33`, `HUONG-DAN` §0 item 6, `STATE.md:10`, `P1-tasks.md` ×3 and T06's acceptance. Two gates that would have failed silently: **P5 scheduled backups via a compose cron entry** on a host whose docker daemon is unreachable — backups would never have run, and P8's restore drill depends on yesterday's real file — and **P2's exit gate demanded a backfill from 01/08** that DEC-017 measured impossible, on a gate `director.md` orders checked literally every evening.
  - **Not fixed, and not pretended otherwise:** roughly forty gaps remain, several high — `P8.md` §restore-drill and §limitations, `P4/P6`'s blind-labelling lines against DEC-019, `director.md`'s E5 validation list, `README.md`'s role table, `STATE.md`'s stale blocker rows, and the P1 cards' references to INBOX items DEC-022 already closed. The full list is in the sweep output; it is a backlog with severities, not a claim of completion.
  - **The mechanism, because a fourth promise to remember would be worthless.** Four times now — DEC-009, DEC-021, DEC-025, this — the pattern has been identical: the governing document moved, the artifact people read did not, and nobody noticed until an agent did the old thing correctly. The `DECISIONS.md` format block gains a **`Propagated to:`** line, listing the artifacts edited **in the same commit as the decision**, with `— none needed` written explicitly when that is true. It sits beside `Drafted by:` from DEC-024(f), and it works for the same reason: it forces the author to look, and it makes the omission visible to the next reader instead of invisible until it bites. A DEC without the line is incomplete, exactly as a card without a runnable acceptance is.
Propagated to: `00-context-pack.md` (§6.1, §6.3, §9) · `01-plan.md` (:9, :107) · `prompts/P1.md` · `prompts/P2.md` · `prompts/P5.md` · `prompts/coder-template.md` · `HUONG-DAN-VAN-HANH.md` §0 · `STATE.md` · `tasks/P1/P1-tasks.md` · `tasks/P1/P1-T06.prompt.md`
Supersedes: —

## DEC-027 · 2026-09-05 · A detector for superseded claims, the backlog made durable, one derived artifact
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Owner (the design) · Director (the build)
Drafted by: Director (Claude)
Context: Fifth attempt at the propagation problem, and the Owner's diagnosis of why the first four failed is exact: DEC-009's note, `Drafted by:`, `Propagated to:` and the manual sweep all **report to a human; none detects**. `Propagated to:` is still a self-report — it lists what the author remembers editing. The Owner also caught the urgent half: the 52-gap sweep existed nowhere on disk, so when this session closed it would have evaporated and each gap would have been rediscovered as it bit someone.
Consequences:
  - **1 · `docs/plan/superseded.yaml` + `backend/tests/test_superseded_claims.py` — 65 rules, 33 asserting hard, 32 xfailing.** Scope is 32 forward-looking files; reports, reviews, audits, `INBOX.md` and `DECISIONS.md` are excluded because they are history and **must** keep the old wording. The Owner's prototype numbers reproduce: unscoped, "docker compose" hits 10 files, most legitimately; scoped, the rules give a handful of reviewable hits. A live document that legitimately names a dead thing carries `<!-- superseded-ok: DEC-nnn reason -->` on the line, the way `noqa` works — seven such lines exist today (a card telling a validator to reject `crown_jewel` **by name**, notes saying the `wazuh-indexer` service is inactive, T07 describing the four-migration state before 017 merges). `status: fixed` asserts, which is the regression guard the project has never had; `status: open` xfails, so `make test` stays green while the backlog burns down, and an **xpass** is the signal to flip a row to `fixed`.
  - **The first version of this did not detect, and that is worth recording.** Rows seeded from the sweep carried the agents' long verbatim fragments — median 90 characters. I pasted a dead claim back into `prompts/P1.md` to demonstrate the failing case per DEC-025, and **nothing fired**: a 90-character prefix only catches an exact re-paste, so all 24 guards were near-vacuous. The fix was a second, hand-written layer carrying the **short canonical form** of each dead claim ("never creates the role", "compose cron", "crown_jewel"). That layer immediately found a real defect the four-agent sweep had missed: `prompts/reviewer.md:19` still ran **bare `pytest`** against DEC-005, in the very file the sweep reported clean. A detector that cannot be made to fail is not a detector — DEC-025 applied to DEC-027's own deliverable.
  - **2 · The backlog is durable now.** All 52 sweep gaps are rows, countable with `-rs`, and a Planner can be told to clear its own area's rows before it plans. They no longer depend on a session transcript surviving.
  - **3 · `scripts/gen_env_example.py` — §6.3 → `.env.example`, the one derived artifact.** Same shape as `build_schema.py`, which regenerates `schema.sql` and has never drifted, against a pair that has drifted twice. It regenerates only the block between two markers, so the four Compose-only keys DEC-003 deliberately kept out of §6.3, the comments and the host-specific values all survive; the first run **migrated** the 53 hand-written contract lines into the block rather than appending a duplicate set (verified: 57 keys before, 57 after, none lost, no value changed). `--check` is wired into `make lint`, and its failing case is demonstrated — deleting `RETENTION_DAYS` makes lint fail naming that key.
  - **A regression I caused and this caught.** My DEC-024 annotation to §6.3 wrote "a **LOGIN** role over **TCP**" inside a parenthetical, and P0-T02's acceptance 7 — which re-implemented §6.3's key extraction inline — began inventing `LOGIN` and `TCP` as config constants. The generator strips parentheticals before extracting; acceptance 7 now **calls the generator** instead of duplicating its regex. One extractor, one place to be wrong.
  - **4 · The limit, stated plainly, because overselling this would be the worse failure.** `test_superseded_claims.py` catches a **recorded** claim resurfacing, and it stops known debt evaporating. **It cannot catch a new supersession nobody recorded** — no scanner can, because the input is a decision that was never written down. Only process covers that, and it now sits where there are teeth: `reviewer.md`'s checklist makes a DEC that supersedes a fact **without** a `superseded.yaml` row and **without** a filled `Propagated to:` a `CHANGES` verdict, even when every acceptance passes. The Reviewer is the only role obliged to run commands and empowered to block.
  - **THE LESSON, which outranks the mechanism it came from.** A new guard must be broken before it is trusted — **including a guard whose whole purpose is guarding.** This was the fifth instance today of the shape DEC-025 enumerates, *green while proving nothing*, and the first in which the hollow thing was the mechanism built to stop that class. The detector was seeded with 24 hard-asserting guards whose patterns were 90-character verbatim fragments; every one passed, and not one could have failed for any reason short of an exact re-paste. Nothing in the suite's output distinguished that from real coverage — it read as 24 green guards either way. It surfaced only because DEC-025's rule was turned on DEC-027's own deliverable within minutes of writing it. Generalise accordingly: a test that has never been observed failing is an untested test, whatever it is guarding, and a guard is not exempt from the discipline it enforces.
    **Independently verified by the Owner, 2026-09-06** (recorded because self-verification is exactly what this entry is about): a dead claim pasted into a clean `prompts/P6.md` turned the suite red naming `P6.md:49`, the rule id and the `superseded-ok` escape; removing it went green again. Broken in a file the builder had not touched, by someone other than the builder — which is the standard this entry argues for.
  - **Deliberately not built:** reference-based rewriting of the documents — the theoretically complete fix, which the Owner and I agree costs more than thirteen days can pay.
Propagated to: `prompts/reviewer.md` (the CHANGES rule, the per-review command, and — added 06/09 — the broken-before-trusted rule extended to guards) · `prompts/coder-template.md` (same extension to working rule 7) · `tasks/P0/P0-T02.prompt.md` (acceptance 7) · `Makefile` (`lint` runs `--check`) · `.env.example` (contract block migrated under markers) · plus the seven `superseded-ok` exemption markers in `00-context-pack.md`, `HUONG-DAN-VAN-HANH.md`, `P0-T02/T04/T05.prompt.md`, `P0-tasks.md`, `P1-T07.prompt.md`
Supersedes: —

## DEC-028 · 2026-09-06 · A Reviewer verdict is an artifact or it did not happen; §12's asymmetry closed
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `00-context-pack.md` §12 · `prompts/reviewer.md` (step 4 and the checklist) · `backend/tests/test_review_artifacts.py` (new guard) · `STATE.md` (P1-T02, P1-T03, P1-T06 rows) · `tasks/P1/P1-T03.prompt.md:14` and `tasks/P1/P1-tasks.md:288` (the missing fixture) · `tasks/P1/P1-T02.review.md` and `tasks/P1/P1-T03.review.md` (rescued into the repo)
Context: Both P1-T02 and P1-T03 returned APPROVE, and both Reviewers reported writing a `.review.md` and setting their STATE row to `approved`. The Owner's check found neither artifact anywhere and every P1 row still reading `todo`, and asked whether a verdict existing only in chat satisfies §12 or whether the reviews must re-run.
Decision: **The premise was false — both artifacts existed, so neither review re-runs and both tasks merge.** They were untracked working-tree changes *inside the task worktrees*, which are **sibling** directories of the repo root (`AI_Support_SOC_1_2-P1-T02`), not children of it, so a `find` rooted at the repo cannot descend into them. Both Reviewers reported truthfully. Going forward the verdict must be **committed on the task branch**, and a test enforces it.
Consequences:
  - **Merged after independent re-verification, not on the reports.** P1-T02 @ `bd0091c`, P1-T03 @ `a44c3d3`. Both `.review.md` files were lifted out of their worktrees byte-identical and committed here; without that they stayed invisible to every reader outside their own worktree. **Correction to this entry's first draft, measured rather than assumed:** plain `git worktree remove` *refuses* on untracked files (exit 128, `contains modified or untracked files`) and the file survives — only `--force` destroys it, silently. The primary defect is therefore invisibility, not destruction; the refusal is an accidental safety net, and a Director who hits that fatal while cleaning up is being told there is unrescued work in there.
  - **The integration check neither Reviewer could run.** Each verified their own migration alone. Measured on the merged tree: a virgin database takes 001–014 in one pass, exit 0; re-run gives `0 applied, 14 already present`; `assets.owner`/`role` present with `enrich_cache` gone (013) alongside all three of `intake`/`source_cursor`/`source_heartbeat` (014); `make test-db` → **48 passed** = 3 base + 13 + 32, exactly the sum of the two separate measurements. `make test` 123 passed, `make lint` clean. Both Reviewers' claims to have dropped their scratch databases also hold — only `soc`, `soc_dev`, `soc_test` remain.
  - **The gap that allowed it, stated exactly.** Context pack §12 artifact-tests the Coder ("the task report exists") but only outcome-tests the Reviewer ("the Reviewer returned APPROVE"), and `prompts/reviewer.md` says *write* the file and *update* the row without once saying *commit* them. In the main checkout that omission was harmless — the Director sees the dirty file and batch-commits it, which is how all seven P0 reviews landed. Under DEC-010's worktree-per-task isolation the same convention is a **loss channel**: the Director never looks in a sibling worktree, and removing it deletes untracked files silently. Two correct rules composed into a defect neither contains. §12 is not a frozen contract (frozen = §6 only), so amending it is the Director's call.
  - **The mechanism** (DEC-027's standard: a guard, not a promise). `backend/tests/test_review_artifacts.py` fails when any STATE row reading `approved` or `done` has no `docs/plan/tasks/<PHASE>/<TASK_ID>.review.md` **tracked by git** — `git ls-files`, not `path.exists()`, because the exact failure was a file that existed on disk and in no commit. It trips at the Director's merge rather than at the Reviewer, which is the stage that matters: `done` is the claim that §12 was met. Escape hatch `review-waived: <reason>` on the row, the way `superseded-ok` works.
  - **Broken before trusted, per DEC-027, on all four branches.** (1) It went red on its very first run, on the real defect — both rescued files present and untracked; `git add` turned it green. (2) A fabricated `P9-T99 | done` row with no file at all → red, naming `docs/plan/tasks/P9/P9-T99.review.md`. (3) The same row with `review-waived:` → skipped. (4) A row edited to carry two cells reading exactly `done` → the parse guard fires, so the parametrized checks cannot go vacuous by silently parsing nothing.
  - **A refinement of DEC-025 found while doing that, and the reason this bullet exists.** Counterfactual (4) did nothing on its first attempt — it edited the wrong row, the string never matched, and the suite stayed green. *A counterfactual that fails to modify anything is indistinguishable from a counterfactual that passes.* It was caught only because the second attempt printed whether the substitution had applied. **Rule: a demonstrated failing case must assert that it actually changed the thing it claims to have broken.** Otherwise the red step is itself green-while-proving-nothing — the same shape, one level up, for the sixth time.
  - **The first case to land on the far side of DEC-027's stated limit, and it confirms the limit was stated honestly.** `superseded.yaml` detects the *presence* of a dead claim; this defect is an *omission* — a word `reviewer.md` never said. No pattern can match an absence, so the right response was a different mechanism (an existence test), not stretching the detector to cover something it cannot see. No `superseded.yaml` row is owed here, and that is a fact about the tool rather than an exemption from it.
  - **Card defect fixed, scope confirmed by measurement.** `backend/tests/fixtures/archive_line_5503.json` is required by P1-T03's design note 8 and acceptance 10 but was missing from its "Files — create"; `P1-tasks.md:288` repeated the omission. Both corrected in place, line counts unchanged so the repo's line-number citations still resolve. The Owner's scoping was verified rather than assumed: T04 and T05 use "fixture" only in the pytest sense and name no fixture *file*, and T01's single `fixtures/` mention is a **read**, not a create — so the defect is T03's alone and the template is untouched, as instructed.
  - **P1-T06 is unblocked** — its only dependency was P1-T03, now merged.
Supersedes: —

## DEC-030 · 2026-09-06 · P1 slipped one day — recorded in numbers, absorbed from P8's buffer, escalation trigger set for tonight's gate
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director

P1 has no cut candidates (`01-plan.md:51`), so the only compliant responses were a recorded slip decision or a D-item escalation. A one-day slip is inside the Director's rights; more than one day is the Owner's.
Decision: The slip is **one day**, recorded now. P2's coders start 07/09 (planned 06/09); P2's planning runs today so the day is not lost twice (DEC-031). The day is absorbed from P8's three-day stabilise buffer (D11–D13 → D12–D13). No D-item is cut. **Trigger:** if the P1 gate is not met at tonight's daily gate (06/09 evening), the slip exceeds one day and goes to the Owner with the playbook's recommendation — cut ② (P5) to protect P6/P7.

Actual: P0 consumed all of 05/09 (its budget note planned 10 h in 8 h, then T07 was added and merged that evening — `P1-tasks.md` budget note); P1's first branches were created 06/09 01:28. As of 06/09 02:40: **merged 2/7** (T02 1.5 h, T03 2 h = 3.5 h of 17 h `must`); **in review 1/7** (T01, 4 h — report on the branch, p95 fails). **Updated in the same run:** the Owner dispatched T01's Reviewer while this entry was being written — APPROVE 11/11 committed on the branch at 02:44 (`200a052`, the DEC-028 procedure followed to the letter) — and T01 merged @ `1349edc`, so **merged 3/7 = 7.5 h**, nothing in review; **dispatchable now 3/7** (T05 3 h, T06 2.5 h; T04 2.5 h off the gate); **T07 1.5 h last.** The model decision is not part of T01's `done` — it is the Owner's, and open in INBOX. Feasible today only if the three sessions open this morning — the slots sat idle from 02:12 (P1-T02/T03 merged) because no worktree was created for T04–T06; DEC-031 records why.

Scope: tactical

That relay is the channel DEC-026 measured as failing 52 times.

A hygiene pass for `open` rows that had silently stopped matching (XPASS) found none *before* the edits and **one after them**: `dec004-migration-013-016`.

Rewritten to the DEC-029 gate. **Broken before trusted:** a probe marker whose head names only `DEC-999` turned both rules on `STATE.md:67` red; restored, 140 passed, 0 xpass. Recorded because it cost a round: the one-row flip that retired `dec004-migration-013-016` dropped the `status:` key and the whole suite failed at collection — the parse error was the right outcome, the fix one line, and the lesson is the one DEC-028 already carries: assert the reconstruction, not just the match.

The runbook, README and the card generator now say so; DEC-010's own Consequences had promised this edit on 05/09 and it never landed.

## DEC-032 · 2026-09-06 · The model decision: run A as flash in non-thinking mode; the ≤ 60 s threshold measured the wrong thing; three framing corrections recorded; P1-T08 contract
Scope: model
Decided by: Owner
Drafted by: Director (Fable 5.1)
Propagated to: `00-context-pack.md` §1 (the dedup ratio, measured) and §6.3 (`LLM_THINKING`) · `.env.example` (regenerated) · `DECISIONS.md` DEC-014 (amendment bullet) · `INBOX.md` (2026-09-06 · P1-T01 · DECISION_REQUEST resolved, correction 1 written in) · `STATE.md` (P1-T08 row; T04 queued; Owner actions on the model, on C8, on Planner P5; P1 and P5 phase rows) · `01-plan.md:12,65` · `prompts/P2.md:16,32` · `HUONG-DAN-VAN-HANH.md:153` · `superseded.yaml` (five `dec032-*` rows, all red-step-first)
Context: P1-T01 measured `deepseek-v4-flash` at 100 % JSON, 100 % schema-valid on the first attempt, 0 of 3 injections obeyed, a 33,564-byte prompt accepted — and p95 101.8 s against §4.5's ≤ 60 s. The generated report said "do not accept", the Coder's report §4 argued for accepting, and the Director's INBOX request recommended re-measuring with a non-reasoning model. The Owner answered with the decision below and three corrections to the framing.
Decision: **Run A — but as `deepseek-v4-flash` itself in non-thinking mode.** The DeepSeek catalog on 06/09 holds `deepseek-v4-flash`, `deepseek-v4-flash-vision-exp` and `deepseek-v4-pro` and no non-reasoning chat model; both text models "support both non-thinking and thinking (default) modes", switched by `{"thinking": {"type": "disabled"}}` sent as `extra_body` on the OpenAI-compatible endpoint; `deepseek-v4-pro` is 3× the price and thinking by default. Same weights, same prices, only the reasoning removed — which is the one move that addresses latency and the 95.2 % reasoning overhead together, for well under $0.16. **Then decide with two datasets.** If non-thinking is not materially better, accept flash in thinking mode with two amendments (below). Whichever way it lands, quality is not the issue and is not discarded over a threshold that measures the wrong thing.
Consequences:
  - **The threshold, reframed rather than failed.** §4.5's p95 ≤ 60 s protects nothing measurable in this stack: G7 guarantees no state depends on a model call, ① is a job and never a request handler, and two analysts read a queue daily. The threshold was written before anyone knew the stack would use a reasoning model; 95.2 % of billed output being reasoning tokens is the fact §4.5 never anticipated. Said plainly, at the Owner's instruction, instead of reporting a model failure.
  - **Correction 1 — the number neither side raised, and it decides more than p95 does.** The ≥ 30 KB class: p50 56.67 s, p95 139.10 s, mean 12,807 prompt tokens. `large-1` (rule 92601, 33,564 bytes, 13,234 prompt tokens) took **139.10 s against `LLM_TIMEOUT_S=120`** and completed — 19,523 completion tokens, 18,957 of them reasoning; `large-2`, the same size, took 33.95 s. Latency is reasoning volume, not prompt bytes. The Coder's §4 cited "timeout already 120 s" as headroom when the measurement already exceeds it. For ②, `CASE_PROMPT_BUDGET_TOKENS=60_000` is 4.7× that class: in thinking mode ② would time out as a rule, not an exception. **Precision on the mechanism, verified in code:** the call did not complete because the script skipped the timeout — `call_model` passes `timeout=120` on the client and the request (`eval/smoke_test.py:891,918`), and the report's own §4 recorded the surprise — but because the OpenAI SDK hands the value to httpx, whose read timeout bounds the gap between received bytes, not a non-streaming call's total duration. `LLM_TIMEOUT_S` is therefore not a wall-clock cap anywhere it is used this way, which makes the amendment below a semantics change, not a number change.
  - **Correction 2 — the dedup ratio is measured, not unmeasured.** Owner, 06/09, full 29-day archive, `IDLE_GAP`/`MAX_AGE`/`MAX_SIZE` applied: **30.2 : 1, 2,778 clusters, ≈ 96 clusters/day.** Break-even 12.5, cleared by 2.4×. The number had lived only in chat — DEC-026's defect with the Owner's own data — so it is now in DEC-014 (amendment), context pack §1, `P2.md`, `01-plan.md` and STATE, and five `superseded.yaml` rows retire the "unmeasured" claim at every forward-looking site, each observed red before its fix. P2's T15 becomes a verification of the coded dedup against 2,778, not the first measurement.
  - **Correction 3 — cost sized over the project, not a notional month.** $0.00415/call × 2 calls per cluster × 96 clusters/day ≈ **$0.80/day**; 06/09 → 18/09 ≈ $10, plus ≈ $5 for P7's B2–B4 runs: **≈ $15 of the $30 cap.** Not tight. Expectation for T08, to be measured: 99 % of the smoke test's cost was output tokens and 95.2 % of those were reasoning, so the non-thinking run should cost a small fraction of $0.16.
  - **If flash stays in thinking mode — two amendments, recorded now, applied only then.** (a) `LLM_TIMEOUT_S` → **240 s, defined as a wall-clock deadline the P3 adapter enforces** (thread join / `asyncio.wait_for` / an explicit deadline — not the httpx value alone): 240 s is 1.7× the measured large-prompt p95 of 139 s, sized on a measurement rather than a guess, and it costs only worker occupancy because every call is a job. Raising the number without enforcing it changes nothing — see correction 1. (b) **② is gated on its own size-class measurement before P5**: 60k tokens is untested and 12.8k already took 139 s. The Owner set (b) for the thinking branch; the Director carries it into the P5 brief in either branch — no ② prompt has ever been sent — as a tactical call the Owner can veto.
  - **`LLM_THINKING` enters §6.3 — approved explicitly by the Owner on 06/09, as an Owner decision, not by implication.** `enabled|disabled`, default `enabled` so behaviour is unchanged, env-overridable, sent as `extra_body.thinking.type`, part of every measurement's cache key; required to run option A at all. The first draft of this bullet recorded the addition "as the Owner's by implication" and offered a veto; the Owner's ruling on that wording is recorded with the approval: *recording a frozen-contract change by implication is the precedent that erodes §6* — offer the veto, never assume the approval. `.env.example` regenerated from §6.3 in this commit.
  - **P1-T08 contract** (precedent DEC-018: no card, the DEC is the acceptance contract; `prompts/coder-template.md` rules 1–11 bind as if pasted here; worktree `../AI_Support_SOC_1_2-P1-T08`, branch `task/P1-T08`, created at dispatch). *Goal:* the same 38-alert smoke test with `deepseek-v4-flash` in non-thinking mode, side by side with the thinking run. *Files — modify:* `eval/smoke_test.py` (read `LLM_THINKING` into `Config`, default `enabled`; `call_model` sends `extra_body={"thinking": {"type": <value>}}`; the mode enters `cache_key` so the two runs never share a cache entry; the report header names the mode; a final side-by-side table — JSON %, schema % first attempt and after one repair, p50, p95, ≥ 30 KB-class p95, mean completion and reasoning tokens, `reasoning_content` present, cost — reading the thinking run's numbers from `docs/smoke-test-D1.md` or its cache). `backend/tests/test_smoke_test.py` (the parameter is sent with the configured value; the default is `enabled`; `disabled` yields a different cache key for the same prompt). *Files — create:* `docs/smoke-test-D1-nothink.md`, `docs/plan/tasks/P1/P1-T08.report.md`. *Do not touch* `docs/smoke-test-D1.md` or §6.3. *Acceptance:* (1) `python3 -m pytest -c backend/pyproject.toml backend/tests/test_smoke_test.py -q` → all pass, ≥ 61 tests; (2) `grep -c 'extra_body' eval/smoke_test.py` → ≥ 1; (3) `env -u INDEXER_PASSWORD -u LLM_BASE_URL LLM_THINKING=disabled python3 eval/smoke_test.py --refresh --report docs/smoke-test-D1-nothink.md` → exit 0, 38 calls, provenance `38 live`, header names `thinking: disabled`; (4) the side-by-side table is present and every thinking-side number matches `docs/smoke-test-D1.md`; (5) `git diff --stat main -- docs/smoke-test-D1.md` → empty; (6) `make test` and `make lint` → exit 0; (7) DEC-025 failing cases, shown red once: the fake-client test fails when `extra_body` is dropped, and the cache-key test fails when the mode is left out of the key; (8) spend printed in the summary block, `--max-spend-usd` left at its 0.50 default. *Estimate:* 1 h. *Depends on:* nothing — `main` carries P1-T01. *Reviewer:* verifies these eight lines; `.review.md` committed on the branch (DEC-028).
  - **Dispatch order under the cap of three:** P1-T05, P1-T06, **P1-T08** now — T08 is on P3's critical path (model chosen before D4 08/09); P1-T04 takes the first slot that frees, and is due before P4 (DEC-029).
  - **The Owner's decision rule, with the Director's proposed reading of "materially better":** the non-thinking run passes the very threshold the thinking run missed (p95 ≤ 60 s) while JSON ≥ 90 % and schema-valid after one repair ≥ 98 % hold. That reading is a proposal; the decision is the Owner's, on two datasets.
Supersedes: — (amends DEC-014; resolves INBOX 2026-09-06 · P1-T01)

## DEC-033 · 2026-09-06 · `LLM_TIMEOUT_S` means a wall-clock deadline the adapter enforces — its documented meaning was false everywhere it was used as an SDK timeout
Scope: contract
Decided by: Owner
Drafted by: Director (Fable 5.1)
Propagated to: `00-context-pack.md` §6.3 (`LLM_TIMEOUT_S` annotated) and §7.5 (the "timeout 120 s" convention) · `prompts/P3.md` (adapter design note, with the test requirement) · `STATE.md` (Owner action "Planner P3 (DEC-033)") · `.env.example` (comment outside the generated block; value unchanged) · no `superseded.yaml` row — see below
Context: §6.3 lists `LLM_TIMEOUT_S=120` and §7.5 says "timeout 120 s, 2 retries", and every reader — the architecture, the P1-T01 card, the Coder's report §4 argument for accepting the model — took that as a bound on a model call. Measured 05/09 (P1-T01, `large-1`): a call made with `timeout=120` on both the client and the request ran **139.1 s, returned successfully, `attempts: 1`**. The OpenAI SDK hands the value to httpx, whose read timeout bounds the gap between received bytes, not the total duration of a non-streaming call. The key's documented meaning was therefore false everywhere it was used this way, and DEC-032 had recorded that only inside its model discussion; the Owner ordered it recorded as a contract item of its own, because it outlives whichever model wins.
Decision: **`LLM_TIMEOUT_S` is a wall-clock deadline per model call, and P3's adapter enforces it** — a thread join, `asyncio.wait_for`, or an explicit deadline around the SDK call; expiry raises the adapter's timeout error and is classified transient (job retry). The SDK/httpx timeout is kept as a network-gap guard and is not the bound. The value stays 120 s until the model decision; 240 s if flash stays in thinking mode (DEC-032). §6.3 and §7.5 are annotated in place; the P3 brief carries the rule and requires a test with a fake that stalls past the deadline.
Consequences:
  - **Why a separate entry.** DEC-032 is about which model; this is about what a config key means, and it is the same defect for `deepseek-v4-flash` non-thinking, `deepseek-v4-pro`, or any successor. A P3 Coder who reads only §6.3 must not be able to build the 05/09 behaviour again.
  - **What §6.3 says now.** The key and value are unchanged — the `.env.example` generator confirms `--check` still matches — so this is an annotation of meaning, not a key change; the Owner approves it explicitly as the owner of §6.3.
  - **No `superseded.yaml` row, and why.** The false meaning was never written as a sentence a pattern could match; it was an unstated assumption behind "timeout 120 s". A canonical row for a phrase that appears nowhere would be `fixed` without ever having been red — exactly the vacuous guard DEC-027 forbids. The guard here is the P3 test the brief requires: the fake that stalls past the deadline.
  - **The report's §4 headroom argument is retired on this ground alone.** "`LLM_TIMEOUT_S` is already 120 s" was offered as headroom for accepting the model; the measurement exceeded it and the number bounded nothing. DEC-032 already says so; recorded here so the contract entry is complete without it.
Supersedes: — (annotates §6.3 / §7.5; extends DEC-032)

## DEC-034 · 2026-09-06 · `risk_score` asset weights on the DEC-004 vocabulary: `high 30 · medium 10 · low 0 · unknown 0`
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `docs/phase-4-enrichment.md:170` (annotated in place — nine documents cite that file by line number, so the line count is unchanged) · `INBOX.md` (2026-09-06 · P2-T10 · DECISION_REQUEST resolved) · `STATE.md` (the Director action it closes)
Context: `docs/phase-4-enrichment.md:170` weights the asset term over `crown_jewel|high|normal|low`, two of which DEC-004 abolished; `inventory.validate()` now rejects them, so `soar/risk.py` (P2-T10) could not be written from the spec. DEC-004's own Consequences ordered this follow-up on 05/09 and it was never taken.
Decision: **Option A — `{"high": 30, "medium": 10, "low": 0, "unknown": 0}`.** v3's `high` inherits `crown_jewel`'s 30 because `high` is now the tier that is a hard auto-close block (G8′), which is the role the abolished value played; every worked example in phase-4 survives verbatim under these numbers.
Consequences:
  - Inside my rights: `risk_score` weights are a v1-spec constant, not a §6 item, and no threshold anywhere reads the score — it is displayed and logged, never compared. Nothing in §6 changes.
  - `unknown` scores 0 rather than a middle value on purpose: an asset missing from the inventory is already a hard auto-close block, so giving it risk points would double-count the same fact in the one direction that matters.
  - The spec line is annotated rather than struck: the old map is quoted in the annotation so a reader meeting a v1 transcript recognises it, per the DEC-022 precedent for frozen text.
  - P2-T10's card already carries option A as its default, so no card changes.
Supersedes: — (completes DEC-004's second follow-up)

## DEC-035 · 2026-09-06 · A replay row is recognised by document shape, for P2 only; P6's `lab` is left open
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `INBOX.md` (2026-09-06 · P2-T10 / P2-T13 · DECISION_REQUEST resolved) · `STATE.md` (the Director action it closes, and the P6 hand-off it leaves open)
Context: `intake.via ∈ pull|webhook` has no value for an archive replay row, and the pipeline job reads one `intake` row that carries no source. DEC-019 provisioned `alerts.source ∈ wazuh|lab|replay` but not the place the pipeline learns which rows are replay.
Decision: **Option A — derive it from the document shape, for P2 only.** A pulled indexer hit carries the envelope keys `_id`/`_index`/`_source`/`sort`; an archive line carries none of them. `source = 'replay'` when `via = 'pull'` and the envelope is absent, `'wazuh'` otherwise. P6's `lab` source is explicitly **not** decided here.
Consequences:
  - Chosen over B and C because both are §6.1 changes needing the Owner and a migration `018` for a distinction the data already makes; the shape test was measured today rather than assumed, and A costs nothing that B could not still buy later if P6 wants it.
  - **The narrowness is the point, and it is a liability, not a saving.** P6's loader has exactly the same gap and A does not solve it: a `lab` row is also envelope-free and would derive as `replay`. P6 must either widen the CHECK (B) or add `intake.source` (C) — a decision that belongs with the P6 cards, before the loader is written, and it is on the board as an open Owner/Planner item, not left implicit in this entry.
  - No frozen contract changes; `intake.via` keeps its two values.
Supersedes: —

Decision: **Option A — `case_alerts` holds heads only.** Every UNION arm in ② filters `duplicate_of IS NULL`; ③b keeps handling duplicates exactly as it already does, by setting `case_id` and leaving `status = 'duplicate'`.

Only ② disagreed with the rest of its own section.

## DEC-037 · 2026-09-06 · The job worker's handler table lives in the composition root; §4's package map is corrected to match
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `00-context-pack.md` §4 (the `infra/` and `web/` rows, in place — the file's line count is unchanged) · this entry is cited by `P2-T02` (loop), `P2-T10` (table, `make run-worker`, and the Makefile diff assertion) and `P2-T11` (the `pull` job seed)
Context: the Planner put the job-type → handler table in `web/worker.py`, left `infra/worker.py` naming no handler, and made P2-T10 repoint `make run-worker` at `app.web.worker`. The Owner asked whether that reading of G1 is right, and said plainly to say so if it is not.
Decision: **It is right, and it is forced — there is no other legal home.** G1 reads "infrastructure packages never import tiers", with one allowlisted exception (`soar/pipeline.py` → `ingest`). The handler table must name tier code — `soar.pipeline` for the `pipeline` job, `tier1.triage` for `triage` — so any module holding it imports tiers. `infra/` may not. §4 gives exactly two packages that may import everything, `web/` and `eval/`, and a long-running process belongs in `web/`, not in the evaluation harness. Therefore §4's package map, which lists `worker.py` under `infra/` and mentions nothing of the kind under `web/`, is now stale and is corrected here.
Consequences:
  - **What each module owns.** `infra/worker.py`: the claim/lock/retry/reclaim loop, no handler names, no tier imports — it stays testable without any tier. `web/worker.py`: the entry point, the table, and the first `pull` job seed. `make run-worker` runs `app.web.worker` (P2-T10 makes that change and asserts the Makefile diff is exactly two lines).
  - **`test_import_rules.py` needs no allowlist entry, and that is the check that the reading is right.** `ALLOWED` grants `web` everything and `ALLOWLIST` holds only `("soar.pipeline", "ingest")`; a handler table in `web/` adds no violation, while the same table in `infra/` would need a new allowlist pair — and the file's own docstring says any change to `ALLOWED`/`ALLOWLIST` requires a `DECISIONS.md` id. The design that needs no exception is the one G1 was written for.
  - **Why the package map had to move rather than the code.** §4 is not a frozen contract (frozen is §6 only), so this is the Director's to correct; had the map been frozen, the Owner would be reading an escalation instead.
  - Recorded because two audiences would otherwise have to guess: a P2 Coder reading §4 to place a file, and whoever next edits `test_import_rules.py`.
Supersedes: — (amends §4's package map)

## DEC-038 · 2026-09-06 · E5 on the Planner's P2 output: accepted, with two cards sent back and the two file overlaps ruled sequential
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `STATE.md` (P2 phase row carries the E5 result; the all-cards blocker row; the struck `P2-T14` row; the Planner re-plan Owner action) · committed separately as `plan: P2 tasks` @ `05e77b5` so the Planner's output is one clean commit and this ruling is another
Context: `director.md` E5 lists six mechanical checks. Run by command on `05e77b5`, not from the Planner's report.
Decision: **Accept the P2 plan and dispatch from it. Two cards go back to the Planner for a one-line fix each; neither blocks wave 1.**
Consequences:
  - **What passed, with the command.** 14 `P2-Tnn.prompt.md` + `P2-tasks.md` (`ls`); `grep -l '{{' docs/plan/tasks/P2/*.md` → silent; STATE rows ↔ card files an exact bijection by `comm -3` over both sorted id lists, 14 = 14; every P2 row `todo` except T12 `blocked`; §11's human-only list assigns nothing to an agent — the heartbeat wodle appears only as an Owner action on T11; the budget note is at the top of `P2-tasks.md` (+144 %), which is what E5 requires when the sum exceeds day × 1.3.
  - **Acceptance lines: 149 items, 148 carry a runnable command.** My first pass flagged three; two were false positives I checked rather than reported — T03's item 3 puts its command on the continuation line, and T10's item 7 delegates to design note 5, which spells out the subprocess, the env var and both SQL counts. **The real one is `P2-T09` acceptance 4** ("Determinism and ordering… Paste the three outcomes"): three assertions, no command, no pointer. It is precisely the DEC-025 shape — an item that can be reported green without anything having been run — so it goes back rather than being waved through.
  - **`P2-T11` goes back for the opposite reason: a missing procedure, not a missing command.** Its Owner action (heartbeat wodle + local rule `100999`) has no exact block anywhere in the repository — `chot-v3-14-ngay.md:68` describes D2 in one sentence and nothing states the XML or the `local_rules.xml` entry. P2-T13 sets the standard: its design note 3 carries the root command verbatim, and its Owner action just points at it. T11 owes the same. I did not write the block myself: the manager side is §11 human-only, `user1` cannot read `/var/ossec/` to check the current `ossec.conf`, and inventing a config block I cannot verify is the failure mode this project has spent two days closing.
  - **The two file overlaps are sequential by dependency, and I am letting them stand.** `infra/puller.py` is modified by T11 then T13, and `web/main.py` is created by T08 then modified by T12; both later cards name the earlier one in `Depends on:` and in `Scope out:` ("P2-T13 modifies this file after you"). E5's disjointness rule exists so two coders never hold one file at once, and a `Depends on: … merged` edge makes that impossible. Ordering and parallelism are the Director's, so this is a ruling, not an exception: **T13 is never dispatched before T11 has merged, and T12 never before T08 has merged** — recorded in the wave plan rather than left to a scheduler's judgement on the day.
  - **`P2-T14` is not a lost task** — folded into P2-T10 by planning decision 10, since the import scanner and the `("soar.pipeline", "ingest")` allowlist pair are what T10 both creates and must prove. STATE now carries a struck `P2-T14` row saying so, because the next reader scanning T01…T15 sees a gap, not a footnote.
Supersedes: —

## DEC-039 · 2026-09-06 · Two guards in the detector were vacuous — the seventh instance, and the second inside the mechanism itself
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `superseded.yaml` (`dec-024-07`, `dec-024-08` — patterns repointed, `pattern_note` added to each) · `tasks/P1/P1-T06.prompt.md:31` · `tasks/P1/P1-tasks.md:322` · `STATE.md` (P1-T06 row: exemption marker, and a table terminator this run had dropped)
Context: P1-T06's Reviewer, in a non-blocking note, measured that `superseded.yaml` rows `dec-024-07` and `dec-024-08` are both `status: fixed` — hard-asserted by `test_fixed_claim_has_not_reappeared` — and that **neither can fire**. `dec-024-07`'s pattern joins two source lines with ` -- ` while `_offending_lines()` matches per line, so the full string exists nowhere; `dec-024-08` carries DEC-023 item 3's verbatim sentence and misses the paraphrase actually written. Both dead claims are still in the tree: `P1-T06.prompt.md:31` says the migration "Needs role app_rw to exist on the cluster" and `P1-tasks.md:322` says "no `CREATE ROLE` — a `pg_roles` guard instead", both withdrawn by DEC-024(a), while the suite reported green.
Decision: Repoint both patterns at short canonical forms, correct both documents, and record this as the count it is.
Consequences:
  - **Red step first, per DEC-027, and it is the whole point of the entry.** Both rows were repointed *before* any document was touched; both went red, naming `P1-T06.prompt.md:31` and `P1-tasks.md:322` — the failure they had been structurally incapable of producing since the DEC-027 sweep created them. Then the documents were fixed and the suite went green. Had I fixed the documents first, both rows would have gone from silently-green to green and the vacuity would have survived.
  - **This is the seventh instance of green-while-proving-nothing, and the second inside the detector.** DEC-027 recorded the first — 24 guards seeded with 90-character verbatim fragments — and its lesson was that a new guard must be broken before it is trusted. These two rows were written in that same sweep and were never broken. The pattern that keeps recurring is now specific enough to name: **a long verbatim pattern is a vacuous guard by default**, because the longer the string the more ways the document can drift out from under it, and a guard that never fires is indistinguishable from a guard that passes. Both replacements are short canonical forms, and each carries a `pattern_note` saying why it is short so the next editor does not "improve" it back into a quotation.
  - **Found by a Reviewer, not by me or by the suite** — worth recording plainly. The mechanism did not catch its own defect; a person reading the card against the guard did. That is a limit of the mechanism, not an argument against it.
  - **Two defects of my own, caught in the same pass and recorded rather than quietly fixed.** (1) Resolving the P1-T06 merge conflict, I appended a note to the STATE row and dropped its closing `|`, breaking the markdown row; a check across every `| P` row now confirms none is missing a terminator. (2) My first correction to `P1-tasks.md` quoted the dead phrase — *DEC-024(a) withdrew the earlier "never creates the role" ruling* — which tripped the canonical `dec024a-never-creates-role` rule. The rule was right and I was wrong: a correction that re-asserts the dead sentence in order to deny it still puts the sentence in a forward-looking document. Rephrased to describe the withdrawal without quoting it. Where a mention genuinely must quote it — the STATE row reporting that the claim is still written elsewhere — the line carries a `superseded-ok` marker naming DEC-024(a).
  - The Reviewer's other three notes are non-blocking and recorded on the task row: `insufficient_privilege` **is** reachable (the report understates its own coverage, an error in the safe direction), the TRUNCATE statement-trigger test is redundant rather than discriminating, and acceptance 12's `N ≥ 12` should have been a numeric count per DEC-023 item 8.
Supersedes: —

Consequences:

## DEC-042 · 2026-09-06 · `LLM_THINKING=disabled` becomes the default; the accuracy question becomes a paired P7 ablation
Scope: model
Decided by: Owner
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `00-context-pack.md` §6.3 (default flipped) · `.env.example` · `01-plan.md` (P7 row) · `prompts/P7.md` (the paired ablation, the exit gate, **and the cache key**) · `STATE.md` (the model Owner action closed, P7 note)
Context: P1-T08 re-measured the 38-alert smoke test with DeepSeek's thinking mode off. All five §4.5 thresholds pass: p95 3.65 s against 101.82 s, the ≥ 30 KB class 2.82 s against 139.10 s, $0.0091 against $0.1577, with JSON and schema validity identical at 100 %.
Decision: **`LLM_THINKING=disabled` is the default.** `LLM_THINKING` stays a §6.3 knob so P7 can flip it.
Consequences:
  - **What this settles and what it does not, stated as the caveat the report itself raised.** The two runs are **not the same 38 alerts** — the acceptance command passes `--refresh`, which re-fetched. So this settles **latency, output format and cost** and settles **nothing about verdict accuracy**. That is not left as an open question: it becomes a P7 deliverable.
  - **The paired ablation, and why P7 rather than here.** P7 runs **B4 twice on the frozen gold set**, `enabled` against `disabled`, reported per cluster with McNemar on the disagreements — same gold sha, same prompts, same clusters, one variable. ≈ 440 clusters × 2 × $0.00415 ≈ **$1.83**. The smoke test measures *format* on **unpaired** samples; the gold set measures *judgement* on **identical** ones. A paired comparison is the only kind that can answer an accuracy question, and the gold set is the only place in this build where inputs are identical by construction.
  - **A defect found while writing the ablation into the brief, which would have voided it silently.** `prompts/P7.md` specified the eval cache as `sha256(prompt + model + template)` — **no thinking mode**. Both B4 runs would have hit the same cache entries, replayed one mode's answers for both arms, and reported a null difference with every command green. This is the same defect P1-T08 had to fix in `eval/smoke_test.py`, one document downstream. The key now includes `LLM_THINKING` and the brief says why in the line itself.
  - **Why disabling is architecturally coherent and not merely cheap.** D9 and D10 build the system on *not trusting the model's reasoning*: the gate re-derives `structured_basis` from database facts, checks every quote is a substring of a named block, refuses `false_positive` without structural evidence, and a facts-only verifier re-derives the verdict. The reasoning trace is never an input to any of that. **95.2 % of billed output tokens were reasoning the gate discards by design** — so removing it takes away something the architecture already declines to rely on. If the ablation shows accuracy depends on it, that is a finding about the gate, not only about the model, and it is better learned on the gold set than in production.
  - `LLM_MODEL_PROPOSER` is unchanged: `deepseek-v4-flash` in both modes, same weights and prices.
Supersedes: — (completes DEC-032's two-dataset decision)

## DEC-043 · 2026-09-06 · Two stale-reference errors: a migration reviewed against a tree that no longer exists, and a "changes vanished" report drawn from a stale snapshot
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Owner (rule) · Director (the second finding)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `prompts/reviewer.md` (the fast-forward checklist line) · `tasks/P1/P1-T04.prompt.md` rule 1 already carries it (written by the other session, 06/09) · this entry
Context: P1-T06 was reviewed at merge-base `a80483e`, before `016` merged; 016 and 017 alter the same five tables, so the combination that actually ships had been run by nobody until the Director checked it at merge time. Separately, another session reported that `DECISIONS.md`'s modification was "gone from the tree without a commit — not mine, another session's."
Decision: **Every migration branch is fast-forwarded onto `main` before the Reviewer runs, and `reviewer.md`'s checklist says so**: `git merge --ff-only main` must succeed and `git merge-base` must equal `main`, or the verdict is `CHANGES`. **And the data-loss report is recorded as a false alarm with its mechanism**, because an unexplained "changes vanished" justifies an investigation nobody needs.
Consequences:
  - **The review rule is structural, not a patch on one task.** A Reviewer verifies a branch against that branch's merge-base; for application code the difference is usually cosmetic, for a migration it is a different database. Anything less means each migration is reviewed against a tree that no longer exists — and the failure is invisible, because every acceptance command passes against the stale base.
  - **The false alarm, measured rather than argued.** `git diff HEAD -- docs/plan/DECISIONS.md` is empty, `git status --porcelain` on it is empty, and DEC-038 is present in both the working tree and `HEAD`. Nothing was lost. The timing corroborates the mechanism the Owner proposed: `DECISIONS.md` last changed in `5e10b22` at 15:39:49, and `1e8d93c` moved `HEAD` at 15:42:07 without touching that file. A session holding a snapshot from before 15:39:49 would have seen the file modified, then clean after the commit, while `HEAD` still looked unchanged *relative to its own remembered snapshot* — so the edit appears to vanish without a commit.
  - **A third instance, mine, in this same run — recorded because it is the sharpest of the three.** I verified the other session's uncommitted work with a net-loss check over `git diff`, found nothing lost, and committed it as `d169b5d`. That commit dropped **all fourteen P2 task rows and the struck `P2-T14` row** from `STATE.md`. My check was not wrong when it ran — it reported 19 removed / 22 added, and 33 removals would have been visible — the session kept editing between my check and my `git add -A`. So I verified one snapshot and committed a different one: the same stale-reference error as the two above, committed inside the run that recorded them. Restored from `5e10b22` and the E5 bijection re-run (14 rows ↔ 14 cards, no diff). **The rule that follows: when committing work another session is still editing, re-run the verification against the exact bytes being staged, not against a diff taken earlier** — or better, do not commit a live tree at all.
  - **Both findings are one shape, which is why they share an entry: a conclusion drawn from a reference that has moved, by a check that could not have detected the opposite.** The Reviewer's acceptance commands could not fail against the stale base; the vanished-changes check could not distinguish "someone reverted it" from "someone committed it". This is the family DEC-025 and DEC-027 name — a green that proves nothing — reached from the other direction, and it has now produced at least four retractions this week, three of them the Owner's own and two of them mine. **The cure is the same in both halves: state what reference you measured against, and prefer a check that can come out the other way.**
Supersedes: —

## DEC-044 · 2026-09-07 · P1-T07's acceptance cases were discriminated by a file count that stopped discriminating; the card is re-cut, the report bullet is the Coder's
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `tasks/P1/P1-T07.prompt.md` acceptance 1 (two independent axes) · `STATE.md` (P1-T07 row, the triage) · no `superseded.yaml` row — no fact was superseded, a card was ambiguous
Context: P1-T07's Reviewer returned `CHANGES` on one blocking finding: `P1-T07.report.md:212` states "The P1 exit gate is not fully met while 015 is absent", which contradicts DEC-029, `01-plan.md:8` and `STATE.md:10` — all three say the gate set is 013/014/016/017 and that it is met — and contradicts its own next clause. All thirteen acceptance commands reproduce; the deliverable is correct and in scope.
Decision: **E3 bucket (b) and bucket (a) together, and the split matters.** The false sentence is the Coder's to fix — one bullet in report §4, no code change, no acceptance re-run, as the Reviewer states. **But the card invited it, and that half is mine**, so it does not count against the task's review-round limit.
Consequences:
  - **The card defect, stated exactly.** Acceptance 1 labelled its cases by *count* — "Case A — five migrations", "Case B — four migrations" — while discriminating them by *which* migration is missing. That was sound until I added the 015-absent rider in DEC-029: from then on a tree with 017 present and 015 absent shows **four** files and reads as case B by its label, and case B is the branch carrying "the P1 exit gate is **not** met". A Coder who counts lands on the gate consequence of a case they are not in.
  - **Attribution corrected 07/09.** This entry first said the gate sentence was "the sentence I put there in DEC-039". It was not: `git log` on the card shows DEC-039's commit (`93bb8a4`) never touched it, and both edits — the 015-absent rider *and* the gate sentence — went in together in `d419f6d`, the DEC-029/030/031 morning run. **That makes the case worse, not better:** the two halves were not separated by a day or by a decision boundary; they were written into the same file, in the same commit, within minutes, by me, and still drifted apart.
  - **The class, named — and this project has not seen it before (Owner, 07/09).** Every failure this week has been an artifact that was wrong when measured against a rule: a vacuous guard, a dead claim, a false report line, a stale reference. This one is different. **The card was correct against every rule. The 015 rider was correct. DEC-029 was correct. The gate sentence was correct.** No artifact was wrong in isolation; what was wrong was the *interaction* between two locally correct rules, and there is no artifact you can open and find the defect in.
    That is why neither review could have caught it. **The review model verifies artifacts against rules, and has no step that verifies rules against each other** — a Reviewer checks a branch against a card, a card against a DEC, a claim against `superseded.yaml`; nothing in the loop asks whether two rules that touch the same text still agree. `superseded.yaml` is the closest thing and it is the wrong shape: it catches a *dead* claim reasserted, not two *live* claims that stopped composing.
    **It will produce this again**, and predictably, at the same seam: wherever one decision adds a case, a rider or an exception to text another decision already conditions. The cheapest partial cover is a discipline the DEC format can carry — when a decision edits an artifact an earlier decision already edited, name that decision and state in one line how the two now compose. `Propagated to:` already records *what* each decision touched, so the overlap is computable from the log rather than remembered; what is missing is the sentence saying they still fit. **Recorded as a named gap rather than closed**, because a mechanism that checks rules against each other is a bigger thing than one morning, and claiming otherwise would be the sixth promise where a mechanism belongs.
  - **The fix is structural, not wording.** Acceptance 1 is re-cut onto two explicitly independent axes — axis 1, is 017 present; axis 2, is 015 present — with the gate sentence bound to 017 alone and axis 2 marked as carrying no gate consequence whatever. The preamble says in terms that neither axis is decided by counting files, and why: with 015 absent, A and B both show four.
  - **What the Coder does:** rewrite `P1-T07.report.md:212` to say the gate set 013/014/016/017 is complete and green under this task's own acceptances 2–3, 6–7 and 10, that 015's absence does not bear on the gate (DEC-029), and keep the existing correct follow-up that 015 must merge before P4. Then set the row back to `review`. Nothing else changes; the Reviewer has already reproduced all thirteen acceptances against the current tree.
  - **The Reviewer's non-blocking evidence is worth keeping on the record**, because it is the DEC-025/027 standard applied without being asked: the 63 new assertions in `test_schema_v3.py` were watched failing under mutation, `build_schema.py --check` exits 0 so the regeneration is provably pure rather than merely plausible, all four sibling `test_schema_v3_0NN.py` files pass unmodified against the full 013+014+016+017 state, and case B was confirmed to be a *collection* difference (53 tests, `skipped` absent) rather than a silent skip.
Supersedes: — (corrects the case structure DEC-029 and DEC-039 left inconsistent)

## DEC-045 · 2026-09-07 · The dispatch rule lands where the actor reads, and a guard makes announcing-without-dispatching fail
Scope: tactical
Decided by: Director
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `prompts/director.md` (morning run step 4 and step 5, E2 step 4, E5's accept line — four points, the file went from **0** mentions of "worktree" to 5) · `backend/tests/test_dispatch_state.py` (new guard) · `STATE.md` (`DISPATCHED:` markers on P1-T04, P2-T02, P2-T04)
Context: on 06/09 the Director printed "Dispatch now: P1-T05, P1-T06, P1-T08" having created no worktree; the miss was recorded, and the rule "the Director creates the worktree at dispatch" was written into `coder-template.md`, `README.md` and `HUONG-DAN-VAN-HANH.md`. On 07/09 it recurred exactly — "three slots, all three filled", zero worktrees, and the Owner built P2-T02's and P2-T04's by hand so dispatch was not held up. The Owner then measured the cause: `grep -ci worktree` gave director.md **0**, coder-template.md 2, README.md 1, HUONG-DAN 3.
Decision: **The rule goes into `prompts/director.md` — the one document the actor reads every run — and a test fails when the board says a task was dispatched and no branch exists.**
Consequences:
  - **The cause is worth more than the miss, in the Owner's words, and it generalises: a fix propagated to every reader except the actor is not a fix.** Yesterday's repair went where the Coder and the Owner read. The Director reads `director.md`, and `director.md` did not contain the word. That is the vacuous guard in prose form — a rule that can never fire because it is not in the loop that would fire it — and it is the same defect as `dec-024-07`'s unmatchable pattern, one layer up.
  - **Dispatch is now an action in the procedure, not a list.** Step 4 says create the worktree *before naming the task in chat*, write `DISPATCHED: <date>` into the row, and **build the announcement by reading `git worktree list` rather than from memory** — so a task that was not created cannot appear in the list. Step 5, E2 step 4 and E5's accept line carry it too, because those are the other three moments dispatch happens; E2 is called out as the commonest place to skip it, since the merge feels like the work.
  - **The guard, and why it checks branches rather than worktrees.** A worktree is local: not in the object database, gone on a clone, so asserting one exists would fail for every reader not on this machine. A branch travels, `git worktree add -b` creates both, and the branch is exactly what was missing — `task/P2-T02` existed nowhere. Two invariants: a row carrying `DISPATCHED:` has its branch, and a row in `in-progress`/`review`/`approved`/`changes` has its branch **without needing any marker**, so forgetting the marker cannot silence it.
  - **Broken before trusted, per DEC-027, on all three branches:** a `DISPATCHED:` marker on `P2-T09`, which has no branch → red, naming the exact `git worktree add` line; `P2-T10` flipped to `review` with no marker and no branch → red on the status invariant; the branch lookup pointed at an empty ref namespace → the guard-the-guard test fires, so the parametrized checks cannot pass on an empty set.
  - **Stated limit.** If the Director creates neither the worktree nor the marker, invariant 1 is silent — nothing in the repository records an announcement made only in chat. Invariant 2 catches it one step later, when the row moves past `todo`. The procedural half in `director.md` is what covers the gap between, and it is honest to say it is a procedure and not a guard.
