  | `manager.name` | `wazuh.manager` in **2,699 of 2,699** documents (08/09: `IA1803`) | `grep -o '"manager":{"name":"[^"]*"' … \| sort \| uniq -c` |
  | agents | 000 `wazuh.manager` **186** · 001 `user1-IA1803` **370** · 002 `HR-computer` **2,143** — **2,143/2,699 = 79.4 %** | `grep -o '"agent":{"id":"[0-9]*","name":"[^"]*"' … \| sort \| uniq -c` |
  | ports | 1514, 1515, 55000, **9200** and **19200** listening; **9400 absent** | `ss -ltn` |
  | `https://127.0.0.1:9200/` | **401** unauthenticated; `subject=CN = 79.79.79.11`, `issuer=CN = Graylog CA`, **`Verify return code: 19`** against `conf/root-ca.pem` | `curl -sk -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}'`; `openssl s_client -connect 127.0.0.1:9200 -CAfile conf/root-ca.pem` |
  | `https://127.0.0.1:19200/` | **401** over HTTPS; plain HTTP → 000 | `curl -sk -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://127.0.0.1:19200/` |
  | `/home/user1/archive/alerts-2026-08-08_09-07.jsonl` | intact, **113,379,904 bytes** | `ls -la /home/user1/archive/` |
  | `docker ps` | `permission denied … /var/run/docker.sock` | `docker ps` |

  **Four corrections to the run note's 22:15 table, measured 25 minutes later:**
  1. **uid/gid 999 *does* resolve locally** — to `dnsmasq:systemd-journal` (`getent group 999` → `systemd-journal`). Those are this host's own accounts that happen to hold 999; they are **not** Wazuh's, and `stat`'s `%U:%G` output must not be read as an owner named `dnsmasq`.
  2. **The file is growing.** 2,624 lines / 6,666,954 bytes at 22:15 → **2,698 lines / 6,903,690 bytes** at 22:40. The manager is live, not a static dump left behind, and any percentage taken off it is a snapshot (78.7 % → 79.4 % for `HR-computer` in 25 minutes).
  3. **Port 19200 also listens** and answers 401 over HTTPS. The note's table names only 9200. There are **two** OpenSearch-shaped endpoints, and question (3) has to name which one.
  4. **`grep -c '"id":"100999"'` is still 0** at 22:40 — 2 h 44 min after manager start. The run note asked me to record timestamps if it were ≥ 1; it is not. The Owner has **not** re-applied the stanza.
Decision: **DEC-061 is amended, not retracted.** Its premise "no manager, no indexer" was **incomplete**: there is a manager, and there are two OpenSearch-shaped endpoints, at least one of which is **not the project's cluster** (different CA, `soc_ro` rejected). No Director decision is available here either — the host is §11 human-only.
Consequences:
  - **What changes in DEC-061's consequences.** "G2 cannot be generated at all" → **not until the Owner re-applies `conf/local_rules.xml` and the DEC-059 stanza on the new manager**; measured proof that it has not happened: `100999` = 0, `1003xx` = 0. "The indexer half is unreachable" → **unreachable until question (3) is answered**; `INDEXER_URL` still names `:9400`, which is dead.
  - **What does not change, and it is still the important half.** The archive is intact at 113,379,904 bytes; **the evaluation spine — intake, dedup, ①, gate, verifier, the blind labeling page, the eval harness — runs end-to-end on `source='replay'` and never was blocked.** Everything dispatched this run is on that half.
  - **The seam with DEC-058, named so the reporter sees it (DEC-044).** DEC-058 ruled that DESKTOP-MIRSO17's **23 archive clusters (0.7 % of 3,070)** stay in G1 and pin `needs_review` — G8′'s first fire, argued as *evidence on a corpus*. That argument **does not transfer to `HR-computer`**, which is a *live* agent producing **79.4 %** of the current stream. `conf/inventory.yaml:10,17` carries `IA1803` and `user1-IA1803` and nothing else, and `P2-T04.prompt.md:43` makes `origin_host` fall back to `agent.name` then `manager.name` — so on today's stream G8′ would pin `needs_review` on agent 002 **and** agent 000: **2,329 of 2,699 documents = 86.3 %**, leaving only agent 001's 370 (13.7 %) able to auto-close at all. DEC-058 stands for the archive; the live estate is **question (5)** and is not decided here.
  - **An inference, labelled as an inference and not as a measurement.** `manager.name` = `wazuh.manager`, uid/gid 999, a `/data/wazuh` bind-mount created 19:55, ports 1514/1515/55000 plus 9200 **and** 19200, and `docker ps` refused — that set is *consistent with* the containerised Wazuh single-node deployment. **I did not verify it and I am not claiming it.** It is why question (1) asks which container and how it is restarted, rather than asking the Owner to describe the host in general.
  - **Merge-cadence check (DEC-043).** This entry asserts no standing equality across branches; it records a host state and five questions. It stays satisfiable after any merge.
  - **The five questions, each with what it bites and when.** These replace the single question DEC-061 left at `STATE.md:70`.
    1. **Was the move deliberate, and does the manager run in a container — which one, and how is it restarted?** Nothing else can be planned around a host that may change again unannounced. Bites every remaining day; costs nothing to answer.
    2. **Where are the new manager's `ossec.conf` and `etc/rules/`, and will the Owner re-apply `conf/local_rules.xml` (9,289 bytes, in the repo) plus the DEC-059 `localfile` stanza there?** Proof, and the Owner can run it alone: `grep -c '"id":"100999"' /data/wazuh/logs/alerts/alerts.json` ≥ 2 twenty minutes after the restart. **Bites the lab day, which is now 15/09** — no rules, no `ransomware`/`data_exfiltration`/`c2_beacon`, and G2 falls back to `01-plan.md:125`'s own cut candidate.
    3. **Is the OpenSearch on `:9200` (or `:19200`) the intended alert store — does the manager ship into it, who owns it (`Graylog CA`), and will a read-only account and CA be created for the app?** This changes `INDEXER_URL`, `INDEXER_CA`, `INDEXER_USER`/`INDEXER_PASSWORD` — **§6.3 values, the Owner's, not mine**; `.env.example` regenerates from `config.py`. Bites P2-T11's one live-smoke line and the indexer half of the P2 gate. **The code does not wait for it** — P2-T11 is dispatched this run against fixtures and a mock transport.
    4. **Do the old manager's dailies for 08/09–14/09 exist anywhere, and do they enter G1?** DEC-053 fixed G1 at the 08/08–07/09 export (30 days, 3,070 clusters); adding days is an **evaluation-validity** decision, not a convenience. Bites P6 sizing, i.e. the moment the Planner runs.
    5. **Do `HR-computer` and `wazuh.manager` enter `conf/inventory.yaml`?** Bites P2-T08/T09's acceptance on real data and the auto-close yield (DEC-012, DEC-058) — see the 86.3 % above.
Supersedes: — (amends DEC-061; conditions DEC-053, DEC-058, DEC-059, DEC-060)

## DEC-064 · 2026-09-14 · Q1 + Q2 — the move was deliberate: the lab was rebuilt as a Docker stack, and re-applying the rules is an Owner **execution** step with a gate, not a decision
Scope: infrastructure
Decided by: **Owner** (both answers, 14/09, quoted verbatim below) · Director (re-derivation, the gate, and the `docker cp` durability warning)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `INBOX.md` (2026-09-14 · P2 / host → the five questions answered) · `STATE.md` (blocker row: moved-and-rebuilt, what is still blocked; the two Owner **execution** rows with their gates) · `tasks/P2/P2-T11.prompt.md` design note 9 (procedure → container form) · `prompts/P6.md` (G2 reachable again **once the gate is green**) · `prompts/P8.md` (relocation + heartbeat-mechanism limitations) · `superseded.yaml` (`systemctl restart wazuh-manager` retired) · **DEC-061** (its "no manager" premise is now fully explained) · **DEC-063** (this answers its questions 1 and 2) · **DEC-056** (route B has a host again — conditionally) · **DEC-059** (the stanza's target moves into the container)
Context: the Owner's words, Q1 — *"Cố ý — tôi dựng lại lab thành Docker stack tối 14/09; manager là container `8e3772d039ed`, restart bằng `sudo docker restart 8e3772d039ed`."* Q2 — *"Áp lại — đây là câu nặng nhất trong năm câu; nó là điều kiện duy nhất để G2 có `ransomware`/`data_exfiltration`/`c2_beacon` (DEC-056 route B), và ~10 phút."*
  **Re-derived by the Director 14/09 23:40–23:46, every command run this session; everything in the hand-off table reproduced, nothing dropped:**
  - Manager = container **`8e3772d039ed`** — `cat /proc/2968231/cgroup` (that pid is `/var/ossec/bin/wazuh-analysisd`). `wazuh-analysisd`, `remoted`, `authd`, `execd`, `syscheckd`, `logcollector`, `monitord`, `modulesd`, `wazuh-db` and five `wazuh_apid.py` workers all running under it (`ps -eo pid,user,lstart,args`).
  - **filebeat runs inside the same container** — pid 2969595, `/usr/share/filebeat/bin/filebeat -e -c /etc/filebeat/filebeat.yml`, `cgroup` → `8e3772d039ed`, supervised by `s6-supervise filebeat`.
  - Indexer = container **`005bea3363a9`** (pid 2966864), dashboard = container **`af6ba99bcec7`** (pid 2967891).
  - **Start times, and this is what "deliberate" looks like in evidence:** indexer `19:55:57`, dashboard `19:56:04`, manager daemons `19:56:14`–`19:56:25` — a **19-second** coordinated bring-up (`ps -o lstart=`), not a failure and not a removal.
  - `/var/ossec` exists **inside** the container; the host has only the bind mount at `/data/wazuh/logs` (`ls -la /data/wazuh`). `/data/wazuh/logs/alerts/2026` and `/archives` were both created `2026-09-14 19:56:18`, mode **750**, gid 999 — so **they hold today only**, and `user1` cannot read them.
  - `/var/run/docker.sock` is `srw-rw---- 1 1001 1001`, mode 660 — **group `docker` (984) does not own it**, so `user1` is denied even though `id` lists `docker`. Every `docker` command below is the Owner's, with `sudo`.
  - **Gate, measured now: `grep -c '"id":"100999"' /data/wazuh/logs/alerts/alerts.json` → 0; `grep -c '"id":"1003' …` → 0.** The file is at 2,735 lines, last timestamp `2026-09-14T16:40:45Z`. **Q2 is answered and not executed.**
Decision: **Q1 is closed — the stack was rebuilt, not lost.** Every DEC-061 measurement stands exactly as written; `/var/ossec` is absent *from the host* because it now lives inside `8e3772d039ed`. **Q2 is recorded as an Owner execution step with a verification gate**, not as a decision and not as a completed fact.
Consequences:
  - **The procedure, rewritten for a container (the Owner runs it; `sudo` required at every line):**
    ```
    sudo docker cp conf/local_rules.xml 8e3772d039ed:/var/ossec/etc/rules/local_rules.xml
    sudo docker exec 8e3772d039ed chown wazuh:wazuh /var/ossec/etc/rules/local_rules.xml
    sudo docker exec 8e3772d039ed chmod 660 /var/ossec/etc/rules/local_rules.xml
    # ossec.conf: docker cp out -> append the DEC-059 localfile full_command stanza -> docker cp in
    sudo docker restart 8e3772d039ed
    ```
  - **The failure mode of this shape, and it belongs in the Owner action rather than a footnote.** `docker cp` writes into the container's **writable layer**: it survives `docker restart` and is **lost on `docker rm` or `compose down/up`**. If the stack is compose-managed, the durable form is a bind mount or a rebuilt image — **the Owner should say which**, because a heartbeat that vanishes at the next `compose up` reproduces the F-02 confusion the heartbeat exists to remove.
  - **`docs/wazuh-manager-changes.md` §4.1 cannot run on this host at all** — it uses `sudo chown … /var/ossec/…` and `systemctl restart wazuh-manager`; there is no such host path and no such unit (DEC-061 measured both). That file is outside `docs/plan/` and stays the Owner's to commit.
  - **The gate, and nothing else counts as evidence (DEC-059's standard):** heartbeat live = `grep -c '"id":"100999"' /data/wazuh/logs/alerts/alerts.json` → **≥ 2 with two timestamps 600 s apart** — startup-only is not enough. Lab rules live = `grep -c '"id":"1003' …` → **> 0 after a scenario runs**. Until the first gate is green, **G2 keeps its `01-plan.md:125` fallback** ("G2 category coverage below 8 — report what exists") and no limitation sentence about G2 is written either way.
  - **Merge-cadence check (DEC-043).** Records a host state, a procedure and two greps; asserts no standing equality across branches, so no merge can break it.
Supersedes: — (answers DEC-063 questions 1 and 2; explains, and does not overturn, DEC-061)

Scope: **frozen contract (§6.3)** / infrastructure
Decided by: **Owner** (14/09, verbatim below) — this is a §6.3 value change and `README.md:49` puts it on the Owner's side; Director records, gates and propagates
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `00-context-pack.md:123` (the indexer paragraph — amended and dated, DEC-060's annotation chain kept intact) · `HUONG-DAN-VAN-HANH.md:14` (the read-only-user step) · `tasks/P2/P2-T11.prompt.md` note 9 verification (b) · `prompts/P8.md` (the `:9400` line) · `STATE.md` (Owner execution row + gate) · `superseded.yaml` (no new row — see the last consequence) · **DEC-001** (the host-service paragraph it wrote is superseded by a container) · **DEC-017**, **DEC-019** (the "indexer for 02/09 onward" half of G1) · **DEC-063** (this answers its question 3)
Context: the Owner's words — *"`:19200`. Việc cần làm: (1) `INDEXER_URL=https://127.0.0.1:19200`; (2) copy CA từ container — `sudo docker cp 005bea3363a9:/usr/share/wazuh-indexer/config/certs/root-ca.pem conf/root-ca.pem`; (3) tạo user chỉ đọc trên Wazuh indexer (dashboard hoặc security API). Đây là đổi giá trị §6.3 → quyền anh, và `.env.example` sinh lại từ `config.py`. Kiểm trước khi hứa: `sudo docker exec 8e3772d039ed filebeat test output` và `_cat/indices/wazuh-alerts-*` — nếu filebeat không trỏ vào 19200 thì không có gì tới bất kỳ indexer nào và puller không có gì để kéo."*
  **Re-derived by the Director 14/09 23:42, by command:**
  - `:19200` → `subject=C = US, L = California, O = Wazuh, OU = Wazuh, CN = wazuh.indexer`, `issuer=OU = Wazuh, O = Wazuh, L = California`. **This is the alert store.**
  - `:9200` → `subject=CN = 79.79.79.11`, `issuer=CN = Graylog CA`. **This is Graylog's store and is not ours** — do not query it for alerts.
  - `conf/root-ca.pem` in the repo → `CN = Example Com Inc. Root CA`, `notAfter=Feb 17 17:00:36 2034 GMT` — the OpenSearch demo CA, matching **neither** live endpoint.
  - The old 9400 cluster is gone from the host: `/usr/share/opensearch`, `/etc/opensearch`, `/var/lib/opensearch` all `No such file or directory`.
  - **Host Logstash is still running (pid 1257923) on the old config, broken at both ends:** `/etc/logstash/conf.d/wazuh-opensearch.conf:7` reads `path => "/var/ossec/logs/alerts/alerts.json"` (gone from the host) and `:17` writes `hosts => ["https://127.0.0.1:9400"]` (dead). Four more conf files point at 9400 (`10-auth.conf:50,62`, `20-journald.conf:23`, `30-syslog.conf:18,30`). Not ours to change — named so nobody reads its presence as a working path.
Decision: **`INDEXER_URL` becomes `https://127.0.0.1:19200`; `INDEXER_CA` becomes the Wazuh indexer's own CA, copied out of container `005bea3363a9`; a read-only account is created on that indexer and `INDEXER_USER`/`INDEXER_PASSWORD` are re-issued.** The Owner writes `.env` and `conf/root-ca.pem` — both git-ignored (`.gitignore:23,26`) — and `.env.example` regenerates from `config.py`.
Consequences:
  - **The Owner attached a precondition to their own answer and it is binding.** *"Kiểm trước khi hứa"* — `sudo docker exec 8e3772d039ed filebeat test output` and `_cat/indices/wazuh-alerts-*`. **The indexer half of P2's exit gate is NOT recorded as reachable until that command has been run and its output pasted.** filebeat runs inside the manager container (measured above); if it does not point at 19200, nothing reaches any indexer and the puller has nothing to pull. An answer is not an execution.
  - **Nine documents cite the §6.3 indexer paragraph**, which is why this has its own id rather than riding inside DEC-064. `00-context-pack.md:123` is amended in place and dated, keeping DEC-060's three-state annotation intact — the line already carries `<!-- superseded-ok: DEC-001 … -->`, which must stay, because the `dec001-wazuh-indexer-service` rule asserts the bare token `wazuh-indexer` hard.
  - **No `.env`, `.env.example` or `conf/root-ca.pem` edit is made by me.** They are the Owner's and git-ignored; `.env.example` is generated and guarded by `make lint --check`.
  - **`backend/tests/test_indexer_probe.py:65,167,181` are left alone, deliberately.** They are fixture URLs inside a test harness and assert nothing about the live host. Recorded here so that no later sweep "fixes" them and breaks a passing suite for a cosmetic reason.
  - **Merge-cadence check.** A value change plus a gate; no standing equality.
Supersedes: DEC-001's host-service indexer paragraph is superseded **as to the endpoint and the CA** (the demo-CA limitation it recorded survives as history, and `dec001-wazuh-indexer-service` stays `fixed`)

## DEC-066 · 2026-09-14 · Q4 + Q5 — G1 stays frozen at 08/08–07/09, and `HR-computer` + `wazuh.manager` enter the inventory before the P4 pilot; neither name is in G1
Scope: evaluation validity / pilot
Decided by: **Owner** (both answers, 14/09, verbatim below)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `prompts/P6.md` (G1 unchanged, stated as ruled rather than pending) · `prompts/P4.md` (the pilot reads the new inventory rows) · `prompts/P8.md` (limitations: the `HR-computer` item is no longer "pending") · `tasks/P2/P2-T08.prompt.md`, `tasks/P2/P2-T09.prompt.md` (the DEC-058 pointer, now covering **both** rulings) · `STATE.md` (Owner execution row: the two inventory rows) · **DEC-053** (its window is confirmed, not changed) · **DEC-058** (option B stands for `DESKTOP-MIRSO17`; this does not overturn it) · **DEC-012** (`is_privileged` reading) · **DEC-004** (the `criticality` vocabulary) · **DEC-063** (this answers its questions 4 and 5)
Context: the Owner's words, Q4 — *"G1 giữ nguyên 08/08–07/09; không thêm ngày."* Q5 — *"Thêm cả hai, trước khi pilot P4 chạy — và điều này không mâu thuẫn DEC-058: lý lẽ của DEC-058 là 'ghi một máy đã biến mất vào kiểm kê là nói sai về hiện trạng'; `HR-computer` là ca ngược lại. Không thêm thì G8′ ghim `needs_review` 79 % luồng sống và sản lượng auto-close của pilot ≈ 0. `DESKTOP-MIRSO17` giữ nguyên option B."*

Consequences:
  - **The general rule behind Q4, worth more than this instance:** adding days changes the **denominator of every figure already published** — 1,812/3,070 clusters `unknown` (59.0 %), the 175 crit+high clusters (5.7 % of 3,070), the severity band (critical 25 · high 150 · medium 1,695 · low 1,200 of 3,070), and A1's sampling design. A corpus is frozen or it is not comparable.

So Q5 changes the **P4 pilot** and changes nothing in G1, G2 or any published cluster count.
  - **Q5 does not contradict DEC-058, and the Owner's reason is the right one.** DEC-058 kept `DESKTOP-MIRSO17` **out of the inventory and in the corpus** because writing a vanished host into an inventory would misstate the estate. `HR-computer` is the opposite case: a live agent producing **2,143 of 2,699 documents (79.4 %)** in the sample measured 14/09 22:40. Left out, G8′ pins `needs_review` on it and on `wazuh.manager` — together **2,329 of 2,699 = 86.3 %** — and the pilot's auto-close yield is ≈ 0, which measures the inventory rather than the system.
  - **What is mine and what is not.** `conf/inventory.yaml` is git-ignored (`.gitignore:24`) and the Owner writes it. My part is the two rows' shape: a `criticality` from DEC-004's vocabulary (`high|medium|low|unknown`), `owner`, `role`, and the check that `validate()` still returns `[]`. The **content** — what criticality a Windows workstation and the manager itself deserve — is the Owner's, and the rows printed in `STATE.md` are a proposal, not a decision.

## DEC-067 · 2026-09-14 · Two card defects of mine, bucket (b): a red step that cannot go red on py312, an R8 counterexample that is a provably equivalent mutant, and an index two fields short of the card
Scope: task cards
Decided by: Director (inside rights — E3 bucket (b): the Coder is not allowed to fix a card defect; I correct `<PHASE>-tasks.md` and the `.prompt.md` and record a DEC)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `tasks/P2/P2-T04.prompt.md:117` (acceptance 4's **Failing case**) · `tasks/P2/P2-T04.prompt.md:121` (acceptance 8's red step, added) · `tasks/P2/P2-tasks.md:231-238` (planning decision 4, five fields → seven) · `STATE.md` (P2-T04 row) · **DEC-025** (its red-step requirement is what these three violate) · **DEC-044** (the seam class: an index and a card conditioning the same contract)
Context: raised by the Reviewer as non-blocking notes B and C on P2-T04 round 1 and carried forward by the Coder rather than acted on — correctly, because a card defect is not theirs to fix. **Each verified by me this run, by command, before writing:**
  - **Note B, acceptance 4.** The card prescribes *"break the `+0700` normalisation → the fallback path raises or differs"*. `python3 -c "from datetime import datetime; print(datetime.fromisoformat('2026-09-02T16:15:36.255+0700'))"` succeeds on py312, so removing the normalisation leaves the fallback parsing the same instant and the test stays green. **This is the same root cause as round 1's blocking finding** — the card and the docstring both believed a false fact about `fromisoformat`, and I wrote the card.
  - **Note B, acceptance 8.** The item had **no** red step at all, and the obvious break — dropping `.astimezone(UTC)` before `.timestamp()` — is a provably equivalent mutant: an aware datetime yields the true POSIX instant regardless of offset. The Reviewer measured the surrounding suite honestly (24 mutations, **17 caught, 7 survived**, two of the survivors provably equivalent) rather than asserting coverage.
  - **Note C, the index.** `P2-tasks.md:232` defined `AlertContext` with **five** fields; `P2-T04.prompt.md:82-83` defines **seven** (`asset_owner`, `asset_role` additionally); `git show task/P2-T04:backend/app/domain/alert.py:65` ships the card's seven. **P2-T08, P2-T09 and P2-T10 read the index paragraph, not the card** — planning decision 4 says so in terms ("Stated in both cards so neither Coder 'fixes' it") — so three unstarted tasks were pointed at a value two fields short.
Decision: **All three corrected in place; no Coder is charged for any of them.** Acceptance 4's failing case becomes "break the no-`fields` fallback lookup" — the substitute the Coder actually ran and the Reviewer reproduced. Acceptance 8 gains the falsifiable break: compute the hash from the **naive wall clock**, which turns `test_cung_thoi_diem_khac_mui_gio_cung_hash` red. `P2-tasks.md` decision 4 is rewritten to the seven-field signature with a note saying which side was wrong.
Consequences:
  - **The acceptance *commands* are unchanged** — only the prescribed red step is. P2-T04 is in `review` as I write this, and a Reviewer already re-ran all twelve items; making the commands move would have invalidated a completed pass for a documentation fix. **No re-run is asked of anyone.**
  - **Bucket (b), so it costs P2-T04 nothing.** Round 1's single (a) finding stands as the only one charged. Two rounds of genuine (a) findings would mean split/cut/change-approach; this is not that.
  - **The rule DEC-025 already carries, restated because I broke it twice on one card:** a red step that cannot go red is not a red step, and an acceptance item with no red step asserts nothing. Prescribing a break is only useful if someone has *run* it — I had not.
  - **Blocking condition for three unstarted cards.** P2-T08, P2-T09 and P2-T10 must not be dispatched against the old five-field index. That is now fixed, so the condition is discharged by this entry rather than carried as a risk.
  - **Merge-cadence check (DEC-043).** Three document corrections; no standing equality across branches.
Supersedes: — (corrects `P2-tasks.md` planning decision 4 and two acceptance items of `P2-T04.prompt.md`; DEC-025 is the rule they violated)

## DEC-068 · 2026-09-15 · The heartbeat blocker, open since 06/09, is closed by measurement: `100999` fires on a 600 s cadence on the rebuilt manager
Scope: infrastructure / P2-T11
Decided by: Director (a blocker closed by measurement, not an option chosen) · **Owner** executed the re-apply (DEC-064 Q2)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `STATE.md` (blocker row closed; P2-T11 row's `Owner action needed?` cell) · `prompts/P6.md` (G2's local rules are loaded on the manager again) · `tasks/P2/P2-T11.prompt.md` note 9 (history line) · **DEC-059** (this discharges the condition it set and retracts nothing) · **DEC-063**, **DEC-064** (their question 2)
Context: **measured by the Director 15/09 13:05, read-only as `user1`.** `grep '"id":"100999"' /data/wazuh/logs/alerts/alerts.json | grep -o '"timestamp":"[^"]*"'` → **six beats**, `05:16:48.595` · `05:26:48.818` · `05:36:49.070` · `05:38:23.904` · `05:48:24.090` · `05:58:24.317` (all `+0000`). Five intervals, four of them **600.223 s · 600.252 s · 600.186 s · 600.227 s** against a target of 600, and one of **94.8 s** which is a restart beat, not a missed period.
Decision: **The condition DEC-059 set is met and the blocker is closed.** `<frequency>600</frequency>` takes effect, so `100999` does not fire only at startup; `HEARTBEAT_MAX_AGE_MIN` (30) keeps a 3× margin and `source_heartbeat.last_seen_at` will not report a live source dead — the F-02 confusion the heartbeat exists to remove.
Consequences:
  - **The same measurement proves two other things, which is why it is worth its own entry.** (1) **`conf/local_rules.xml` is loaded on the manager**: `grep -o 'rule id="[0-9]*"' conf/local_rules.xml` → `100301`, `100302`, `100303`, `100999`, and it is `100999` that is firing — a rule from that file, so the file parsed and loaded. (2) **filebeat ships**: the indexer returns exactly the same number the file holds, `_count?q=rule.id:100999` → **6** against **3,535** documents in `wazuh-alerts-*`. File and indexer agree, so nothing is stuck between them.
  - **`1003xx` → 0 is NOT a failure and must not be read as one.** Those are the three lab rules (T1486 ransomware, T1041 exfiltration, T1071 c2_beacon; DEC-056 route B). They fire only when a scenario runs, and no scenario has been run. The gate for them stays "`grep -c '\"id\":\"1003'` > 0 **after** a scenario", unchanged.
  - **What is still NOT claimed, and I am not claiming it.** Design note 9's synthetic fixture retires *mechanically* — record one real document into `backend/tests/fixtures/indexer_heartbeat_hit.json`, drop `synthetic` from the docstring, and acceptance 5's `grep -c 'synthetic'` flips from `≥ 1` to `0`. That is **Coder work in the P2-T11 worktree**, not `main`, and P2-T11 is not `done` until the Coder reports it. The cadence being proved does not do the swap.
  - **One command I could not run, named rather than skipped (`prompts/director-optimized.md`, edge cases).** The hand-off cites `ossec.log` at `05:16:46Z` printing `wazuh-logcollector: INFO: Monitoring full output of command(600): …`. `/data/wazuh/logs/ossec.log` is `root:systemd-journal` and `grep` on it returns **Permission denied** for `user1`, so **I did not verify that line**. The alert evidence above is independent of it and stronger: the beats are the thing the log line predicts.
  - **Adopt the log-line check anyway, for the next time.** `ossec.log` prints `Monitoring full output of command(N): …` for every command logcollector accepted, so a stanza that never arrived is visible in seconds instead of looking like a beat that has not come round yet. It needs root on this host; it belongs in the Owner's procedure, not in an agent's.
  - **Merge-cadence check (DEC-043).** A measurement and a closed blocker; no standing equality across branches.
Supersedes: — (discharges DEC-059's condition; closes the blocker opened 06/09)

## DEC-069 · 2026-09-15 · DEC-064's procedure corrected: a manager-config change must be written to **both** the live file and the host's bind-mounted source
Scope: infrastructure / procedure
Decided by: Director (a correction to a procedure I recorded; the measurements are the Support Agent's two committed retractions plus my own re-derivation)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `tasks/P2/P2-T11.prompt.md` note 9 (the procedure block) · `STATE.md` (the Owner-execution row) · **DEC-064** (this corrects its procedure and retracts nothing else in it) · `docs/wazuh-manager-changes.md` §0.1 is the working block and is **outside `docs/plan/`** — already committed by the Owner at `969f514`, `1d7efa0`, `b576462`
Context: **re-derived 15/09 13:04.** The stack is the official `wazuh-docker` single-node **4.14.7**, compose world-readable at `/opt/wazuh/wazuh-docker/single-node/docker-compose.yml` (`root:root`, mode 644): `wazuh/wazuh-manager:4.14.7` with `1514:1514`; `wazuh/wazuh-indexer:4.14.7` with **`19200:9200`**; `wazuh/wazuh-dashboard:4.14.7` with `8443:5601`. Two claims in the chain were **retracted by their author and committed** before this entry, and DEC-064 quotes the first, so they are named here rather than left in a file nobody re-reads:
  - `1d7efa0` — *"the heartbeat stanza is already applied"* was **false**. `grep -c 'soc_heartbeat\|full_command'` → 2 was read as both terms present; both matches were stock `full_command` localfiles and `soc_heartbeat` occurred **zero** times.
  - `b576462` — *"the image copies `/wazuh-config-mount/etc/ossec.conf` over the live file at every start, so never `docker cp` an ossec.conf"* was **false on this deployment**. With the stanza in the host file and the manager restarted, `docker exec … grep -c soc_heartbeat /var/ossec/etc/ossec.conf` → **0**; the copy runs only while the `wazuh_etc` volume is seeded, and that volume has held a config since 14/09 19:56. The competing explanation was excluded by measurement too: at **05:04:22Z, past the +600 s mark**, the count was still 0.
Decision: **A manager-config change is written to BOTH files: the live `/var/ossec/etc/ossec.conf` inside the container (via `docker cp`, which is what runs now) and the host's `config/wazuh_cluster/wazuh_manager.conf` (which a rebuilt volume inherits). Neither alone survives both failure modes.** DEC-064's block, which named only the `docker cp`, is corrected here.
Consequences:
  - **Why both, stated as the two failure modes rather than as a rule to memorise.** Live file only → the edit is lost the moment the `wazuh_etc` volume is recreated (`compose down -v`), and the manager comes back configured as it was shipped. Host file only → nothing changes until that volume is recreated, which is what `b576462` measured: the stanza sat in the host file, the manager restarted, and the count stayed 0.
  - **The durability question DEC-064 asked is answered, and the answer is better than expected.** `wazuh_etc:/var/ossec/etc` is a **named volume**, so a `docker cp` into it survives `restart` **and** `docker rm`, dying only on `compose down -v` — verified in practice: `local_rules.xml` is `-rw-rw---- wazuh wazuh 9289` bytes, md5 `afd2ef60d7d3418cbdc27c493ad9eccf`, still present after a restart. DEC-064's warning ("lost on `docker rm`") was correctly cautious and is narrowed by this measurement, not contradicted.
  - **Do not quote `docs/wazuh-manager-changes.md` from memory.** §1–§4 describe the destroyed host installation; §0.1 is the working block. Two of its sentences moved today.
  - **Merge-cadence check.** A procedure correction; no standing equality.
Supersedes: corrects the procedure block of **DEC-064** (the `docker cp`-only form); everything else in DEC-064 stands

## DEC-070 · 2026-09-15 · DEC-065 is MET, and its recorded value is corrected: `INDEXER_URL=https://wazuh.indexer:19200`, not the address form
Scope: **frozen contract (§6.3)** / infrastructure
Decided by: **Owner** (the §6.3 values and the read-only account — `README.md:49`) · Director (the correction, and the verification through the application's own configuration)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `00-context-pack.md:123` (the indexer paragraph — amended and dated; DEC-060's annotation chain kept intact) · `HUONG-DAN-VAN-HANH.md:14` · `tasks/P2/P2-T11.prompt.md` note 9 verification (b) · `prompts/P8.md` (limitations: demo credentials, and the `internal_users.yml` caveat) · `STATE.md` (Owner-execution row closed) · **DEC-065** (this records it met and corrects one value) · **DEC-001** (inverted — see below)
Context: **verified by the Director 15/09 13:06 through the four §6.3 values the application itself reads**, not with admin credentials. `app.infra.config.load()` returns `INDEXER_URL=https://wazuh.indexer:19200`, `INDEXER_USER=soc_ro`, `INDEXER_CA=conf/root-ca.pem`, `INDEXER_INDEX=wazuh-alerts-*`; every request below used exactly those:
  | check | result |
  |---|---|
  | `GET {INDEXER_INDEX}/_count` | **200**, `{"count":3535}`, 6 of 6 shards successful |
  | `GET …/_count?q=rule.id:100999` | **200**, `{"count":6}` — the same six beats the file holds |
  | `POST /soc-ro-write-probe-2026-09-15/_doc` | **403** |
  | `GET /.opendistro_security/_count` | **403** |
  | `curl --cacert conf/root-ca.pem https://127.0.0.1:19200/` | **exit 60**, HTTP 000 — the address form cannot verify |
  `conf/root-ca.pem` now holds the stack's CA — `subject=OU = Wazuh, O = Wazuh, L = California`, `notAfter=Sep 11 12:47:26 2036 GMT`. `/etc/hosts` carries `127.0.0.1 wazuh.indexer`. `conf/inventory.yaml` carries `wazuh.manager` (`high`, `:39-40`) and `HR-computer` (`medium`, `:45-46`) per DEC-066, `DESKTOP-MIRSO17` still absent, and `validate()` → `[]`.
Decision: **DEC-065 is met.** Its recorded value is corrected from `https://127.0.0.1:19200` to **`https://wazuh.indexer:19200`**, with `127.0.0.1 wazuh.indexer` in `/etc/hosts`. The read-only account `soc_ro` exists, scoped to `cluster_composite_ops_ro` plus `read` on `wazuh-alerts-*` and `wazuh-monitoring-*`.
Consequences:
  - **This is DEC-001 inverted, and that is the whole reason the value was wrong.** On the destroyed 9400 cluster the certificate's SAN carried only `localhost`/`127.0.0.1`/`::1`, so the **name** form was unusable and DEC-001 pinned the address. Here the only SAN is `DNS:wazuh.indexer`, so the **address** form is unusable and the name is pinned. §6.3 forbids an insecure mode, so neither host could be worked around; the fix is one `/etc/hosts` line, not a flag.
  - **Two negative proofs are mine; one is not, and I am not passing it off as mine.** I re-ran the write refusal against a **scratch index name** (`soc-ro-write-probe-2026-09-15` → 403, and the index was not created — a follow-up `_count` on it also returned 403) precisely so a mis-scoped account could not destroy live data while being tested. I **did not** re-run `DELETE wazuh-alerts-4.x-2026.09.15`: that probe is only safe if its expected answer is already true, and re-running it to confirm what it is testing would risk today's alerts. The 403 recorded for it is the Support Agent's measurement, cited as theirs.
  - **Two items for `docs/limitations.md`, neither of them a schedule problem.** (1) `soc_ro` lives in the security index while `internal_users.yml` is bind-mounted from the host, so re-running `securityadmin.sh` from those files would **delete the account**. (2) The stack runs on the distribution's **demo credentials and demo certificates**, written in plaintext in a world-readable compose file. Fine for a lab; it must be stated rather than discovered by a reader.
  - **`.env`, `conf/root-ca.pem` and `conf/inventory.yaml` were not touched by me** — all git-ignored (`.gitignore:23,26,24`) and the Owner's.
  - **Merge-cadence check.** A value correction and five measurements; no standing equality.
Supersedes: corrects the `INDEXER_URL` value recorded in **DEC-065**; supersedes **DEC-001**'s address-form pin for the new cluster (its demo-CA limitation survives as history)

## DEC-071 · 2026-09-15 · Schedule: option A — the deadline moves to 02/10, nothing is cut today, and the §10 cut becomes a **dated trigger on 20/09**
Scope: schedule
Decided by: **Owner** (14/09–15/09, both answers) — schedule slip > 1 day and a D-item cut are theirs under `README.md:49`
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `01-plan.md` (title line, phase rows P3–P8, the D14 row) · `STATE.md` (`## Phase gates` P3–P8; the §10 Owner-action row replaced by the trigger; two dated 20/09 checks) · `LICH-TRINH-08-09.md` (marked superseded, **not** edited — its day-by-day from 09/09 is history) · `prompts/P6.md` (the labelling weekend) · `INBOX.md` (2026-09-15 · P2 / schedule → Resolved) · **DEC-062** (this answers its escalation) · **DEC-041** (the re-dating precedent)
Context: the Owner settled the precondition DEC-062 could not assume — ***"18/09 là ngày tôi tự đặt — dời được"*** — so 18/09 was never the faculty's, and options A and C both existed. The answer: ***"A — dời 02/10, chưa cắt"***. Budget against calendar: ≈ **11.5 days** of remaining budgeted work (P2 ≈ 1.5 + P3 1 + P4 1 + P5 1 + P6 3 + P7 1 + P8 3) against **18 calendar days** 15/09 → 02/10 = ≈ **6.5 days of buffer**, the plan's original shape. Against the old 4 days it was short by 7.5.
Decision: **Option A.** The deadline is **02/10**. Nothing is cut today; ② (D11) survives. **The §10 cut becomes a binding dated trigger: if P2's exit gate is not met by end of 20/09, ② goes automatically in §10 order (② → digest UI → health job → login; auto-close kept, DEC-058) with no further Owner turn.** I apply it without asking — that is exactly what separates A from an open-ended slip.
Consequences:
  - **The calendar adopted, with the constraint that shapes it stated first: labelling cannot precede P4, because the labelling page is P4's deliverable.** 15–16/09 P2 closes · 17/09 P3 · 18/09 P4 (pilot starts) · 19/09 P5 · **20–21/09 buffer and the 20/09 trigger check** · 22–23/09 P6 part 1 (lab scenarios on the rebuilt stack) · 24–25/09 P6 part 2 (`build_gold.py`, G1 + G2) · **26–27/09 labelling, two people, blind** · 28/09 gold freeze (sha256 committed) · 29/09 P7 · 30/09–01/10 P8 · **02/10 submission**. The earlier weekend 19–20/09 is a fallback only, and only if P4 lands early.
  - **What the extension does not buy, kept from the escalation because it has not changed.** It does not buy G2 (that needed Q2 executed — now done, DEC-068), the indexer half of the gate (Q3 — now done, DEC-070), **two labellers' availability**, or host stability. The week 08–14/09 produced **zero merged tasks** while five Owner answers sat on the board: the bottleneck was decision→board→dispatch latency, and a calendar does not touch it. **A pays for that week once, not twice.**
  - **The two commitments A is being spent on, both dated 20/09 and both written as Owner actions, not reminders.** (1) **Book the two labellers by name and date for 26–27/09**, written into `STATE.md` — other people's availability is the one constraint an extension cannot buy back, and P6 cannot label without it. (2) **The ② trigger check.** Same date, same place: two checks, one gate, so they cannot drift apart.
  - **Merge-cadence check (DEC-043), and it matters here because a dated trigger is exactly the shape DEC-043 got wrong.** The trigger fires on a date and a gate state, not on an equality between branches, so no merge to `main` can break it and no open branch is put on a treadmill. It is satisfiable after any merge.
Supersedes: **DEC-041**'s dates for P3–P8 and `LICH-TRINH-08-09.md`'s day-by-day from 09/09 onward (both kept as history, neither edited); answers **DEC-062**'s escalation

## DEC-073 · 2026-09-15 · `test_dispatch_state.py` invariant 3 is a DEC-043 treadmill in test form: every merge to `main` turns it red on every open branch
Scope: tooling / process
Decided by: Director (the diagnosis and the two repairs) · **Owner** (the code fix — the file is outside `docs/plan/`)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `INBOX.md` on `task/P2-T10` (2026-09-15 · P2-T10 · BLOCKER → Resolved) · `STATE.md` (Owner action) · `tasks/P2/P2-T10.prompt.md` and `P2-T13.prompt.md` are **not** edited — nothing in either card is wrong · **DEC-043** (same shape, named there as a rule; here it is a test) · **DEC-045** (the guard this invariant belongs to)
Context: **reproduced by the Director before acting, not accepted from the report.** `make test` on `task/P2-T10` → **2 failed, 506 passed**, both `test_dispatch_state.py::test_approved_task_has_something_left_to_merge`, for `[P2-T09]` and `[P2-T11]`. `task/P2-T13`, dispatched at the same commit, carried the **identical** failure — so it is not one Coder's environment. Cause exactly as the P2-T10 Coder reported: both branches were cut at `ea7656a`, `main` then advanced by `30cad5a` (a `docs/plan/STATE.md`-only commit fixing the `--theirs` clobber from the previous batch), and each branch's copy of the board still called P2-T09 and P2-T11 `approved` while their branches were already ancestors of `main`.
Decision: **Option A, both branches.** `git merge main` into each (docs-only, one commit each), with the `STATE.md` conflict resolved **row by row** — the branch's own row kept, `main`'s taken for every other row. Re-measured: `task/P2-T10` → **509 passed, 1 skipped, 409 deselected, 3 xfailed**; `task/P2-T13` → **507 passed, 1 skipped, 417 deselected, 3 xfailed**. Acceptance 14 (T10) and 9 (T13) — the `make test` exit-0 half — now pass.
Consequences:
  - **Bucket (b). Nothing is charged to either Coder**, and both behaved correctly: P2-T10's filed an INBOX BLOCKER with three options and a measurement, P2-T13's disclosed it in their report's Deviations. Neither hid a red suite, which is the behaviour the project is built on. **Option C — wave it off for the Reviewer to reproduce — was rejected deliberately:** a red `make test` handed to a Reviewer spends a review round on a defect that is not the Coder's.
  - **The mechanism, and why it will recur until the test changes.** On a task branch, `main` is the *real* ref (worktrees share the object database) but `STATE.md` is a *snapshot*. Invariant 3 compares the two. So **after any merge to `main`, every open branch whose board snapshot still says `approved` for the merged task goes red through no fault of its own** — the treadmill DEC-043 described, now living in a test instead of a rule. With three coder sessions running and batched merges, this fires on every batch.
  - **Recommended fix, and it stays an Owner action because the file is outside `docs/plan/` (`HUONG-DAN-VAN-HANH.md:186`):** make invariant 3 conditional on the checkout actually being `main` — skip it when `git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` is not `main`, or when `docs/plan/STATE.md` differs from `main`'s copy. Invariants 1 and 2 are branch-safe and must keep running everywhere; only 3 compares the board against live git. **Do not delete the invariant** — it caught the real `P2-T02` silent-revert on 07/09, which is why it exists.
  - **Merge-cadence check (DEC-043), applied to this entry itself.** The repair is two one-off merges and one recommendation; it asserts no standing equality and cannot be broken by the next merge. The thing it *describes* is the equality that breaks — which is the point.
Supersedes: — (does not change DEC-045's guard; names a defect in its third invariant)

  4. **18 transitions tested — MET.** `test_transitions.py` **47 passed, 2 skipped**, both skips declared (`:1274`, `:1292` — *P2-T07 not merged*, a forward dependency in the P3 window, DEC-041).
  Item 2's blocker, measured: as `app_rw` through `DATABASE_URL`, `select count(*) from alerts` → `permission denied`, `from intake` → `relation "intake" does not exist`. As `user1` on the socket, `schema_migrations` holds **12 rows** (`001`…`012`) while `docs/Schema/` holds **17**; `pg_tables` shows the v1 set with no `intake`, no `source_cursor`, no `source_heartbeat`, and `enrich_cache` still present though `013` drops it; `role_table_grants` on `alerts` lists **only `user1`**.
Decision: **The P2 exit gate is 3 of 4. It does not close today, and the missing item is an environment step, not code.** The execution is escalated: every table is empty (`alerts` 0, `jobs` 0, `cases` 0, `audit_events` 0, `enrich_cache` **0**), so `bash scripts/migrate.sh "$DATABASE_URL"` destroys nothing and `017` then grants `app_rw`. I did not run it: it changes the shared application database, and item 2 needs the Owner anyway because `P2-T13.prompt.md`'s scope-out makes the real archive replay Owner-run.
Consequences:
  - **Why 527 passing tests said nothing about this, which is the finding worth keeping.** Every suite builds its own database — `make test-db` creates `soc_test`, each card used `soc_p2t<nn>_test`, and the **P1 exit gate applied `013`–`017` to a *clean* `soc_dev`** (DEC-029's wording), meaning a freshly created one, not the one `DATABASE_URL` names. So the migrations are proven correct and have never been applied where the application runs. **Green everywhere, proving nothing about the one database that ships** — the project's named failure, found in its own environment rather than in a report.
  - **The gate wording did not hide it; the gate wording is what exposed it.** The Owner's 15/09 re-write of item 2 asks for *one real `pull_once`* and *a real replay* rather than a test. A test-shaped item would have passed on fixtures and this would have surfaced in P4, with a pilot on it.
  - **It bites DEC-071's trigger.** The ② cut fires automatically at end of **20/09** if this gate is not met. One `make migrate` plus two runs stands between P2 closing and ② being cut, so the item is dated, not open-ended.
  - **P4 inherits the same database.** Fixing it now costs one command; fixing it during the pilot costs the pilot.
  - **Merge-cadence check (DEC-043).** A gate verdict and an escalation; no standing equality across branches.
Supersedes: — (records the P2 gate verdict of 15/09; does not change `01-plan.md:9`, which the Owner re-worded and which is satisfiable as written)

## DEC-077 · 2026-09-15 · P2-T15's card was written against the wrong corpus and two of its three red steps could not go red; the Coder found all of it and the code is right
Scope: task cards
Decided by: Director (E3 bucket (b) — a card defect is never the Coder's to fix)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `tasks/P2/P2-T15.prompt.md` (a governing banner at the top; acceptance 2's failing cases; acceptance 4's path, `--expect` and line count; design note 4's verdict spec at `:46`) · `STATE.md` (P2-T15 row) · **DEC-025** (the rule all three violate) · **DEC-032** (where 2,778 comes from) · **DEC-053** (where 3,070 comes from) · **DEC-051** (the same denominator error, one corpus earlier) · **DEC-067**, **DEC-072**, **DEC-074** (the same class, fourth occurrence)
Context: **three defects, all mine, all found by the Coder and all verified by me before being accepted.**
  - **Two of the three prescribed red steps cannot discriminate.** Acceptance 2 said `MAX_CLUSTER_SIZE=2000` → file B gives 1 and `MAX_CLUSTER_AGE_HOURS=6` → file C gives 1. File B is **1,001** alerts and file C is **31**, so *deleting* the predicate gives 1 as well: the step could not tell "ceiling raised" from "ceiling gone". This is conclusive by construction, not by a run. Only `DEDUP_IDLE_GAP_MINUTES=30` → file A drops to 2 actually discriminates.
  - **Acceptance 4 named a file that never existed** — `/home/user1/archive/alerts-2026-08-08_09-05.jsonl`; `ls` → `No such file or directory`. The Owner's export is `alerts-2026-08-08_09-07.jsonl`.
  - **`--expect 2778` had the wrong denominator, and so did `lines within 1 % of 83,957`.** 2,778 clusters and 83,957 lines belong to the **29-day / 84,376-alert** export (DEC-032). The file that exists is the **30-day / 92,030-line** export (`wc -l` → 92,030, measured 15/09), for which DEC-053 independently recorded **3,070** clusters.
Decision: **All corrected in the card, and none of it is charged to the Coder.** Acceptance 2 keeps the three original overrides and adds three that discriminate — `MAX_CLUSTER_SIZE=500` (file B must give **3**), `MAX_CLUSTER_AGE_HOURS=3`, `DEDUP_IDLE_GAP_MINUTES=20` — because a cap is only proven read when **tightening** it changes the answer. Acceptance 4 takes the real path and `--expect 3070`, and a banner at the top of the card governs every remaining 29-day figure as history rather than target.
Consequences:
  - **The `FAIL` the Coder reported is the card's, not the code's, and the numbers say so.** Their fold gives **3,051 clusters** from **92,011 parsed** lines (19 rejected: rule id 11, missing `rule.description`) — ratio **30.2:1**, which is the Owner's own measured ratio exactly. Against `--expect 2778` that is **+9.83 %** and exits 1. Against DEC-053's independently recorded **3,070** for the same file it is **0.62 %** apart. Every one of those figures reproduces: 92,011/3,051 = 30.2:1, |3051−3070|/3070 = 0.62 %, |3051−2778|/2778 = 9.83 %.
  - **That 0.62 % is the result P2-T15 exists to produce.** Two independent implementations — the Owner's script in early September and this fold today — run on the same file at different times by different actors, and land within 0.62 % of each other. The dedup predicates as coded reproduce the measured compression. **The verification passes; the card's expectation was pointing at another corpus.**
  - **Changing `--expect` is not bending the assertion to fit the output, and the distinction is the whole point.** It is not moved to 3,051. It is moved to **3,070** — a figure recorded by the Owner, with a different script, before this code existed. The comparison becomes within-corpus instead of across two, and `--tolerance-pct 1.0` still has to be earned.
  - **Fourth card defect of this class in three days** (DEC-067 two, DEC-072 one, DEC-074 two, now three). Every one is a rule stated in one part of a card and contradicted in another, or a figure that outlived its denominator. The guard is unchanged and I keep failing to apply it: when a decision changes a corpus or a constant, grep every card for the old number, not only the one being edited.
  - **Merge-cadence check (DEC-043).** Document corrections; no standing equality across branches.
Supersedes: — (corrects `P2-T15.prompt.md`; DEC-032's 2,778 and DEC-053's 3,070 both stand, on their own corpora)

## DEC-078 · 2026-09-15 · The two execution items of tonight's gate are delegated to one agent as a **card-free acceptance contract**; three of the five stay the Owner's
Scope: process / dispatch
Decided by: Director (inside rights — task ordering and parallelism; no frozen contract, no D-item, no evaluation rule moves)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `STATE.md:73` and `STATE.md:76` (both Owner-action rows now point here) · this entry **is** the acceptance contract — precedent **DEC-018**, **DEC-032**, **DEC-055** (a decision entry stands in for a card when the work is one sitting and needs no Planner)
Context: **where this is recorded, and why not the other two options.** Not a task card: `prompts/director.md` says in terms that the Director does not create task cards, the Planner does, and this is an errand rather than a deliverable. Not a `## Tasks` row either: a row implies a `P<n>-T<nn>` id and a branch, and `backend/tests/test_dispatch_state.py` invariants 1 and 2 are built around exactly that shape — a fake row would make the board lie to its own guard. So: a DEC that carries the acceptance contract, and two pointers from the Owner-action rows that already exist.
  **The board state the agent will meet, measured 15/09 immediately before this entry:** `alerts` **0** · `intake` **95,735** of which **95,694** unprocessed · `jobs` **95,694 `pipeline`, all `pending`**, no `pull` job of any status · `source_cursor` **0 rows** · `source_heartbeat` **0 rows**.
Decision: **Two items are delegated; three are not, and the three are named rather than packaged.**
  **Delegable — both are shell-and-code with no root and no other person's agreement:**
  1. **Gate item 2 (`STATE.md:73`).** Drain the 95,694 pending `pipeline` jobs into `alerts`, and get one real `pull_once` executed so `source_cursor` and `source_heartbeat` stop being empty.
  2. **DEC-073's one-line guard fix (`STATE.md:76`).** Make the edit to `backend/tests/test_dispatch_state.py`, demonstrate it red-then-green, and **leave it uncommitted** — the file is outside `docs/plan/` (`HUONG-DAN-VAN-HANH.md:186`), so committing it is the Owner's.
  **Not delegable, and the Owner's reading is right on the one they named:**
  3. **Booking the two labellers for 26–27/09 (`STATE.md:83`).** **Agreed, and it is the clearest case on the board.** It is two people agreeing to spend a weekend; an agent can neither obtain nor represent that consent. It is also the one constraint DEC-071's fourteen extra days cannot buy back.
  4. **Choosing the canonical Director prompt (`STATE.md:84`).** `director.md` (96 lines, `grep -ci worktree` → 6) against `director-optimized.md` (302 lines, → 15). Not delegable for a different reason: it decides how the Director itself operates, and an agent picking its own governing prompt is the circularity DEC-056 amendment 3 names in another setting. **This one is the Owner's alone.**
  5. **The 20/09 ② trigger check (`STATE.md:82`).** Not delegable and **not the Owner's either** — it sits on the Owner-action list but the work is mine: DEC-071 made it a rule I apply without asking. There is nothing to execute until 20/09. Recorded here so it is not counted as a fifth thing anyone owes.
Consequences:
  - **The acceptance is the gate's own wording, not a proxy, and that is the whole point of the contract.** `alerts` non-empty with `source` observable for **both** halves (`'wazuh'` and `'replay'`); `source_cursor` and `source_heartbeat` non-empty; every number quoted with its denominator. **`intake` filling is explicitly NOT the proof** — that is the exact mistake that cost the 15/09 gate (DEC-076): 95,735 intake rows looked like success and `alerts` was 0.
  - **The agent must not invent a `pull_once` invocation.** The gate names `pull_once`; the CLI exposes only `backfill` and `replay` (`backend/app/infra/puller.py:710`, `:717`). Measured pointer for it to confirm rather than assume: `backend/app/web/worker.py:24` is `HANDLERS = {"pull": pull_job, "pipeline": run_pipeline_job}` and `:32` calls `enqueue(conn, "pull", "indexer")`, while `pull_job` calls `pull_once` (`puller.py` ~`:331`) — so `make run-worker` (`Makefile:78-79`, `python3 -m app.web.worker`) plausibly satisfies both the drain and the pull in one process. **The agent reads that for itself and says where, or reports that no `pull_once` path exists and stops.**
  - **A trap I am naming because the worker's own docstring names it.** `backend/app/infra/worker.py:50-52`: *"A handler's exception never escapes this function: it is classified transient/permanent and the job is finished accordingly — a bad job must not kill the worker."* So **"stop on the first error" cannot be implemented by waiting for the worker to crash** — it will not. The stop condition is observed in the table: `select count(*) from jobs where status='failed'` becoming non-zero. Job statuses are `pending|running|succeeded|failed` (read from the CHECK on `jobs`), and `PULL_INTERVAL_S` is **60** (`config.py:118`), so the `pull` job reschedules itself every minute and the worker must be stopped deliberately rather than left running.
  - **Boundaries.** The agent works against the **shared** application database: no `drop`, no `truncate`, no re-migration, no retry loop. It touches none of `STATE.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `INBOX.md`, `superseded.yaml` — those are mine. It commits nothing outside its own report back to the Owner.
  - **Merge-cadence check (DEC-043).** A dispatch record and an acceptance list; no standing equality across branches.
Supersedes: — (does not change DEC-071's trigger, DEC-073's recommended fix, or DEC-076's gate verdict; it routes the execution half of two of them)

## DEC-080 · 2026-09-15 · DEC-073's prescribed guard form has a hole in detached worktrees; the fix is hardened before the Owner commits it
Scope: tooling
Decided by: Director (a correction to a form I specified) — raised by the DEC-078 agent, who implemented as briefed and flagged the trade-off instead of deviating alone
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `backend/tests/test_dispatch_state.py` — **uncommitted, and it stays that way**: outside `docs/plan/` (`HUONG-DAN-VAN-HANH.md:186`), the Owner commits or strikes · `STATE.md` (the DEC-073 Owner-action row) · **DEC-073** (this corrects the form it recommended) · **DEC-046/DEC-047** (whose route the hole would have blinded)
Context: DEC-073 recommended gating invariant 3 on `git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` being `main`. **That command returns the literal string `HEAD` in a detached worktree** — demonstrated here rather than argued: in `git worktree add --detach <dir> main`, `--abbrev-ref HEAD` → `HEAD` while `rev-parse HEAD` == `rev-parse main`. And `prompts/reviewer.md:11` **mandates** `git worktree add --detach <scratch> task/<ID>` for the shipping-state route, which is also how I run DEC-047's composition checks. So the fix as specified would have skipped invariant 3 **in exactly the place it matters most**, and the module docstring's own motivating sentence is *"Run in a detached worktree at `main`"*.
Decision: **`_on_main()` returns true for `main` by name and for any detached checkout whose `HEAD` equals `main`'s commit.** The alternative DEC-073 floated — comparing `STATE.md` against `git show main:docs/plan/STATE.md` — is rejected: it goes silent whenever the board carries uncommitted edits, i.e. precisely while the Director is editing it, which is when the guard is most useful.
Consequences:
  - **Proved in all three shapes, after hardening.** On `main`: the only skip is pytest's own *"got empty parameter set"* — my guard does not fire. In a **detached worktree at main**: identical, so the hole is closed. On a task branch (`P2-T05`): **4 passed, 2 skipped**, the skip carrying the DEC-073 reason, with invariants 1 and 2 still running. The P2-T05 worktree was restored clean afterwards (`git status --short` → 0 changes).
  - **The blast radius was wider than DEC-073 knew, measured by the agent across all eleven worktrees: nine are red right now** — P2-T04, T06, T08, T09, T10, T11, T13, T16 with one failure each and P2-T05 with two; P2-T02 is green only because the test file does not exist on that branch. **T10 and T13, which I repaired on 15/09 by merging `main` in, have gone red again** at 10 and 14 commits behind. The treadmill is confirmed empirically, not merely argued.
  - **On today's board invariant 3 has an empty parameter set** — no row is `approved` — so "the invariant still runs and passes on main" cannot be shown as a count in either state. The agent did not smooth that over: it proved the guard does not fire and executed the invariant's body directly, getting **PASS on P2-T15** (not an ancestor at the time) and **FAIL on P2-T13** (an ancestor), i.e. the P2-T02 silent-revert detection is intact. That is the right way to report an unprovable count.
  - **Merge-cadence check (DEC-043).** The guard now describes a property of the checkout rather than an equality between the board and a moving `main`, which is exactly what made the original a treadmill.
Supersedes: the guard form recommended in **DEC-073** (its diagnosis and its purpose are unchanged)

Their Files lists are disjoint — verified pairwise, zero overlap.
  - **P2-T07 takes the next free slot, and it is not optional.** `P3-T09` depends on `P2-T02, P2-T03, P2-T04, P2-T05, P2-T06` **and `P2-T07`**, which is `todo` and runs in this window by DEC-041. The Planner amended its card today for the post-P2 world; it is a 2 h task blocking a 3 h task that gates T10 and T11.

It bites if P3 runs past **18/09**, because P4 is dated 18/09 and the labelling page is P4's deliverable, which is what 26–27/09 depends on. `01-plan.md:82` lists P3's own cut candidate — detector heuristics beyond the 20 regex patterns — and that is mine to apply if the day runs out.
