## DEC-082 · 2026-09-15 · The replayed corpus in `soc_dev` is not G1, and the plan said so before it was built; the inventory was never loaded either. Both escalated.
Scope: evaluation validity
Decided by: Director (the verification and the routing) · **Owner** (both remedies — escalated, unanswered)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `INBOX.md` (2026-09-15 · P3 / P4 / P6 · QUESTION → Resolved) · `STATE.md` (two Owner actions) · `prompts/P6.md` and `prompts/P4.md` at the Owner's answer, **not before** · **DEC-079** (the gate it does *not* reopen) · **DEC-053**, **DEC-077** (the 3,070 / 3,051 figures) · **DEC-058**, **DEC-066** (the inventory rulings this makes inoperative on the stored corpus)
Context: raised by Planner P3 as a QUESTION. **Both halves verified by me before being accepted, and both hold.**
  - **(1) The inventory was never loaded into the application database.** `select count(*) from assets` → **0**, `identities` → **0**, `iocs` → **0**. `conf/inventory.yaml` itself is correct — it carries `IA1803`, `user1-IA1803`, `wazuh.manager`, `HR-computer`, and `validate()` → `[]` — but `inventory.load()` / `POST /api/admin/reload-inventory` was never run against `soc_dev`; every P2 card loaded it into its own `soc_p2t<nn>_test`.
  - **(2) The stored corpus is 307 replay heads, not ~3,051.** `select source, count(*) from alerts where duplicate_of is null` → `replay` **307**, `wazuh` **76**, total **383**. Against P2-T15's offline fold of the same archive — **3,051 clusters** (DEC-077) — that is **9.9× fewer**.
Decision: **Escalated, both. Neither is a defect, and (2) is not even a surprise — `P2-tasks.md:270-275`, planning decision 9, predicted it in terms** before any of this was built: *"The dedup predicates anchor on `last_seen_at >= now() − IDLE_GAP` (DB clock, B2/D9). Replaying 29 days of history through the pipeline in an afternoon would put every alert of a key inside one 15-minute window of **wall-clock** time — it cannot reproduce 2,778 and would measure nothing."* Confirmed in the code: `backend/app/ingest/dedup.py:90-91` anchors on `now()`, and `:105` writes `last_seen_at = now()`.
Consequences:
  - **This does not reopen P2's gate, and I want that stated rather than assumed.** The re-worded item 2 (`01-plan.md:9`) asks the history half to prove the archive is *"replayed as `source='replay'`"* — an **ingestion** claim — and the phase objective is alerts reaching `queued_tier1`. Both are met (DEC-079). The gate never asked the replay to reproduce the cluster count, because decision 9 had already ruled that it cannot. **P2 stays closed.**
  - **What it does break is G1, and that is P6's foundation.** A gold set drawn from `alerts` today would be 307 replay heads whose clustering reflects the afternoon the replay ran, not the 30 days the alerts describe. **G1 must come from the offline fold** — `eval/dedup_verify.py`, which reproduces 3,051 within 0.62 % of DEC-053's 3,070 — or from a loader that sets `last_seen_at`/`first_seen_at` from `alert_time` instead of `now()`. **Which one is the Owner's: it is an evaluation-validity decision** (`README.md:49`), and the second option changes append-only history on a table migration 017 protects.
  - **The inventory gap makes three rulings inoperative on the stored corpus, measured rather than inferred.** With `assets` empty, `lookup_status.asset = 'not_found'` and `criticality = 'unknown'` on **383 of 383** stored heads — including the **289** replay heads from `user1-IA1803` (164) and `IA1803` (125), both of which *are* in the file. So **G8′'s asset block fired on 100 % of stored heads**, not on DESKTOP-MIRSO17's 0.7 % (DEC-058); the gate's step 4 forbids `false_positive` on every one of them; and B1 (`kb.apply_table`) cannot return `false_positive` either. DEC-058 and DEC-066 are both about *which hosts are in the inventory* — a question with no effect while the table is empty.
  - **Neither blocks P3, and I checked that rather than taking it on trust:** P3's live gate needs ≥ 50 replayed alerts and there are 307 heads. P3 proceeds at full speed; these bite **P4's pilot** and **P6's sizing**.
  - **The cheap half is one command and it is not mine.** Loading the inventory is `POST /api/admin/reload-inventory` (P2-T08 shipped it) or `inventory.load()` — but `conf/inventory.yaml` and the application database are the Owner's, and doing it changes what every subsequent enrichment reads. It costs minutes; the G1 question costs a decision.
  - **Merge-cadence check (DEC-043).** Two escalations and a verification; no standing equality.
Supersedes: — (does not disturb DEC-079; makes explicit what `P2-tasks.md` decision 9 already said)

## DEC-083 · 2026-09-15 · DEC-082's first half is closed by measurement: the inventory is loaded, the 383 stored heads are permanently `unknown`, and the IoC term is structurally dead
Scope: environment / evaluation
Decided by: **Owner** (ran the load) · Director (independent verification and the two consequences)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `STATE.md` (the DEC-082 Owner-action row, first half ticked) · `prompts/P8.md` (limitations: the IoC term) · **DEC-082** (first half discharged; the G1 half stays open) · **DEC-058**, **DEC-066** (both confirmed against a loaded table) · **DEC-012** (the `identities` content it settled, now actually in the database)
Context: **re-derived by me, not accepted from the report; every figure the Owner gave reproduces.**
  - `assets` **4** — `HR-computer|medium|active`, `IA1803|high|active`, `user1-IA1803|medium|active`, `wazuh.manager|high|active`. `identities` **2** — `root|privileged|active`, `user1|not privileged|active`, which is DEC-012's ruling, now in the database rather than only in a file. `iocs` **0**.
  - **The zero is the file, and I checked rather than assumed:** `conf/iocs.csv` is 31 lines, of which the **data rows are 0** — line 1 is the header `value,reputation,expires_at,source` and every other line is a `#` comment, which DEC-013 made legal. A successful load of an empty table, not a failed load.
  - **The upsert holds.** The Owner ran it twice; `assets` is **4**, not 8.
  - **`lookup_asset` now resolves**, tested against the loaded table through the product function: all four inventory hosts return `status: found` with their criticality, and `DESKTOP-MIRSO17` returns `status: not_found, present: False, criticality: unknown` — which is DEC-058 option B working exactly as ruled.
  - **The 383 stored heads did not move, exactly as predicted:** `asset_context->>'criticality'` × `lookup_status->>'asset'` over `duplicate_of is null` is **`unknown` / `not_found` on 383 of 383**, and the head count is still 383.
Decision: **DEC-082's first half is closed. Its second half — the G1 route — stays open and is the one that matters.**
Consequences:
  - **The 383 stored heads are permanently `unknown` and no supported path changes that.** Enrichment is written into the row at the A5/A6 edge (`transitions.py:311`, columns set at `:337-347`), and the only edge into `enriching` is **A4, from `received`** (`transitions.py:76`) — there is no `queued_tier1 → enriching` transition. So **the P4 pilot must not be run on these heads** without saying what they are: a corpus on which G8′'s asset block fired on 100 % of rows because the table behind it was empty at the time, not because the estate is unknown.
  - **A structurally dead term in the risk formula, worth a limitation sentence rather than a shrug.** `soar/risk.py:23` is `IOC = {"malicious": 25, "suspicious": 10, "clean": 0, "not_found": 0, "skipped": 0}` and `:34` sums `asset + identity + ioc + occurrence`. With `iocs` empty, every alert on this estate scores **`ioc = 0`**, so **one of the four context addends can never contribute**, and no `risk_score` this project reports is sensitive to IoC reputation. That is a property of the estate, not a defect — but unstated it would let a reader believe the IoC path was exercised.
  - **DEC-058 and DEC-066 are confirmed rather than revisited.** The file carries exactly the four hosts DEC-066 put in it; `DESKTOP-MIRSO17` is out, per DEC-058 option B; and the lookup behaves accordingly on both sides.
  - **A denominator footnote, because the same host now has two true numbers.** DEC-058 sized `DESKTOP-MIRSO17` at **23 clusters, 0.7 % of 3,070** in the archive; in the stored corpus it is **18 of 383 heads, 4.7 %**. Two corpora, both right, never to be quoted against each other.
  - **Merge-cadence check (DEC-043).** A closure record; no standing equality across branches.
Supersedes: — (discharges the first half of DEC-082; the G1 question is untouched)

## DEC-084 · 2026-09-15 · G1 takes **Route A**: built from the offline fold, not from a re-load with `alert_time`-anchored clocks
Scope: **evaluation validity**
Decided by: **Owner** (`README.md:49` — anything touching evaluation validity is theirs) · Director (the recommendation and the propagation)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `prompts/P6.md:18` (the `build_gold.py` paragraph — **G1's source clause rewritten; G2's left alone**) · `STATE.md` (DEC-082's second half closed) · **DEC-082** (its open half, now answered) · **DEC-053** (window untouched) · **DEC-052** (nothing filtered) · **DEC-077** (the fold's 3,051 is the figure this route builds on)
Context: the choice was between building G1 from `eval/dedup_verify.py`'s offline fold over each alert's own `alert_time`, or reloading the archive through a pipeline whose dedup anchors on `now()` (`ingest/dedup.py:90-91`, `:105`). The measurement behind the recommendation, and the only one that mattered: **the fold already reproduces 3,051 clusters against DEC-053's independently recorded 3,070 for the same file — 0.62 % apart**, by a different implementation, at a different time, from a different author. That agreement is the project's only evidence that the cluster definition is sound.
Decision: **Route A.** G1 is built from the offline fold. The database's 307 replay heads are **not** G1 and are not to be quoted as a cluster count.
Consequences:
  - **What still has to be built, stated plainly because the route is not free.** `eval/dedup_verify.py` is **read-only** — it defines `read_archive`, `fold_clusters`, `build_report`, `format_text/json`, imports no `psycopg` and writes no row — and its `Cluster` holds `key, first_seen, last_seen, count, closed_by` and **no member-alert ids**. So it can say *how many* clusters the data implies and not *which alerts* form one. **Two pieces of new code: emit per-cluster membership from the fold, and materialise those clusters where the labelling page can read them.**
  - **Which card carries it: P6-T01.** `prompts/P6.md:35` already names it — *"T01 `build_gold.py` + coverage table"* — and `eval/build_gold.py` is a **one-line docstring stub** today, so T01 is written from scratch either way. `docs/plan/tasks/P6/` does not exist. **The route choice does not add a card; it decides what T01's card says.**
  - **Yes — P6's Planner can now be run against a decided source.** That was the thing blocking it, and it is the reason the date in DEC-082 mattered. Nothing else in P6 waits on this.
  - **What changes in `prompts/P6.md:18`, and what deliberately does not.** The clause *"from `source IN ('wazuh','replay')` (G1) and `source = 'lab'` (G2)"* **splits**: G1 no longer comes from `alerts`; **G2 still does, and correctly**, because the lab scenarios run in real time on 22–23/09, so `now()`-anchored dedup is the right clock for them. The stratification (`category × severity`, the `rule.level` band pinned by DEC-053), the targets (300 + 100) and the floors (200 + 60) are unchanged — only where G1's rows come from.
  - **Nothing frozen is re-derived and nothing is filtered.** DEC-053's 08/08–07/09 window is the fold's own window. DEC-052's "keep everything, filter nowhere" is untouched — the fold excludes nothing. Every published figure stands as written: `INVENTORY-2026-09-07.md:18` (A1 severity bands over 3,070), `:19` (A2, 1,812/3,070 = 59.0 %), `A1-A5-decision-material-2026-09-07.md:13`, `prompts/P6.md:17`.
  - **G1 and the verification become one measurement, not two.** That was Route A's point: after this, `eval/dedup_verify.py`'s count and G1's size are the same number, and the standing warning never to quote them against each other retires.
  - **What Route A does not prove, said once so the thesis does not overclaim.** It does not show that the *shipped pipeline* produces this corpus: the fold applies `IDLE_GAP`, `MAX_AGE`, `MAX_SIZE` only, three of the live path's six predicates. The pipeline's own correctness rests on P2-T05's 47 tests and the G7 test, not on G1.
  - **Merge-cadence check (DEC-043).** A source decision and one brief edit; no standing equality across branches.
Supersedes: — (answers the open half of **DEC-082**; leaves DEC-052, DEC-053 and DEC-077 intact)

## DEC-085 · 2026-09-16 · Lab tagging takes **option D**: a post-hoc time-window retag by `eval/lab_tag.py`, no schema change and no migration 018
Scope: evaluation / contract (none touched)
Decided by: **Owner** (DEC-035 left the choice open and it touches evaluation validity — `README.md:49`)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `INBOX.md` (2026-09-16 · P6 / lab tagging → Resolved) · `prompts/P8.md` (the pilot export excludes `source='lab'`) · **DEC-035** (this closes the `lab` gap it deliberately left open) · **P6-T05**'s card, already written for D · `tasks/P6/P6-T01.prompt.md` (`--g2` refuses on an untagged head)
Context: DEC-035 left P6 to choose between widening `intake.via` (B) or adding `intake.source` (C). The Planner measured that **neither can carry the tag on this host**, and the reason is physical rather than technical: **the lab host *is* the production host** — `user1-IA1803`, agent 001, producing ≈ 34 alerts/hour of its own operating noise (`docs/lab-run-log.md:8-16`). An agent-name rule would tag the P4 pilot's real traffic from that machine as `lab` for the rest of the project, and an intake-time column cannot carry a scenario window that is only known **after** the Owner has written the start and end seconds down.
Decision: **Option D.** The Owner runs a scenario, logs its seconds, then runs `eval/lab_tag.py --agent … --since … --until … --scenario … --category … --kind attack|benign`, which updates `alerts.source` to `'lab'` for heads inside the window and appends the window to `eval/lab_windows.csv` — committed, so the tag has provenance.
Consequences:
  - **Nothing frozen moves, and each reason is a separate check the Planner made rather than one claim.** `alerts.source` is not `status`, so G2's monopoly on the state machine is untouched; **`alerts` is not append-only** — §6.1 names `audit_events`, `llm_runs` and `intake`, and I confirmed on 15/09 that `alerts` carries none of the seven triggers; the value `lab` is already inside the existing CHECK. **No `LAB_AGENTS` key, no §6.3 change, no migration 018.**
  - **A forgotten tag fails loudly instead of shrinking G2.** `build_gold.py --g2` selects heads inside recorded windows and **exits 3** when a head in a window is still `wazuh`. That is the difference between this and a retag that silently under-collects — the failure mode a post-hoc mechanism would otherwise have.
  - **Why B and C lose on information, not on cost.** Both need the worker to know at intake time that a document is lab, which on this host means a `LAB_*` key read by a running worker whose config is static: the Owner would restart the worker before every window, and **rows arriving before the restart are lost to G2 permanently**. D has no such window.
  - **DEC-035's residual gap does not arise.** The G3 loader (P6-T04) sets `source='lab'` explicitly and never passes through `soar.pipeline._derive_source`, so the "a bare `lab` row derives as `replay`" hole DEC-035 named is not reachable here.
  - **What it buys P8:** the pilot export can exclude `source='lab'` rows, which is the only reason a tag exists at all.
  - **Timing, and it is not tight.** T05 is written for D and dispatchable today; the mechanism must be on `main` before the first scenario on **22/09**. A ruling for B or C would have cost a re-cut of T05 plus a migration card inside the 20–21/09 buffer.
  - **Merge-cadence check (DEC-043).** A mechanism choice; no standing equality across branches.
Supersedes: — (closes the choice DEC-035 left to P6)

## DEC-086 · 2026-09-16 · G1's sampling design: **take every crit+high cluster, cap `unknown` at 40 %, category floor 25** — and every rate reported from it must say "stratified, not random"
Scope: **evaluation validity**
Decided by: **Owner** (`README.md:49`) — DEC-057 capped the `unknown` stratum without naming a number, and inventory A1 left "take every crit+high" explicitly to the Owner
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `INBOX.md` (2026-09-16 · P6 / G1 sampling design → Resolved) · `tasks/P6/P6-T01.prompt.md` (the three CLI defaults) · **`prompts/P7.md:18`** (the metrics line) · **`prompts/P8.md`** (limitations) · **DEC-057** (its unnamed cap now has a value) · **DEC-084** (the fold this samples from) · **DEC-053** (the loopback exclusion that defines the pool)
Context: measured 16/09 on the DEC-084 fold — `eval/dedup_verify.py`, **3,051** clusters, minus DEC-053's **67** loopback `ssh_brute_force` clusters = a pool of **2,984**. In that pool: crit+high **137** (`unknown` 94 · `ssh_brute_force` 12 · `privilege_escalation` 31 · `suspicious_login` 0); `unknown` **1,801**. Every figure below I re-derived before recording it.
Decision: **Option A** — `--take-all-crit-high --unknown-cap-pct 40 --category-floor 25`, giving **G1 = `unknown` 120 (14 crit · 80 high · 13 med · 13 low) · `ssh_brute_force` 115 (1 · 11 · 95 · 8) · `suspicious_login` 25 (0 · 0 · 0 · 25) · `privilege_escalation` 40 (0 · 31 · 1 · 8)** = 300.
Consequences:
  - **What the choice actually does, in the terms P7 and P8 inherit — this is the part that must not be lost.** The **137** crit+high clusters are **4.6 % of the 2,984-cluster pool** and **45.7 % of the 300-cluster sample**: a **≈ 10× oversample** (9.9×, computed). It is deliberate, because **recall(escalate) is the headline metric** (`prompts/P7.md:18`) and at a proportional draw the sample would hold ≈ 14 crit+high clusters, where A1's tables show no B4-vs-B1 difference the estate could plausibly produce is detectable at n ≤ 20. At 137 the escalate pool is ≈ 40–70 positives before G2 adds its attack clusters.
  - **And the other half of the same trade:** the `unknown` stratum goes from **60.4 % of the pool (1,801/2,984)** to **40 % of the sample (120/300)**.
  - **Therefore: every reported figure that reads as an estate-wide rate must say the sample is stratified, not random.** That sentence is not a footnote — it is the difference between "the system escalates X % of alerts" and "the system escalates X % of a sample built to contain ten times the estate's share of severe clusters". It goes in `eval/gold_coverage.md`, in `prompts/P7.md:18` beside the metrics, and in `prompts/P8.md`'s limitations list, **not only in P6-T01's card**.
  - **Arithmetic checks, so nobody re-derives them under time pressure:** 137 + (120−94) + … the composition sums to **300**; the crit+high entering the sample sum to **94 + 12 + 0 + 31 = 137**, i.e. *every* crit+high cluster in the pool; 3,051 − 67 = **2,984**.
  - **The alternatives, and why they lose.** Alternative 1 (proportional, cap 20 %) yields ≈ 14 crit+high and lets `ssh_brute_force` medium — 817 clusters — dominate the sample. Alternative 2 (cap 33 %) puts the cap at 99, **below** the 94 `unknown` crit+high plus any medium/low, so the tool reports a collision and draws only 5 medium/low `unknown`. Neither is wrong; both answer a different question than recall(escalate).
  - **Cheap to revisit.** The composition is one flag away and a re-run is one command (`P6-T01.prompt.md` note 7) — but **not after the gold set is frozen**: a re-draw after labelling starts creates `gold_v2`, never an overwrite.
  - **Merge-cadence check (DEC-043).** Three CLI defaults and three document edits; no standing equality across branches.
Supersedes: — (gives DEC-057's cap a value; answers inventory A1's open sampling-design question)

Context: the Owner asked for P6-T05 to be dispatched now, correctly: `P6-tasks.md:152-155` says `eval/lab_tag.py` **is** the tag, without it no row is `source='lab'`, and `build_gold.py --g2` refuses to select (exit 3, by design); DEC-085 makes that the mechanism; it must be on `main` before the first scenario on **22/09**, six days out; and the Owner executes that runbook, so they need it in hand first.   - **The measurement, taken at 07:59:21 on 16/09 and re-taken because the branches moved between two commands.** `task/P3-T03` — **0 commits ahead**, worktree clean, **no report file**; nothing produced since its 07:24 dispatch. `task/P3-T07` — **2 ahead**, `P3-T07.report.md` on the branch, worktree clean, tip `0d68887` at **07:57:55**: **reported**. `task/P3-T08` — **3 ahead**, `P3-T08.report.md` on the branch, worktree clean, tip `fb7cc6e` at **07:56:30**: **reported**. Two of the three P3 sessions finished in the two minutes between the Owner's message and this run, which is why the Owner's own figures (`T07 +0`, `T08 +1`) read one commit and two commits low — they were true when taken.
  - **Therefore concurrent coder sessions are at most two, against a cap of three.** A reported coder is a finished coder; T07's and T08's sessions are spent. The Owner asked me to judge whether T03 is early or never opened — **it does not matter here**, because both readings land at or under the cap: live, it is one of two; never opened, it is zero of one.

If, before that merge, T03's session proves live *and* a third P3 card is opened, the authorisation may be drawn once — for P6-T05 and nothing else.
  - **DEC-047 composition check, run anyway because the record needs it.** T03 → `backend/app/security/linter.py` + five `backend/tests/fixtures/linter/*.txt` + `test_linter.py`. T07 → `backend/app/security/detector.py` + `test_detector.py`. T08 → `backend/app/security/gate.py`, `backend/app/security/output_guard.py` + `test_gate.py`. P6-T05 → `docs/lab-scenarios.md`, `eval/lab_tag.py`, `eval/lab_windows.csv`, `backend/tests/test_lab_tag.py`. **Zero shared files and zero shared directories**; the pair is as clean as the Owner said.

One of the card's four files exists; three do not. There was nothing for me to create, and I created nothing.
  - **The defect that finding exposes, and the only thing owed to that coder.** The branch was cut at `d2f4e4e`, **one commit before `ccd0a2a`**, which is where **DEC-085 landed** — the decision that defines this card's entire mechanism. The checkout it is reading does not contain it. `main` adds nothing at any of the card's four paths, so the merge is clean: the coder runs **`git merge main`** in its own worktree before it reports. **I did not run it.** A live coder owns its checkout (`README.md:37`, DEC-010: no agent ever shares a checkout), and the Director running git under a working session is the failure that rule exists to prevent.
  - **Two E1 intakes are now owed** — P3-T07 and P3-T08 both reported and both moved to `review` in `STATE.md` by this run. Neither has a Reviewer prompt yet.

## DEC-090 · 2026-09-19 · The 20/09 ② trigger is inert — it watches P2's gate, which is met — while the slip it existed for has happened; A′/A″ escalated with a recommendation, and Planner P4 runs now, in parallel with P3
Scope: **schedule** (slip > 1 day is the Owner's, `README.md:49`; the Director frames and does not decide the cut) · **task ordering** (Planner P4 now, not after P3 — inside my rights)
Decided by: Director (the ordering and the sweep) · **Owner** (A′ vs A″ — open)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `STATE.md` (the 20/09 KIỂM 1/2 row rewritten; the KIỂM 2/2 row kept and extended with the advisor's `admin` account; the 20–25/09 slot plan under Agent actions) · **`prompts/P4.md`** (swept 19/09 — the DEC-031/DEC-054 treatment; see below) · DEC-071 (the trigger this DEC declares inert) · DEC-088 (the cap the slot plan runs under) · DEC-044 (the class: a guard conditioned on the wrong gate)
Context: DEC-071 (15/09) armed ② to fall "if P2's exit gate is not met by end of 20/09". DEC-079 closed P2 the same evening. The condition is therefore **satisfied and the trigger cannot fire** — while the situation it was built against has occurred by another route: **3.3 days** of no runs (16/09 08:02 → 19/09 16:35), DEC-071's two buffer days (20–21/09) already spent, and — measured on `STATE.md` this run — **P3 due 17/09 at 5/12 `done`** (3 in `review` after this run's row moves — T03, T07, T08 — and 4 `todo`: T09–T12), **P4 due 18/09 at 0 cards** (`docs/plan/tasks/P4/` does not exist), **P5 due 19/09 at 0 cards**. A trigger that watches the wrong gate is DEC-044's class in schedule form.
Decision:
  1. **`STATE.md`'s 20/09 KIỂM 1/2 row is rewritten** to say the condition is met, the trigger is inert, and the slip in numbers. It no longer promises an automatic cut that cannot happen.
  2. **The cut itself is escalated, two options, one recommendation** (the Support Agent has already put both to the Owner; the framing is carried unchanged):
     - **A′ — cut ② now.** §10's first item; digest UI, health job, login and auto-close stay. P5 has zero cards, so nothing built is lost. What it buys: certainty for the Planner P5 brief, the P7 ablation table and `docs/limitations.md`, all of which can then be written this week.
     - **A″ — re-arm the trigger on the right condition:** *"if P6-T02 (the labelling page) is not merged **and** `eval/gold_candidates.csv` is not built by end of 25/09, ② is cut."* If it survives, ② gets **exactly one agent-day, 28/09**, with DEC-032's 60k-token measurement first.
     - **Recommendation: A″.** Not because ② is safe — because A′ and A″ cost the same until 25/09. No ② card exists under either; Planner P5 does not run before P4 under either; the earliest day ② could consume is 28/09 under both. A′ spends the option today for information A″ receives free on 25/09. The cost of A″ is stated so it is not hidden: if ② survives, 28/09 carries one agent-day beside the Owner's gold freeze (agent and human hours, parallel), and the P7 brief must be written with ② as a conditional arm until 25/09. **The Owner decides; until then the trigger reads as A″ in `STATE.md` so that a silent 20/09 does not pass as a decision.**
  3. **Planner P4 runs today or tomorrow, in parallel with P3 — not after it.** The critical path to the 26–27/09 labelling weekend runs through P4: `P6-T02.prompt.md:17,28` needs **P4-T01 (auth), P4-T02 (visibility filter), P4-T04 (base layout)** plus P6-T01 — not all of P4. Order P4 by what P6-T02 needs; T03/T05/T06 after; T07/T08 last.
  4. **`prompts/P4.md` swept before the Planner reads it.** It was last touched 14/09 23:57 (`1d036e6`), cites nothing after DEC-066, and its title still says 10/09 (DEC-041). Added in one dated paragraph: the date (18/09 by DEC-071, past; the brief runs 19/09 beside P3); **DEC-079** (P2 closed — and its "what P2/P3 built" list checked against `main` by line count: `infra/auth.py`, `tier1/{queue,decide,digest,triage}.py`, `audit/llm_runs.py`, `infra/intake.py` are **1-line P0-T06 skeletons**, not code; `web/main.py` 57 lines, `web/worker.py` 38, `domain/transitions.py` 621, `llm/{adapter,builder,schemas}.py`, `security/wrap.py`, `kb/lookup.py` are real); **DEC-082/083** (inventory loaded: `assets` 4 · `identities` 2 · `iocs` 0; the pilot's queue is the replayed corpus — **383 `queued_tier1`** of 95,952 `alerts`, 95,569 `duplicate` — and it is not G1); **DEC-085** (`alerts.source ∈ wazuh|lab|replay` — the `labeling` mode's forbidden-substring test hides all three); **DEC-086** (the admin labels route serves 300 + |G2| clusters); **DEC-088** (three sessions, one held by P6 while P4 runs); **DEC-070** (`INDEXER_URL` is a name, `https://wazuh.indexer:19200`, if any P4 card touches `.env.example`); and item 3's "before the pilot" inventory clause is **done** (DEC-066 executed 15/09, `STATE.md` row XONG). `users` is **0 rows** — the seed CLI is the first thing the pilot needs. `kb/decision_tables/*.yaml`: **10/10 still `reviewed_by: null`** — P4 does not need them; the note says so, so the Planner does not plan around them.
Consequences:
  - **The slot arithmetic the Owner is deciding against.** 20–25/09 = **6 days × 3 slots = 18 slot-days**; P3's remaining 7 cards + P4's ≈ 8 + P6's remaining 4 ≈ **19 cards**, most under 3 h — the Support Agent's ≈ 11 slot-days is plausible and **not re-derived here** (the P4 estimates do not exist until the Planner writes them). What is derived: **every day without three Coder sessions open pushes P6-T02 past 22/09**, and the labelling weekend with it. The review cadence, not the slot count, is the binding constraint: four reviews are owed tonight before any of the four can merge.
  - **What P5 does under either option.** Its non-② items (digest UI, health job, login, auto-close) have no slot in the 20–25/09 plan at all. They are not cut by this DEC; they are **unplanned**, which is a different and worse state, and the Planner P5 brief is the next thing after P4's.
  - **Merge-cadence check (DEC-043).** The rewritten trigger conditions on two artifacts landing in `main` by a date; a merge cannot break it.
Supersedes: — (declares DEC-071's trigger inert; does not cut anything)

## DEC-091 · 2026-09-19 · P6-T05 card defect, bucket (b): the pre-flight's `alerts.json` greps cannot run since the reboot — replaced in the card by the indexer count; the runbook's seven copies are the Coder's to swap, free of a review round
Scope: **card correction** (bucket (b) — the Director's, never charged to the Coder) · a host fact recorded so no later check fails for the wrong reason
Decided by: Director
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: **`tasks/P6/P6-T05.prompt.md:22`** (the two pre-flight commands) · **`tasks/P6/P6-tasks.md:177`** (the index's copy) · **`prompts/reviewer-run-2026-09-19.md` §P6-T05** (one line: the seven runbook copies are (b), not (a)) · `superseded.yaml` (one `fixed` row on the card) · DEC-068/DEC-069 (the checks these commands were written for) · DEC-089 (the run that found it)
Context: measured 19/09: `/data/wazuh/logs/alerts/alerts.json` is `-rw-r----- dnsmasq systemd-journal`, **mode 640**, and `head -c 1` as `user1` fails — the reboot re-created it under the container's uid/gid without the 777 the 14/09 rebuild had left. `P6-T05.prompt.md:22` prescribes `grep -c '"id":"100999"' /data/wazuh/logs/alerts/alerts.json → ≥ 2` and `grep -c '"id":"1003' … → note the number` as the runbook's pre-flight; the Coder followed the card, and `docs/lab-scenarios.md` on `task/P6-T05` carries the file grep in **7 places** (`:56, :60, :92, :93, :584, :647, :711` — pre-flight and the three "Before:" baselines). On 22/09 every one of them fails with `Permission denied`, which is not the answer the runbook is asking for.
Decision: **the card is corrected, not the Coder.** The two pre-flight commands become rule-scoped `_count` queries through `.env`'s `soc_ro` — the source the application reads anyway, and the runbook's own §2 step 5 already uses it (`docs/lab-scenarios.md:154-160` on the branch). Measured this run, verbatim as written into the card:
  `curl -s --cacert "$INDEXER_CA" -u "$INDEXER_USER:$INDEXER_PASSWORD" "$INDEXER_URL/wazuh-alerts-*/_count" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"query":{"term":{"rule.id":"100999"}}}'` → `{"count":523,…}` (≥ 2 holds);
  the same with `-d '{"query":{"prefix":{"rule.id":"1003"}}}'` → `{"count":0,…}` — the negative-check baseline.
Consequences:
  - **The Reviewer classifies the seven runbook copies as (b)** and requires the swap in the same round; the round is free to the Coder (`director-optimized.md`, bucket (b)). A finding that the runbook *also* keeps the indexer path (it does, `:154-160`, `:213`) is not a defence — the "Before:" baselines at `:584/:647/:711` are the file path alone.
  - **No file-based alert check anywhere from now on.** The Director's own gate rows use the indexer count (this run: **7,369** documents in `wazuh-alerts-*`; heartbeat `100999` **523**, latest `09:36:43Z` and `09:26:43Z`, 600 s apart; `1003xx` **0**). `docs/wazuh-manager-changes.md:69-70` still prints the grep — it is the Owner's document and outside `docs/plan/`, so it is named here and not edited.
  - **Not a permissions fix.** `chmod`/`sg wazuh` on the manager's files is explicitly out (`director-run-2026-09-19-restart.md` §7), and would be the wrong fix anyway: the application never reads that file.
Supersedes: — (corrects a card; DEC-068's measurement stands, re-taken on the indexer)

## DEC-092 · 2026-09-19 · Four round-1 reviews in: P3-T08 APPROVE → merged; P3-T03, P3-T07, P6-T05 CHANGES — every blocking finding bucketed, two cards amended, every paste verified by an independent agent and then attacked by a second
Scope: **process** (E2 + E3 result intake, DEC-048 batching; one library-free design rule inside a `security` module — inside Director rights)
Decided by: Director
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `STATE.md` (P3-T08 `done`; P3-T03/P3-T07/P6-T05 `changes` with the bucket and the paste pointer; Owner actions) · **`tasks/P3/P3-T03.prompt.md:37,40`** (design note 5 addendum + acceptance 1, bucket (b)) · **`tasks/P3/P3-T07.prompt.md`** (new design note 6 + acceptance 1, bucket (b) — DEC-092 is the DEC-044 seam with DEC-091 only on `reviewer-run-2026-09-19.md`, which both touched) · DEC-091 (P6-T05's single finding is that decision executing) · DEC-089 (the batch composition that let T08 merge alone) · DEC-025 (every paste carries its red case)
Context: the Owner pasted "Review P3-T08,7,3,5 done. Check it" at 17:41. Review files on the branches: P3-T08 `Verdict: APPROVE` (`e7d6aed`, reviewed commit `fb7cc6e`, **0 code commits after it**); P3-T03 `CHANGES` (`4d47781`), P3-T07 `CHANGES` (`dcf1ca2`), P6-T05 `CHANGES` (`79ad146`) — one blocking finding each. Planner P4 was **live in the primary checkout** throughout (cards written 17:41–17:54, `STATE.md` rows at 17:53, an INBOX item at 17:53), so every agent this run spawned was read-only against its own detached scratch worktree, and no E5 was run — a Planner mid-write is not a Planner finished.
Decision:
  1. **P3-T08 → `done`.** `git merge --no-ff task/P3-T08` → `dcbb19d` (STATE.md the only conflict, resolved to `main`'s row); `make lint` 0; `make test` **722 passed / 1 skipped / 450 deselected / 3 xfailed** (the one red seen first was `test_dispatch_state` invariant 3 firing on a row still `approved` between merge and `done` — the guard working, not the code); `make test-db` **450 passed / 726 deselected**. Committed `39ae8d6`.
  2. **P3-T03 — finding 1 is bucket (a); the gap under it is (b), closed by amending the card.** The false comment (`linter.py:10` names `test_ids_match_the_builders_patterns`; `git grep -n ids_match task/P3-T03` → 3 hits, all prose, 0 in tests; authored in the Coder's `49ff8c7`; the Coder's own report §4 says the guard was "deliberately not added unasked") is the Coder's. The missing drift guard is the card's: design note 3 (`:23`) and design note 5 (`:37`) force a private copy of `builder.ID_PATTERNS` and the test list (`:40`) never pins it. **Resolution: keep the comment, add the test it names** — `tuple(builder.ID_PATTERNS.values()) == linter._ID_PATTERNS` pairwise, the test file imports `app.llm.builder`, the module never does (import ban proven red-then-green in a scratch: `from app.llm import builder` in `linter.py` → `test_real_tree_has_zero_import_violations FAILED`). Card amended: design note 5 addendum, acceptance 1 → **≥ 13 passed**, the test named, its DEC-025 red case (`agent_id` `\d{3,}` → `\d{2,}`) written in. **Round 1 counts as (a)** — had the comment been honest, the round would have been APPROVE with a Director note.
  3. **P3-T07 — finding 1 is bucket (a).** `scan()` guards `block.plain` with `isinstance` at `:288` for the regex loop only; all 8 heuristics get the raw block and read `.plain` unguarded at `:112, :130, :142, :156, :164, :186, :204, :225, :262` — **24/24** (8 heuristics × {`None`, `bytes`, `int`}) raise. Design note 4 (`:42`) says "does not raise on any input (empty block, `None` text, binary garbage)"; nothing in the card commanded the half-guard. **Fix chosen: one guard** — replace `:288` with `if not isinstance(block.plain, str): continue` + `text = block.plain` (verified equivalent to the Reviewer's "coerce inside each heuristic": every heuristic returns `[]` on `plain=""` for `truncated` False and True) + a parametrised `test_scan_never_raises_on_non_str_plain[None|bytes|int]`; measured in a scratch **45 passed**, `make test` 724, lint 0, red case 3 FAILED at `detector.py:112` with the guard reverted.
  4. **P3-T07 — the non-blocking double count is bucket (b), and it is a Planner error the reference exposes.** The card's design note 3(3) calls `hidden_chars` "*ported*, `must`" — but `prompt_guard.py:123-134` has **no** such heuristic; the hidden-chars check lives only inside its `_PATTERNS` (19th pair), which design note 2 ports verbatim. So the card commands the same four code points twice, and one U+200B yields **2** `Finding`s (`pattern='[\u200b\u200c\u200d\ufeff]'` and `pattern='heuristic:hidden_chars'`, otherwise identical — both `m.group(0)`, the single character), `a<ZWSP>b<ZWSP>c` yields 4. `gate.py:176` already stores `findings:N` from that list. **Rule (new design note 6): per block, a heuristic tuple whose `(source, category, matched_text)` equals a regex `Finding` already collected for that block is dropped; the regex one wins; regex findings are never deduplicated against each other.** Keeps `len(PATTERNS) == 20`, `len(HEURISTICS) == 8`, all 42 tests; adds `test_hidden_chars_counted_once` (1 for one char, 2 for two — not 4). Measured under the rule: hidden chars 2→1, 4→2; every other pair unchanged. **Two other same-category pairs exist and are deliberately not touched:** (A) regex 18 `</?(system|instructions?|untrusted_data)>` vs the `delimiter_lookalike` heuristic — each catches shapes the other misses (case, the attrs-shaped forged boundary, fullwidth), matched_text coincides only when the tag ends the block (then it *is* the same occurrence and the rule collapses it — correct); (B) regexes 15/16 vs the `proximity` heuristic — **inherited verbatim from the reference** (`prompt_guard.py:131-133` runs both with no dedupe), matched_text never coincides (phrase vs 120-char window). B stays as the reference behaves; both are named here so P3-T10's measurement and P8's limitations know the `forbidden_action_request` count can carry 2 per qualified `auto-approve`. Rejected: dropping the regex (breaks 20), dropping the heuristic (contradicts note 3 and the recorded `20 8`), a no-op heuristic (DEC-027 anti-pattern), global or all-findings dedupe (under-counts two identical hidden chars), dedupe in the consumer (every reader repeats it).
  5. **P6-T05 — finding 1 is bucket (b), DEC-091 executing.** `git show task/P6-T05:docs/lab-scenarios.md | grep -n 'alerts\.json'` → **7 lines = 5 commands + 2 prose**: `:92/:93` (pre-flight) and `:584/:647/:711` (the three "Before:" baselines, one per `1003xx` rule) are commands and are swapped for the rule-scoped `_count` (`term` `100999`; `prefix` `1003`; `term` `100301/100302/100303`); `:56` quotes DEC-068's 15/09 measurement and is kept-and-marked (the treatment `P6.md:18` and `STATE.md:78` already got); `:60` **is the Permission-denied evidence itself** and is kept. The card at the Coder's merge-base commanded both pre-flight greps and tied them to "the baseline for the negative checks" — the three copies are the card's instruction extended, not the Coder's invention. Round 1 is **free to the Coder**. One more line the review did not name and DEC-091 forbids: `P6-T05.report.md:199` still hands the Owner a morning `grep -c` on the file — swapped in the same round.

## DEC-098 · 2026-09-19 · The KB Drafter: decision-table content is **drafted by an agent and authored by the two humans at the signing sitting** — a recorded deviation from `01-plan.md:77`; card-free contract and its own worktree
Scope: **process** (a new agent role without a task card) · **thesis limitation** (one sentence, P8 item (xi)) · **no evaluation-validity change** — nothing reaches ① before `reviewed_by` is non-null
Decided by: **Owner** (the choice to have the tables drafted; the sitting stays human) · Director (the contract's mechanics)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `prompts/P8.md` (limitations item **(xi)**) · `STATE.md` (Agent actions row for the KB Drafter; the §11 sitting row stays the Owner's) · `prompts/kb-drafter-run-2026-09-19.md` §6 (the text that asked for this record) · DEC-056 amendment 3 (the neighbouring author-as-labeler / author-written-detection limitations) · `01-plan.md:77` (the sentence this deviates from — not edited; the deviation is the record)
Context: `01-plan.md:77` says the ten tables' content is *"authored by Owner + advisor; agent provides format, loader, apply_table, and a consistency test"*. P3-T06 delivered the format, loader and ten skeletons with `reviewed_by: null`; the §11 sitting to fill them has not happened. The Owner has chosen to have an agent **draft** the ten tables from archive statistics and to hold the 90-minute sitting as review-amend-sign; `kb-drafter-run-2026-09-19.md` §6 asks the Director to record that as a DEC and a limitation.
Decision:
  1. **Recorded as a deviation, not a re-wording.** `01-plan.md:77` stands as written; this DEC and P8's item (xi) say what actually happened: drafted by an agent from archive statistics, reviewed, amended and signed by the Owner and the advisor on `<date>` — the sitting is the act of authorship. The chapter carries that sentence beside *author-as-labeler* and *author-written detection*.
  2. **Card-free contract (from `kb-drafter-run-2026-09-19.md` §4–§5, §8):** branch **`kb/decision-tables-draft`** cut from `main`, worked in **its own worktree `../AI_Support_SOC_1_2-kb-draft`** (created 19/09 22:20 @ `6ffbecc`, read back from `git worktree list`) so the session never checks out a branch in the primary checkout; one commit per table plus one for the sheet; deliverables **(a)** the ten `kb/decision_tables/*.yaml` with `reviewed_by: null` intact, **(b)** `docs/plan/kb-review-sheet-2026-09-19.md` (one section per table: rules, evidence, the open question, a `[ ] signed` line; the three PROPOSALS; the two playbook lines that are P3-T12's; the exact command that turns a signature into `reviewed_by`/`reviewed_at` and the test that proves it), **(c)** `docs/plan/kb-draft-report-2026-09-19.md`; **acceptance = §5's eight commands**, including the DEC-025 red case (a contradicting rule → `check_consistency` non-empty) and `git diff main...kb/decision-tables-draft --stat` touching only `kb/decision_tables/*.yaml` and the two `docs/plan/kb-*.md`.
  3. **Not a Coder session.** It has no task card, no `STATE.md` task row and sits outside the three-Coder cap (`HUONG-DAN-VAN-HANH.md:94`); it is tracked under Agent actions. When it reports, the Director runs the eight §5 commands (an E5-shaped check, not a Reviewer round) and the Owner schedules the sitting.
  4. **Nothing it writes is live.** Gate step 4 treats a table with `reviewed_by: null` as absent (`decision_table_unreviewed:<category>`); only a human signature changes that. The draft therefore cannot leak into ① or into P7's B1 arm before the sitting.
Consequences: the §11 sitting remains the Owner's and the advisor's and is now a 90-minute review of a drafted sheet rather than authorship from a blank page; `kb/playbooks/ssh_brute_force.md:34` and `malware.md:36` stay P3-T12's (dispatched tonight) — the drafter names them, does not fix them. Files shared with any running card: **0** (`kb/decision_tables/` vs P3-T12's `kb/playbooks/`).
Supersedes: — (deviates from `01-plan.md:77` and says so)

## DEC-099 · 2026-09-20 · The live cursor survived a real reboot — P2-T11's `pull_once` proved on the hard case; `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1` goes into the operator's paste block, not the Makefile
Scope: **record** (an INBOX blocker cleared by measurement) · **ops** (one env var in a paste block; no product change)
Decided by: Director (the record and (b)); the measurement is the Owner Assist's (`prompts/owner-assist-P6-run-2026-09-19.md` §0), re-taken by the Director
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `INBOX.md` (2026-09-19 · P6 / worker · BLOCKER → Resolved) · `prompts/owner-assist-P6-run-2026-09-19.md:36` (the paste line) · `STATE.md` (the "bật lại worker + puller" Owner action → done) · DEC-076/DEC-079 (the `pull_once` half of P2's gate item 2, which DEC-079 closed on a first live pull and this DEC closes on a reboot) · DEC-089 (which measured the 3,381-document backlog on 19/09 16:40)
Context: DEC-089 found worker + puller dead after the 16/09 22:24 reboot with **3,381** indexer documents newer than the last ingested alert and an intact `source_cursor` (`last_pull_at` 2026-09-15 20:05:33, `last_sort` 1789477112587). The Owner Assist restarted the worker at **19/09 22:21:15** with the §0 paste block (`nohup setsid make run-worker …`, process group 1365593 → `python3 -m app.web.worker` pid **1365598**, own session).
Decision: **(a) recorded — the cursor mechanism holds across a reboot; (b) `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1` is prefixed to the paste block; `make run-worker` is not changed.**
Consequences:
  - **The measurement, the Owner Assist's and mine.** Pull #1 (job 96343, 6,078 ms): `{"hits": 3446, "new": 2936, "duplicates": 0, "heartbeats": 510, "last_sort": 1789831010692}` — **3,446 = 2,936 + 510 + 0**, every document accounted for, none lost, none duplicated; the 60 s overlap produced 0 duplicates because the dedup key already held them. `alerts` `source='wazuh'` **3,941 → 6,875** by 22:24; the `pipeline` queue peaked at 1,196 pending (22:22:05) and drained by 22:22:40 with 0 failed lines. Re-measured by the Director at **20/09 08:43:43**: worker pid 1365598 up **10 h 22 min**, `source_cursor` = `1789868223773 | 2026-09-20 08:43:33 | null`, `alerts` `wazuh` **6,918**, `source_heartbeat` age **10 s**, log tail `{"hits": 2, "new": 0, "duplicates": 0, "heartbeats": 2, …}` — the steady state is heartbeats only. `jobs` pending: `pull` 1, **`triage` 495** (was 383 — the new heads since 15/09 queued for ①; P3-T10's handler drains them). The 3,381 of DEC-089 became 3,446 by the time the pull ran (65 more minutes of heartbeats and noise); the arithmetic is the same claim.
  - **What this closes.** DEC-076 refused P2's gate on `backfill` because `_backfill_loop` neither reads nor writes the cursor; DEC-079 closed it on one `pull_once` in a live process. This is the case neither could show: a process killed by a reboot, a cursor four days old, a single pull that lands everything in between with the same-transaction cursor advance and no double-count. The claim P2-T11 was written for is now measured on the hard case; the lab (22/09) runs on a worker that has been up for a day.
  - **(b), and why not the Makefile.** `-u` in `make run-worker` is a product edit outside `docs/plan/` (`HUONG-DAN-VAN-HANH.md:186`), would need a card, and buys nothing the environment variable does not; `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1` on the paste line makes the JSON log lines land as they happen for anyone tailing the file. One line, in the file the operator pastes from.
  - **Nothing here changes evaluation validity:** the alerts landed as `source='wazuh'` through the same parser and pipeline; G1 comes from the offline fold (DEC-084) and is unaffected; G2's windows start 22/09.
Supersedes: — (extends DEC-079's evidence)

Each branch's STATE row read `approved`; worktrees clean; 0 code commits after the reviewed commits.

The composition run of DEC-096 (924 / 477) was on the same code plus the review files.
  - **The slip.** The row-by-row STATE resolver (DEC-089's mechanism) asserts that a task id occurs once as `| <id> |`; for P6-T01 that string also occurs in **P6-T03's dependency cell** (`| P6-T01 |`), the assertion fired, the script exited **before writing**, and the `git add && git commit` that followed committed `docs/plan/STATE.md` **with its conflict markers** in `a9c667a` (and `e43e5ce` inherited them). Caught by the post-merge marker grep — `grep -c '^<<<<<<<\|^>>>>>>>' docs/plan/STATE.md` → 3 — and fixed by hand in `74a04d9` (main's three rows kept, P6-T01 → `done`). The resolver's guard is now `\n| <id> |` (row start), and the `git add` must never follow a resolver that did not report success: the commit step is gated on the script's exit code from now on. `main` carried markers for two commits and one test run (`make test` reads STATE.md only through `test_dispatch_state`, which parsed around them — a green suite did not catch it; the grep did).
  - **P3's gate (`01-plan.md:10`) after the batch: 2/4 on `main`, and the ① chain is one card from item 3.** `llm/triage.py` (786 lines, 50 tests) is on `main`; P3-T10 (`tier1/triage.py`, `audit/llm_runs.py`, the `triage` handler in `web/worker.py`) is what writes `llm_runs` — **dispatched now**, `../AI_Support_SOC_1_2-P3-T10` on `task/P3-T10` @ `74a04d9`, read back; its five files share nothing with P4-T01, P4-T02, P3-T12 or the kb-draft tree. `jobs` holds **495 pending `triage`** rows for its handler to drain (DEC-099). Then P3-T11 (live ≥ 50) closes items 3 and 4.
  - **Slots.** P3-T12 reported (22:30) so its session is spent; running: P4-T01, P4-T02, **P3-T10** = 3/3. Queue: **P6-T03** (needs P6-T01 ✓ — dispatchable, worktree on the next free slot) → P4-T04 (after T01) → P6-T02 (after P6-T01 ✓ + P4-T01/T02/T04) → P4-T07 → P4-T03 (after T01 + T02).
  - **Follow-ups from DEC-096 that the overnight APPROVEs did not see — none blocks a merge, one has a date.** (1) **`eval/gold_coverage.md` lacks DEC-086's "stratified, not random" sentence** and the generator must write it before the Owner's 25/09 `--g1 --g2` run regenerates the file: **one addendum card for the Planner (P6-T06, ≤ 0.5 h, `must`)** — `build_gold.py` writes the sentence after the "enriched by design" line and a test asserts it; regenerate and commit; the amended card note 5 already says so. Not a hand edit — the four files are generated, never hand-edited (`P6-T01.prompt.md:14`). (2) P6-T01's header-first CSV and allocator-shortfall rulings: conformant as shipped, no action. (3) P6-T04's CGNAT srcip, the corrected `user=root` grep and the vector-2 ruling: conformant as shipped; the corrected grep measured 0/48; P7's brief must report v2 as blocked-by-construction. (4) P6-T04's report `:67-68` stale red-case totals: the Reviewer approved past it; recorded here as a known report inaccuracy, not reopened.
  - **Worktrees:** P3-T09, P6-T01, P6-T04 removed (0 refused); the three `skeptic-*` scratches the dead agents left were removed (`git worktree list` → main, P3-T10, P3-T12, P4-T01, P4-T02, kb-draft).

## DEC-105 · 2026-09-20 · The Owner moved PostgreSQL, the app and the worker under docker compose (20:52); DEC-021's no-docker ruling is superseded for the run/db targets by the same authority; the restart rule, the paste block and every open card's test DSN follow; the frozen native cluster is named as a trap; P3's gate is met 4/4 on the container's `soc_dev`
Scope: **infrastructure record** (the Owner's decision, recorded — `docs/db-docker.md`, commits `5b1deb1` 21:06 and `c526a6a` 21:08, outside `docs/plan/`) · **DEC conflict resolved** (DEC-021 vs the new `Makefile`) · **process** (cards, template, reviewer prompt, the Owner Assist's §0) · **P3 gate** (evening measurement)
Decided by: **Owner** (the cutover; the dump in git) · Director (the propagation, the gate, the recommendation on the native cluster)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `STATE.md` (P3 phase row `done` 4/4; gate-log row 20/09; Owner actions) · `superseded.yaml` (`exclude_completed_phases` + P3) · `prompts/owner-assist-P6-run-2026-09-19.md` §0 (the worker block, compose form) · `prompts/coder-template.md:26`, `prompts/reviewer.md:12` (the TCP test DSN, derived from `.env`) · the eight open cards' Acceptance preambles and 24 DSNs (P3-T11, P4-T03/T04/T05/T06/T08, P6-T02, P6-T06) · `P3-T11.prompt.md:71` (the red case's DSN) · `INBOX.md` (2026-09-20 · P6 / worker · BLOCKER → Resolved) · **DEC-021** (superseded for `run-*`, `up`/`down`, `db-*`, `test-db`, `migrate`) · DEC-099/DEC-102 (the restart rule and paste block, replaced) · DEC-104 (the private-database rule, re-based on TCP) · DEC-047 (the `.env` reading, clarified) · DEC-079/DEC-099 (the cursor's third proof)
Context: the Owner pasted at 21:1x: "PostgreSQL, app and worker run under docker compose since 20/09 20:52 — data migrated (counts identical), `make lint/test/test-db` green on the container (549 db tests), worker resumed from the native cursor with no gap; native cluster kept as fallback only. `make run-worker` now starts the container; DEC-099's restart rule becomes `docker compose restart worker`; cards that say `nohup`/pidfile are stale; docs/plan is yours to update." The Owner Assist's INBOX blocker (cleared by measurement, 21:09) asked for the same amendments and the third cursor proof.
Decision and consequences:
  - **Recorded, and re-measured by the Director at 21:19–21:25.** `docker ps` → `ai_support_soc_1_2-db-1` (healthy), `-app-1`, `-worker-1` up 12 min (the `c526a6a` rename restarted them) beside the three Wazuh containers. Through the `.env` DSN (`127.0.0.1:55432`): `alerts` **98,983** = `replay` 92,011 + `wazuh` 6,972; `source_cursor` `1789913954600 | 14:19:48Z | null`, age **18 s**; `schema_migrations` 17. **The native cluster still answers on the socket: `postgresql:///soc_dev` → 98,972 alerts, frozen at 20:52** (`llm_runs` 1,006 there vs 1,011 live). Cursor continuity: native `1789912039634 @ 20:51:44` → container `1789913240454 @ 21:08:43` (Owner Assist, 5 hits / 0 new / 5 duplicates — the 60 s overlap, no gap) → `1789913954600` at 21:19 (Director). The cutover record in `docs/db-docker.md` §Cutover carries the reference counts (alerts 98,972; llm_runs 1,006; users 4; jobs 100,792; audit_events 100,706; 21 tables · 40 indexes · 81 constraints · 7 triggers · 75 grants — "identical") and the container's `make test-db` **549** — the Owner's figures, not re-run here.
  - **DEC-021 is superseded by the Owner's own hand, and this DEC names both.** DEC-021 (05/09) logged the Owner's ruling that *no `Makefile` target may require docker* — measured then against a host where `docker info` failed. The 20/09 `Makefile` makes `run-worker` = `$(COMPOSE) up -d --wait db worker`, adds `up`/`down`/`db-*`, and `test-db`/`migrate` reach the DSN in `.env`, which is the container. The ruling was the Owner's and the reversal is the Owner's; `run-worker-native`/`run-app-native` keep the 05–20/09 form for the fallback. `scripts/dec_overlaps.py` names no other DEC on the `Makefile` seam. `superseded.yaml`'s `dec-021-02` (backup scheduled by a compose cron entry never fires) stays `fixed` — `make backup` is a host command in both worlds.
  - **The restart rule (DEC-099/DEC-102), new form:** start = `make run-worker` (idempotent; a running worker is left alone); **after any merge touching `backend/app/web/worker.py`, `soar/`, `infra/puller.py` → `docker compose restart worker`** — the code is bind-mounted read-only, no rebuild; stop = `docker compose stop worker`; the three proofs unchanged (`psql "$DATABASE_URL"` — TCP now). **The `nohup setsid … / pidfile / kill -- -<pgid>` block is retired** and must never run beside the container: two pullers on one `source_cursor` corrupt it (`docs/db-docker.md` §Fallback). §0 of the Owner Assist's prompt rewritten accordingly.
  - **Every open card pointed its tests at the wrong database.** DEC-104's private names were socket DSNs (`postgresql:///soc_p4t03_test`) — libpq's socket default is the **native** cluster, which still answers: a Coder following the card would build and test against the frozen copy and see green. Rewritten in all eight open cards (**24** DSNs → `"$TDB"`) with one preamble at the top of each Acceptance section: `TDB="$(sed -n 's/^TEST_DATABASE_URL=//p' ../AI_Support_SOC_1_2/.env | sed 's|/soc_test$|/soc_<task>_test|')"; eval "$(python3 ../AI_Support_SOC_1_2/scripts/dsn_env.py "$TDB")"` — the operator derives their own database's DSN from the primary checkout's `.env` without typing the password, and the `PG*` exports make the fixtures' bare `createdb` reach the container (**proven from `../AI_Support_SOC_1_2-P3-T11`: `PGHOST=127.0.0.1 PGPORT=55432 PGUSER=soc`, a probe database created on the container and dropped**). DEC-047 is not breached: no *test* reads `.env`; the operator does, once, to set a variable. The script path goes through the primary checkout because **every open worktree predates `5b1deb1`** (P3-T11, P4-T03, P4-T04, P4-T08, P6-T06 lack `scripts/dsn_env.py` and the compose `Makefile`) — each open Coder session runs `git merge main` first; the cards say so. `coder-template.md` and `reviewer.md` carry the same form. `P3-T11.prompt.md:71`'s red case named `postgresql:///soc_dev` (now the frozen copy) — amended to the `.env` DSN.
  - **Recommendation to the Owner, not a Director action: stop the native cluster.** A fallback that answers is a trap of the project's own named class — green on a dead copy. `sudo systemctl stop postgresql` (or `pg_ctlcluster 16 main stop`) turns every remaining socket DSN into a loud connection error; start it again only for the §Fallback procedure. The Director changed every DSN it owns; the host is the Owner's.
  - **P3's exit gate (`01-plan.md:10`) is met 4/4, measured on the container's `soc_dev` at 21:25 — the phase closes, three days after DEC-071's 17/09.** (1) linter: `test_linter.py` 13 passed, the four `legacy_*_outside` fixtures rejected; (2) gate: `test_gate.py` 38 passed; (3) **① ran live on 307 replayed alerts with `gate_result` written** (`llm_runs` proposer rows joined to `alerts.source='replay'`, `gate_result IS NOT NULL`) plus 207 `wazuh` heads; `gate_result IS NULL` → **0 of 1,011** (G11); the run was the daemon's option-A drain 15:07–16:03 (Owner Assist: 501 `triage` jobs succeeded · 0 failed; proposer 501 = 494 + 7 `stopped_by='schema'` → `unavailable`; verifier 494 = 472 + 22 `schema`; 494 `ready`; `sum(cost_usd)` $0.2767 then, $0.2858 now with the heads since); (4) cost recorded: `cost_usd` on **1,011 of 1,011** rows, min $0.00006, max $0.00109. **P3-T11 stays open as work** — the repeatable live harness and the deletion of the legacy `llm/` directory are deliverables; the gate is measured on `llm_runs`, not on the harness (the P2-T07 precedent, DEC-079). `superseded.yaml` gains `P3` in `exclude_completed_phases` (the scan test demands it; P3-T11's card is reviewed against its text, not the scan). No cut.
  - **The dump in git.** `backups/latest.dump` (32,566,681 bytes; real alerts, argon2id hashes) is tracked on a public remote — the Owner's decision, recorded here with its cost (each changed dump adds ~32 MB of history; the hashes are not reversible but are real account material). Not the Director's to reverse.
  - **Untracked and outside `docs/plan/`, not committed by the Director:** `docs/kb-sitting/` (five sitting documents, 15:13) and `docs/lab-agent-check-2026-09-20.md` (the ClamAV/auditd halves of P6 item (2), paste blocks for the Owner). Named on the board.
Supersedes: **DEC-021** (the no-docker ruling, for the run/db/test-db/migrate targets; the `make` exit-code finding stands) · DEC-099/DEC-102's process-based restart rule and paste block · DEC-104's socket-form private names

## DEC-106 · 2026-09-22 · The host moved to ATTT-M1 and the runbook's "đợt 1" premise broke in the same move: Wazuh is neither left on IA1803 nor migrated from it — a second, independent stack runs on this box and the worker reads it through a new `soc_ro` / `soc_ro_role`. The restore is proven on five counts, 1,372 documents = 1,372 rows with nothing skipped, the live stream carries a 38 h 41 m hole that nothing can backfill, the shared demo CA means TLS cannot tell the two stacks apart, and three host-prep steps of the runbook are measured unmet
Scope: **infrastructure record** (the Owner's move, recorded) · **§6.3 premise** (all four `INDEXER_*` values unchanged; the cluster behind them is a different one) · **runbook correction** (`docs/migration-runbook.md` §3.3, §3.4, §5 — đợt 1's IA1803 premise) · **enrichment/pilot risk** (`Windows_Endpoint` absent from the inventory)
Decided by: **Owner** (the host move, the second Wazuh stack, the scoped account — all §6.3-adjacent infrastructure, `README.md:49`) · Director (the record, the measurements, the runbook correction, the Owner actions)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `docs/migration-runbook.md` (§ header note, §3.3's `INDEXER_HOST_IP` line and the `/etc/hosts` line, §3.4's `make migrate` ordering, §5 items 2/3/7 plus a new item 8 and the old item 8 renumbered to 9, §7's cursor rule and its two new paragraphs) · `STATE.md` (Owner actions: four rows; daily gate log: the 22/09 row) · **DEC-070** (its account scope and `/etc/hosts` fact, amended for this host — no §6.3 value changes) · DEC-065/DEC-001 (the indexer-location chain, continued) · DEC-045 (the worktree list, re-read) · DEC-066 (its inventory ruling, now unmet by a new hostname) · DEC-105 (the container stack, the `TDB` preamble and the `git merge main` rule — all unchanged, re-checked) · DEC-017 (the 4-day retention that closes the backfill window)
Context: the Owner's paste — *"Host moved to ATTT-M1: repo at the same path (ownership fixed, was attt→user1), docker compose up from backup 2026-09-21 (counts matched, cursor continued: alerts 98990/llm_runs 1015/jobs 101011/audit_events 100740). Wazuh is NOT on IA1803 as planned — this box runs its own independent Wazuh stack; worker reads it via a new scoped `soc_ro_role` created on this indexer (read-only, `wazuh-alerts-*` only), not IA1803's `soc_ro`. Old host's compose is presumed down (not independently verified — no network path to IA1803 from here). Re-read `git worktree list` (5 task worktrees + main); record a DEC for the indexer deviation from the runbook's 'đợt 1' plan."* Measured by the Director 20:25–20:5x on ATTT-M1 (`hostname` → `ATTT-M1`, Ubuntu kernel 7.0.0-31-generic).
Decision and consequences:
  - **The deviation named exactly, because it is neither of the two cases the runbook wrote.** `migration-runbook.md:3-6` defines **đợt 1** as: move app + DB + worker + working state; **Wazuh (manager, indexer, dashboard, agent) stays on IA1803**, the new server reads that indexer over the network; and **đợt 2** (moving Wazuh) *"là việc khác, cần DEC riêng"*. What happened is a third case the runbook did not contemplate: IA1803's stack was **not** moved and is **not** being read — a **second, independent stack was stood up here** (`single-node-wazuh.manager-1`, `-indexer-1`, `-dashboard-1`, up 6 h at 20:25, indexer published on `0.0.0.0:19200`) and the worker reads that. **No Wazuh data came across**: this stack's history begins 22/09. **Đợt 2 is therefore still open and this DEC does not close it** — IA1803's manager, its `client.keys`, its four enrolled agents and its indexer volume are all still there, unmoved.
  - **All four `INDEXER_*` values are unchanged, and that is precisely the trap.** `.env` still carries `INDEXER_URL=https://wazuh.indexer:19200`, `INDEXER_USER=soc_ro`, `INDEXER_CA=conf/root-ca.pem`, `INDEXER_INDEX=wazuh-alerts-*` — **the same four DEC-070 verified on 15/09**, now resolving to a different cluster. Inside the container the name resolves through `docker-compose.yml:72` (`wazuh.indexer:${INDEXER_HOST_IP:-host-gateway}`); **`INDEXER_HOST_IP` is absent from `.env`**, so the `host-gateway` default applies and the request lands on this box's own `:19200`. **Runbook §3.3's instruction is inverted by this deviation** — it tells the operator to set `INDEXER_HOST_IP=<IP Tailscale của IA1803>` on any server that is not IA1803, which here would point the puller back at the old stack. Corrected in the runbook rather than left to be discovered.
  - **TLS cannot distinguish the two stacks — measured, and it is the DEC-105 trap class in a new place.** `conf/root-ca.pem`: `subject=OU=Wazuh, O=Wazuh, L=California`, `notBefore=Sep 14 12:47:26 2026 GMT`, `notAfter=Sep 11 12:47:26 2036 GMT`, sha256 `76:7C:77:3C:EA:82:1B:B7:70:33:3E:A0:98:9A:5E:57:51:A1:55:63:5C:31:86:43:B7:54:C3:EA:74:59:73:35` — **the same CA DEC-070 recorded**, and the certificate this indexer serves is issued by it (`CN=wazuh.indexer`, SAN `DNS:wazuh.indexer`, `notBefore=Sep 14 12:47:27 2026 GMT`, one second after the CA). The certificate material was **copied from IA1803's `single-node-config.tgz`, not regenerated** — the generation instants match to the second. Consequence, stated plainly: **a successful TLS handshake proves nothing about which stack answered.** Only the network path decides, and the network path is one unset Compose variable. This is the same shape as DEC-105's frozen native cluster that still answered on the socket — an identity that verifies against the wrong thing.
  - **The account: verified by probe, not taken on report.** `_plugins/_security/authinfo` → user **`soc_ro`**, roles **`['own_index', 'soc_ro_role']`**, backend roles `[]`. The Owner's "`soc_ro_role`" is the **role**; the **account name is still `soc_ro`**, the same string as IA1803's — worth writing down, because `INDEXER_USER` is therefore unchanged too and nothing in `.env` marks which stack it belongs to. Scope measured from the worker container, through the four values the application itself reads:
    | probe | result |
    |---|---|
    | `GET wazuh-alerts-*/_count` | **200**, `{"count":1372}`, 3 of 3 shards |
    | `GET /_cluster/health` | **200**, `cluster_name=wazuh-cluster`, `status=green`, 1 node |
    | `GET wazuh-monitoring-*/_count` | **403** |
    | `GET /.opendistro_security/_count` | **403** |
    | `POST /soc-ro-write-probe-2026-09-22/_doc` | **403** (scratch index name, as DEC-070 did — never a live index, and no `DELETE` probe was run) |
    | `GET /_cat/indices/wazuh-alerts-*` | **403** |
    So the account is **narrower than IA1803's**, which DEC-070 recorded as `cluster_composite_ops_ro` plus read on `wazuh-alerts-*` **and** `wazuh-monitoring-*`. The application does not notice: `INDEXER_INDEX` is `wazuh-alerts-*` and `infra/puller.py:120` only ever `POST`s `_search`. **`eval/indexer_probe.py:249` does notice** — it calls `_cat/indices/{index}`, which this account is denied. Recorded here so that the P0-era probe's failure is read as a deliberate scope choice, not as a broken host.
  - **The restore is proven on five counts against the backup's own file, not against the Owner's message.** `~/soc-backup/2026-09-21/db/counts-before.txt`, last block, `taken_at 2026-09-20 17:13:31.057735+00`: alerts **98,990** (replay 92,011 + wazuh 6,979) · llm_runs **1,015** · jobs **101,011** · audit_events **100,740** · users **4** · `source_cursor 1789924048963 | 2026-09-20 17:13:04.034017+00`. The Owner's four figures match that file exactly. `MANIFEST.md` names HEAD `bddae6e` (2026-09-21 00:09 +07), the tip this checkout is on. `schema_migrations` → **17**; `make migrate` → **`0 applied, 17 already present`**.
  - **The cursor carried across a different document population, and it was safe by ordering — not by design.** This indexer holds exactly **1,372** documents in one index, `wazuh-alerts-4.x-2026.09.22`, spanning **`2026-09-22T07:41:28.868Z` → `13:24:17.507Z`**. The carried cursor (`1789924048963` = 20/09 17:07:28Z) is **older than this stack's earliest document**, so nothing could be skipped, and nothing was: `alerts where source='wazuh' and alert_time >= 2026-09-22T07:41:28.868Z` → **1,372**, exactly the indexer's count; total alerts **98,990 → 100,362** with `replay` unchanged at 92,011, so all 1,372 new rows are `wazuh`. Live state at 20:2x: `last_sort` **1790083457507** — the newest document's own timestamp, i.e. fully caught up — `last_error` null, cursor and heartbeat age **30 s**, worker log steady at `{"hits": 1, "new": 0, "duplicates": 1}`. **The rule this buys, because next time the inequality may not hold:** `search_after` is a high-water mark, so a cursor carried onto a *different* stack silently skips every document older than it. Had this stack been producing before 20/09 17:07:28Z, those documents would be gone with no error and no gap in any log. Any future stack swap either resets the cursor with `PULL_START` (§7's own rule, now also in §3.3) or proves this same inequality **before** the worker starts.
  - **A 38 h 41 m hole in the live stream that nothing can backfill.** Last alert from IA1803: **`2026-09-20 17:00:11.278Z`**; first from this stack: **`2026-09-22 07:41:28.868Z`**; rows in between: **0**. This is not a puller fault and not a cutover loss — the source stopped being read. It cannot be recovered from here: IA1803 is unreachable, and DEC-017 measured that indexer's retention at **4 days**, so the window closes around **24/09** even if a path appears. **G1 is untouched** — frozen at 08/08–07/09 by DEC-066, and `replay` is byte-identical at 92,011 — so the gold set and every evaluation built on it are unaffected. What the hole costs is **live-stream continuity for the P4 pilot and for any lab scenario dated 20–22/09** (`docs/lab-run-log.md`, `docs/lab-scenarios.md`): a scenario run on IA1803 in that window has no alert rows on this host and never will.
  - **86 % of the live stream now comes from a host that is not in the inventory — the exact case DEC-066 ruled on, re-opened by a new name.** Agents on this stack: **`Windows_Endpoint` 1,186** and `wazuh.manager` 186, of 1,372. `conf/inventory.yaml` carries `IA1803`, `user1-IA1803`, `wazuh.manager` (`high`) and `HR-computer` (`medium`) — **`Windows_Endpoint` is absent**. DEC-066's reasoning applies verbatim (*"Không thêm thì G8′ ghim `needs_review` 79 % luồng sống và sản lượng auto-close của pilot ≈ 0"*), and it now bites on 86 % of what arrives. The file is git-ignored and the Owner's; this is an Owner action, below, not a Director edit. The other half of the same fact: **`HR-computer` (last 18/09), `DC01` (18/09), `kali` (18/09) and `user1-IA1803` (20/09) have stopped reporting entirely** — the estate the inventory describes is no longer the estate that reports, and `DESKTOP-4OQGCVD` (23 alerts, 17–18/09) never was in it.
  - **Three host-prep steps are measured unmet; none of them is a code defect and none was fixed by changing code.**
    **(a) `/etc/hosts` has no `wazuh.indexer` line.** §3.3 requires it *"cho psql/curl/test chạy ngoài container"*. The container is unaffected (`extra_hosts`); the host is not — `.venv/bin/python eval/indexer_probe.py` → `indexer_probe: ConnectError talking to https://wazuh.indexer:19200 — TLS is verified against …/conf/root-ca.pem and cannot be turned off`. One line, `127.0.0.1 wazuh.indexer`, and it needs root: Owner action.
    **(b) `python3` on this box is 3.14.4 and has neither `pytest` nor `ruff`.** The project's toolchain is `.venv` (Python **3.12.13**, ruff **0.16.6**); `Makefile:15` is `PY ?= python3`, so `make lint` fails at `No module named ruff` and `make test` at `No module named pytest` — an import failure, not a red test. With **`PY=.venv/bin/python`** everything is green and matches §5's expected numbers exactly: **`make lint` clean** (`.env.example` 56 keys · `schema.sql` 1446 dòng · `output_schemas.json` == §6.2 · ruff · black 133 files) · **`make test` 1042 passed, 1 skipped, 549 deselected, 2 xfailed** · **`make test-db` 549 passed, 1045 deselected** (no concurrent run — `ps aux` checked first, per the `soc_test` race). Recorded as a **host-prep gap** (§2 installs `python3.12`; on this box `python3` is 3.14), not a `Makefile` change: `PY` is already an override and the `Makefile` is not this host's to bend. Whether `.venv` becomes the default is the Owner's call, below.
    **(c) §3.4 runs `make migrate` one line before `set -a; . ./.env; set +a`.** In that order, on a fresh shell, it fails: `migrate: no DSN — pass one as argument 1 or set DATABASE_URL_OWNER (or DATABASE_URL)`. Measured, then `0 applied, 17 already present` once the environment is sourced. The runbook's own step order was wrong; corrected in place.
  - **Worktrees re-read from the command, per DEC-045 — not from memory and not from the Owner's count.** `git worktree list` → `main` at `bddae6e` plus **P3-T11** (`2135709`), **P4-T03** (`2135709`), **P4-T04** (`97cd993`), **P4-T08** (`300eb65`), **P6-T06** (`2135709`) — five task worktrees, **identical line for line to `~/soc-backup/2026-09-21/git/worktrees.txt`**. Every one still predates `5b1deb1`, so **DEC-105 is unchanged in full**: each open Coder session still runs `git merge main` first, and the `TDB` preamble still derives from the primary checkout's `.env`, whose port is still `55432`. **No card needs re-pointing and none was edited.**
  - **Not verified, and therefore not claimed: IA1803's compose is down.** There is no network path from here — no `tailscale` binary on the box, no route to it; this host's interfaces are `79.79.79.14/23` and `79.79.78.119/23`. The Owner's "presumed down" is recorded as **presumed**. The one-worker rule (`migration-runbook.md:8`) rests on it, so the exposure is named rather than assumed away: if IA1803's worker is in fact alive it writes to **its own** database and **its own** `source_cursor` — the two hosts share no database and no indexer — so the failure mode is a **divergent second copy**, not corruption of this one. Confirm when a path exists; until then no action here depends on it.
  - **The working tree, so the next `git status` is not misread.** `Makefile`, `README.md` and `docker-compose.yml` show modified with **zero content change** — mode `100644 → 100755` only, an artifact of the `attt` → `user1` ownership fix. `backups/latest.dump` is genuinely different (32,566,681 → **32,978,015** bytes): the 21/09 dump copied in per §3.4, which is what that step instructs. Nothing here is work in progress.

## DEC-107 · 2026-09-23 · Morning run on ATTT-M1: two Coder reports sat unread for three days and both pass E1 — the board's `todo` was wrong, not the work; three host-prep gaps found by running the thing (git identity, `python-multipart` in the venv **and** in the image, the native-cluster action discharged); the 23/09 worker blocker closed with a dated predicate; and G2 escalated, because zero of eight lab categories can run on this box
