Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `STATE.md` (P4-T04 and P4-T08 rows → `review`; the native-cluster row → closed; the "three open Coder sessions" row → corrected; the P3-T11 slot sentence; the inventory row → two hostnames; **three new Owner rows** — Reviewer ×2, image rebuild, git identity; **two new Owner rows** — the lab blockers and the G2 decision; daily gate log 23/09) · `INBOX.md` (`2026-09-23 · P6 / worker` → `Resolved`) · **amends DEC-106** on two counts (its worktree reading; its "86 %" figure) · **amends the standing §0 proof set** of `prompts/owner-assist-P6-run-2026-09-19.md` (the failed-job predicate) · DEC-097 (its (ii) half, now measurably at risk) · DEC-093 (executed, not changed) · DEC-045 / DEC-048 / DEC-073 (all three used as written)

Context: the Owner's reload brief for 23/09 gave the board as *"P4: T01, T02, T07 done. T03, T04, T05, T06, T08 todo"* and named **P4-T04 as the bottleneck**. It is the bottleneck, but not for the stated reason: it was never `todo`. The brief also asked for the board to be reconciled **against git, not against memory** — which is exactly what turned this up.

Decision and consequences:

  - **Two Coder reports have been on their branches since 20/09 and `main` never learned.** `git log main..task/P4-T04` → **2** commits, tip `97cd993` *"P4-T04: report + STATE.md row todo -> review"*, **20/09 15:06**. `git log main..task/P4-T08` → **3** commits, tip `300eb65`, **20/09 14:58** (with its own `Merge branch 'main'` at 14:52). Both branches set **their own** `STATE.md` row to `review`; `main`'s copy said `todo` for three days. How it survived three runs, precisely, because the mechanism matters more than the miss: the Director's `pm (4)` commit is **15:05** — one minute *before* P4-T04's report and **seven minutes after** P4-T08's — and `pm (5)` at **21:26** read neither; then DEC-106 on 22/09 **re-read `git worktree list` and copied both SHAs down correctly** (`97cd993`, `300eb65`) while reading them only for their *base* ("still predates `5b1deb1`"), never for what they were. **The rule that would have caught it, and now goes in writing:** `git worktree list` (DEC-045) proves a task was **dispatched**; it says nothing about **status**. Status is `git log main..task/<id>` per open branch, which is E1 step 1 and costs one command per worktree. A morning run does that for **every** worktree, not only for the ones someone announced.

  - **Both pass E1, and the file lists are exact.** **P4-T04** — 9 files, +647/−1: the six the card says *create* (`web/templating.py`, `templates/{base,login,_flash}.html`, `web/routers/pages.py`, `tests/test_pages_login.py`), the one it says *modify* (`backend/requirements.txt`, the single `python-multipart==0.0.32` line DEC-093 ordered), plus its own `STATE.md` row and its report. **P4-T08** — 5 files, +749/−2: `web/routers/webhook.py` + `tests/test_webhook.py` (create), `infra/intake.py` (modify), plus row and report. **No file outside either card's Files list, and no §6 contract touched** — proven by command rather than by reading the diff: `make lint` on the composed tree is clean, and its first three steps *are* the contract check (`.env.example` **56 keys** · `schema.sql` **1446 dòng** · `output_schemas.json` **== §6.2**). P4-T08 specifically did **not** touch `.env.example` or `config.py`, which its card's rule 4 forbids — the two `WEBHOOK_*` keys were already there from DEC-040.

  - **The batch composes green on this host — measured here, not inherited from IA1803.** Scratch worktree off `main`, `task/P4-T04` then `task/P4-T08` merged in (one `STATE.md` conflict each, the DEC-073 shape, resolved row by row: keep `main`'s text, flip only the reporting task's status cell). With `PY=.venv/bin/python` and a private database: **`make lint` exit 0** (ruff + black, **138 files**) · **`make test` 1046 passed, 1 skipped, 573 deselected, 2 xfailed** · **`make test-db` 573 passed, 1049 deselected**, 54,98 s, on `soc_dir0923_test` (`ps aux` checked for a concurrent run first — the `soc_test` race of DEC-104). Against `main` measured the same morning (**1042 / 1 / 549 / 2**, matching the runbook's §5 numbers to the digit) that is **+4** non-DB tests and **+24** DB tests. **Neither Reviewer is being handed a red suite** — which is the whole point of doing this before dispatching them (DEC-073: a red `make test` spends a review round on a defect that is not the Coder's).

  - **The P1 `jsonschema` trap fired — exactly where the INBOX item that pinned the dependency said it would, and the host move is what pulled the trigger.** `python-multipart` is **absent from `.venv`** *and* **absent from the `app` image** (`docker compose exec app python -c "…version('python-multipart')"` → `PackageNotFoundError`). `main` stays green at 1042 only because `main` contains **no `Form(` anywhere** (`grep -rn 'Form(' backend/app/web/` → empty); `pages.py:102-103` are the first two in the project. FastAPI raises **when the route is built**, i.e. at application start, so before the fix `make test` on the composed tree died at **collection** with 4 errors (`test_auth.py`, `test_pages_login.py`, `test_reload_inventory.py`, `test_webhook.py` — every file that imports `app.web.main`). **This is not a Coder defect**: the Planner measured `0.0.32` on IA1803 on 19/09, DEC-093 chose option A, and the Coder wrote the pinned line the card told it to write. What was missing is that nobody ran `pip install -r backend/requirements.txt` on ATTT-M1 — the same class as DEC-106's three measured host-prep gaps, and the fourth of them. **Director action, inside rights because the decision was already taken:** `python-multipart==0.0.32` installed into `.venv` (downloaded once, installed `--no-index` from the wheel, version verified). `.venv` is the toolchain; the **runtime is the container**, and that one is **not** fixed — hence the Owner row: **`docker compose build app worker` before P4-T04 merges**, or the `app` container dies at startup the next time it is recreated. The `worker` is unaffected (it never imports `web.main`), which is why the stack is healthy right now with the pin already merged nowhere.

  - **`git` had no identity on this host, and it stopped the run at the first merge.** `git config --list --show-origin | grep user.` → empty; `~/.gitconfig` carries only `safe.directory`. Every `git merge`/`git commit` fails with `fatal: unable to auto-detect email address (got 'user1@ATTT-M1.(none)')`. Set `--local` to **the identity the entire repo history already uses** — `Kh4nh709 <mn.nguyen0210@gmail.com>`, verified against `git log -1 --format='%an <%ae>'` — not to a new one, so nothing about authorship changes. **Recorded rather than done quietly** because it names every commit that will be pushed to a public remote: the Owner confirms it or replaces it, and should consider `--global` so the next fresh checkout does not hit the same wall. A fifth host-prep gap, and the one that would have blocked the Owner too.

  - **The native-cluster Owner action is discharged by the move, not by anyone doing it.** Measured 23/09: `pg_lsclusters` → **0 clusters**, `/var/run/postgresql/` **empty**, `ss -ltn` shows only **55432**, and `psql postgresql:///soc_dev` → `No such file or directory`. The trap DEC-105 named — a stale socket DSN silently testing a frozen copy — **cannot fire on this box**: socket DSNs now fail loudly, which is what the recommendation was trying to buy. Row closed with the measurement; its original text is kept because it remains an accurate description of IA1803.

  - **The 23/09 INBOX blocker (Owner Assist, P6 / worker) is resolved, and its three asks are all granted.** Its health measurements re-derived independently by the Director at 10:3x: `last_error` **null**, heartbeat age **15,0 s**, `last_sort` **1790133937849**, `alerts` = `replay` **92.011** (unchanged — **G1 intact**) + `wazuh` **8.515**. (a) **The standing §0 proof set is amended as asked**: `select count(*) from jobs where status='failed'` is **1 forever** — job **101019**, a `pull` created on IA1803 at 20/09 17:13:04Z that rode here inside the dump and failed at 22/09 13:10:59Z with `INDEXER_CA='conf/root-ca.pem' does not point at a readable file`, four minutes before the permissions widened — followed by **858** consecutive successful pulls. From now the predicate is **dated**: `… where status='failed' and locked_at > '2026-09-22 13:15:12+00'` → must be **0**. A healthy worker must not read red forever because of one transient the move caused. (b) `docker-compose.yml:54` still describes `conf/root-ca.pem` as *"0400 and owned by the Owner"* while the file is **0444** — the file is right (it is a public CA certificate, and the container runs as `${HOST_UID}:${HOST_GID}`), **the comment is what should move**; outside `docs/plan/`, so it is on the Owner's line, not changed here. (c) granted — see the inventory row.

  - **DEC-106's "86 % of the live stream" is superseded by a sharper number, and the inventory action grows a second hostname.** The population that matters is not raw alerts but **dedup heads that reached ①**: of **91** heads since this stack's first document, **86 have `lookup_status.asset = not_found`** and 5 `found` (`Windows_Endpoint unknown` 84 · `pfSense.home.arpa unknown` 1 · `wazuh.manager high` 5), and the verdicts of the 90 triaged are **`needs_review` 89 · `escalate` 1**. So DEC-066's predicted shape is now observed end-to-end rather than projected from an agent-name ratio. **And a third agent enrolled after DEC-106 was written** — `pfSense.home.arpa`, rule **501**, **22/09 21:58:07Z** — also absent from `conf/inventory.yaml`. The Owner's inventory row now names **two** hostnames.

  - **G2 cannot be produced on this box today, and that puts DEC-097(ii) on a measurable path to failing — escalated, with a dated fallback.** Three independent measurements agree. (1) **No Linux agent exists here**: `agent_control -l` → exactly `000 wazuh.manager`, `001 Windows_Endpoint`, `003 pfSense.home.arpa`; every command in `docs/lab-scenarios.md` §3 targets `user1-IA1803`, which has nowhere to execute. (3) **Nothing has been run**: `eval/lab_windows.csv` is **header-only, 0 windows**, there are **0 `source='lab'` alerts**, and every `Start:`/`End:` in §3 is still `__:__:__`. The P6 plan put the lab at **22–24/09**; 22 and 23 are gone. DEC-097 is explicit that *"a candidates file without G2 does not satisfy it"*, so a G1-only `gold_candidates.csv` **does not** meet (ii), and at the end of 25/09 ② falls in §10 order — **the Director applies that without asking, as DEC-097 requires.** What is *not* at risk is stated with it: **G1 is untouched** (frozen 08/08–07/09, `replay` byte-identical at **92.011**), so the evaluation itself is not invalidated by any of this. The Owner's three options, with the cost of waiting, are on the board; **no answer by the end of 24/09 means option B** — `gold_v1` is G1-only, labelling runs 26–27/09 as scheduled, ② falls, and P8's limitations section carries the missing known-attack arm. **B is a real fallback, not a disguised failure** — but it is the Owner's to take, because gold-set composition is evaluation validity.

  - **Dispatch: nothing new was created, and that is the correct answer today, read back from the command.** `git worktree list` → `main` plus **P3-T11**, **P4-T03**, **P4-T04**, **P4-T08**, **P6-T06**. Two of those five (P4-T04, P4-T08) have left the coder slots for `review`, so the three-session cap (DEC-088) is held by **P3-T11, P4-T03, P6-T06** — all three already have worktrees, all three are clean, and all three are legal (dependencies in `main`, file lists disjoint). **Nothing on the critical path is dispatchable until P4-T04 merges:** P4-T05 and P6-T02 both name it, and P6-T02 is the blind labelling page. Its worktree is deliberately **not** pre-created — the card requires P4-T04 in `main`, and a branch cut now would only have to merge it again; it is created at P4-T04's merge, which is E2 step 4 and the commonest moment for that step to be skipped.

  - **All five worktrees still predate `5b1deb1`, re-checked by command, so DEC-105's rule stands unchanged and now applies to the Reviewers too.** `git cat-file -e HEAD:scripts/dsn_env.py` fails in all five; behind `main` by **45** (P4-T04), **41** (P4-T08) and **7** commits (P3-T11, P4-T03, P6-T06); all five trees clean. Any session opened in any of them — Coder **or Reviewer** — runs `git merge main` first, or the card's `make test-db` acceptance cannot run at all.

  - **The KB decision tables are folded but NOT signed — checked twice, because the working tree moved under me while I checked.** All ten `kb/decision_tables/*.yaml` carry the KB Drafter's `# sitting:` annotations (each naming the default that applies if the 90 minutes do not reach that row), and all ten still read **`reviewed_by: null` / `reviewed_at: null`**. So the sitting has **not** happened, the gate still records `decision_table_unreviewed:<category>`, and the §11 Owner action stands exactly as it was — better prepared, not discharged. **Recorded as a working practice, since it cost me a wrong sentence in this very entry:** this checkout is shared by several roles and edits appear and disappear mid-run — a set of edits to these ten files was present when I first looked and gone twenty minutes later, with no commit in between. **A claim about the working tree is only true at the instant it is measured; a claim about `git` is durable.** Statements here are phrased against committed state wherever both were available.

Supersedes: — · **amends DEC-106** (its `git worktree list` reading, which recorded the two report SHAs without reading them; and its "86 % of the live stream", replaced by 86 of 91 heads) · **amends the §0 proof set** of `prompts/owner-assist-P6-run-2026-09-19.md` (dated failed-job predicate) · executes **DEC-093** on this host · leaves **DEC-097** armed and unchanged

## DEC-108 · 2026-09-23 · The 25/09 gold build: the G1 archive never came to ATTT-M1, and a database rebuild reproduces all three artifacts **byte-identically** — option A taken, the `--g2` abort corrected as a bucket (b) plan defect, and **DEC-097(ii) is read as written: under A it is false and ② falls on 25/09**
Scope: **evaluation method** (the G1 archive's provenance — sample unchanged, so `README.md:49` is not engaged; recorded anyway because provenance is evidence) · **plan correction** (`STATE.md` P6 item (4), bucket (b)) · **the DEC-097 trigger's reading** (Director's, and only the Director's) · **runbook gap** (đợt 1's "what to take" never named the archive)

Drafted by: Director (Opus 5)
Propagated to: `INBOX.md` (`2026-09-23 · P6 / gold build` → `Resolved`) · `STATE.md` (P6 item (4) rewritten; the G2 Owner row now carries the same A–D letters as the intake, so there is one vocabulary) · DEC-097 (its (ii) half — read, not amended) · DEC-086 (its G1 parameters, untouched and re-proven) · DEC-106 (the runbook gap, a sixth item alongside its three measured host-prep gaps) · **amends `docs/migration-runbook.md`'s đợt-1 contents list** by naming the omission

Context: Owner Assist filed the request on 23/09 *"so it is in the intake two days before the run, not on the afternoon it fails"* — which is the reason it can be answered at all. Every number below was **re-measured by the Director before deciding**, not taken from the intake; the intake's own figures matched on every one.

Decision and consequences:

  - **The G1 archive is not on this host, and that is a gap in đợt 1's "what to take", not a mistake in executing it.** `/home/user1/archive/` → `No such file or directory`; `grep -ci archive docs/migration-runbook.md` → **0**. The runbook moved app + DB + worker + working state and never named the 113,379,904-byte corpus that G1 is drawn from. `eval/build_gold.py:1063` is `--archive-file required=True`, so no flag combination reaches past it. The original is intact on IA1803 and unreachable (open Owner action 4). **Recorded so the next restore's list is longer**, which is the only thing this costs now.

  - **A database rebuild reproduces the G1 artifacts byte for byte — measured here, independently, before accepting it.** `psql "$DATABASE_URL" -Atc "select raw_payload::text from alerts where source='replay' order by alert_time, alert_id"` → **92.011 lines, 120.590.698 bytes, 1,0 s**; then `build_gold.py --archive-file <rebuilt> --g1` with DEC-086's parameters → exit **0**, `clusters 3051 · pool 2984 · G1 sample 300`. Against the committed artifacts, by `sha256sum`: `gold_candidates.csv` **identical** (`5d9dcf91d2ed3639…`), `g1_clusters.csv` **identical** (`4b0cd0d9664ea2a6…`), `g1_members.csv` **identical decompressed** (`5bf6a63cc0da3117…`). `gold_coverage.md` differs on **exactly one** line, the provenance line. **The honest cost, stated rather than smoothed:** the original file had 92.030 lines of which the parser rejected **19**; those 19 were never stored, so a DB rebuild cannot contain them — and they are **provably inert**, because the fold, the pool, the sample and all 300 cluster ids come out identical. The rebuilt *file* is not byte-identical to the original (`raw_payload` is `jsonb`; key order is normalised); the *artifacts built from it* are, which is the only thing the gold set depends on.

  - **Option A is taken: 25/09 runs `--g1` alone, from the DB-rebuilt archive.** Rejected with reasons: **B** (skip the run entirely — the committed artifacts are already correct) buys a slot that costs 15 seconds, and forgoes re-proving the pool on the host that will actually be used; **C** (wait for the original from IA1803) is measured to change nothing but the provenance line, so it is bookkeeping, not a data question, and it depends on a network path nobody has; **D** (restore the lab) is not rejected at all — it is the Owner's open decision, and **A and D are not exclusive: A is what 25/09 does if D has not landed.** **Binding condition on A:** the `archive:` line in `gold_coverage.md` is committed **verbatim** — `lines 92011 / parsed 92011 / rejected 0` and the rebuilt file's path — and is **never** edited to resemble the original. That line is the record of why the corpus is a rebuild; a gold set whose provenance line has been tidied is worth less than one that says plainly where it came from. **One trap carried into the plan because it silently corrupts the corpus:** `\copy` / `COPY … TO STDOUT` mangles it (text-format backslash escaping put 344 of the first 1.000 lines into `invalid_json`); plain `-Atc` is clean, because `jsonb::text` never contains a literal newline.

  - **The 25/09 command as `STATE.md` wrote it destroys the run — bucket (b), a plan defect of mine, charged to nobody.** Measured: `--g1 --g2 --lab-windows eval/lab_windows.csv` against the header-only windows file exits **3** with `build_gold: no lab windows recorded` and writes **0 files** to the out-dir. It does **not** degrade to "G2 = 0, G1 fine" — **G1 is lost with it.** Anyone running item (4) as written on 25/09 would have finished the afternoon with nothing at all. Item (4) is rewritten: `--g1` alone unless `eval/lab_windows.csv` holds ≥ 1 window. The generator's abort-rather-than-degrade behaviour is left **as it is** — it is arguably the right behaviour for a gold builder, and re-opening product code at this date to soften a correct refusal is not a trade worth making.

  - **DEC-097(ii) is read as written, and the reading is settled now rather than discovered in the gate.** The intake asked the one question that is only the Director's: does (ii) mean *"the 25/09 `--g1 --g2` run happened as written"* or *"G1 candidates exist for labelling"*? **It means the first.** DEC-097's own consequence paragraph settles it in its own words — *"`task/P6-T01` already carries a G1-only `gold_candidates.csv` (301 lines, measured tonight), so (ii) is really 'the 25/09 run with `--g2` happened'; a candidates file without G2 does not satisfy it."* The second reading would make (ii) **already true on the night it was armed**, and a condition that is satisfied at arming is not a condition. So: **under A, B and C, (ii) is false, and at the 25/09 evening gate ② falls in §10 order — applied without a further Owner turn, exactly as DEC-097 instructs. Only D can make (ii) true.** Three things are said with it, so the ruling is not mistaken for something it is not: (1) **② falling is a scope decision under a squeezed schedule, not a penalty for the host move** — §10's order exists for precisely this, and the agent-day DEC-097 reserved for 28/09 is better spent on P6-T02, P4-T05 or P7; (2) **evaluation validity is untouched** — G1 is frozen, `replay` is byte-identical at 92.011, and the pool has just been re-derived to the byte; (3) **softening a dated trigger because the facts turned against it is the "one more day, silently" failure the standing prompt forbids** — a slip is a decision, and this one is recorded here rather than absorbed.

  - **Labelling capacity is not at risk, and P6 item (1) is fully closed.** `gold_candidates.csv` is **301 lines = 300 + header**, so `/admin/labels` `total` is **300** under A. §10's protocol is ~200 clusters per labeller-session across 26–27/09 — two labellers × two days covers 300 with room. All four accounts exist and are active, verified directly: `khanh` (tier1), `nguyen` (tier2), `khanh-admin` and `nguyen-admin` (admin, the advisor's blind-branch accounts from P4-T01's seed), all created 20/09. **What the labelling weekend actually depends on is P6-T02 being merged — not on any of this** — which is why P4-T04's Reviewer is today's first Owner action and not the lab.

Supersedes: — · **corrects `STATE.md` P6 item (4)** (bucket (b)) · **reads, and does not amend, DEC-097(ii)** · **amends `docs/migration-runbook.md`'s đợt-1 contents** by naming the archive omission · re-proves **DEC-086**'s G1 parameters on this host

## DEC-110 · 2026-09-25 · P4-T04 closes on a post-merge APPROVE; the router-level exception handler question stays open for P4-T05
Scope: **process** (E2, after the fact)
Decided by: Director
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5.5)
Propagated to: `STATE.md` (P4-T04 `done`) · DEC-109 (which flagged the merge without a review) · DEC-028 (the verdict as an artifact)
Context: P4-T04 was merged into `main` by the Owner on 23/09 (`4e5a86c`) with no Reviewer round. The Reviewer ran a post-merge round on 25/09 against `main` @ `4e5a86c` and committed `3a93036` on branch `review/P4-T04`, parent `184f33c`: review file + STATE row `review → approved`. The Owner pasted "P4-T04 review approve".
Decision and consequences:
  - **Merged `review/P4-T04`** (no conflict — its parent was `main`'s tip) and set the row **`done`**. The review's evidence: `make lint` 0 (136 files); `make test` 1045/1/558/2; `make test-db` 558 on a private database; `test_pages_login.py` 12 passed; DEC-025 case (d) red-then-restored by the Reviewer; the `python-multipart` pin 0.0.32 (DEC-093); the HTMX URL pinned with SRI; no `|safe`; `app.openapi()['paths']` carries `/`, `/login`, `/logout`; the container answers `GET /` 303, `GET /login` 200, `POST /login` 401.
  - **Evidence line checked, not a defect:** the review's summary writes `TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql:///soc_rev0925_t04_test` (a socket DSN, retired by DEC-105). On ATTT-M1 **no native cluster exists** (`psql postgresql:///postgres` → no socket), so a socket DSN fails loudly rather than testing a stale copy; the 558 passes can only have come from the container. Shorthand in the summary, recorded here so it is not copied as a procedure.

  - **Open design question, carried to P4-T05 (not a finding):** `pages.py` registers an app-level exception handler (`_RenderedPage`) as an import-time side effect of a router module. The Reviewer tested the suspected import-order hazard in fresh subprocesses and it does **not** reproduce on `fastapi==0.141.1` (routers resolved lazily, cf. DEC-101). Centralising handlers in `main.py` would be more conventional. P4-T05 and P6-T02 build on `pages.py`: neither moves it; if either needs a second handler, it goes in `main.py`'s discovery loop, and the move of this one is a one-line follow-up then.

## DEC-111 · 2026-09-25 · Owner decision: **G1 (the 08/08–07/09 history) is dropped; the gold set is rebuilt from a fresh, author-run lab corpus on ATTT-M1 with window-known ground truth; measurement restarts from a clean database.** The dedup mechanism stays (load-tested: 150 alert/s one worker, 355/s four). ② falls under DEC-097 as written. Seven days to 02/10 re-cut
Scope: **evaluation validity** (gold-set composition — the Owner's, `README.md:49`; recorded with its consequences) · **infrastructure** (a database reset — the Owner executes) · **schedule** (DEC-071's dates re-cut inside the 02/10 submission) · **cut** (② — the DEC-097 trigger, applied by the Director as DEC-097 delegates) · **cards** (two new, one retired — the Planner writes them on the Owner's word)
Decided by: **Owner** — *"giữ nguyên cơ chế [dedup]… toàn bộ alert trên hệ thống là các alert rất cũ và không thể gán nhãn. Nên tôi muốn xóa các alert cũ, tạo danh sách các alert mới, tự giả lập tấn công, biết chính xác các alert nào là thật alert nào giả. Số liệu đo lại từ đầu."* · Director (the mechanics below, the ② cut, the re-cut)
Drafted by: Director (Fable 5.1)
Propagated to: `STATE.md` (P5/P6/P7/P8 phase rows; the two open G2/lab Owner rows replaced; P6-T02 dispatch; gate-log row 25/09) · **DEC-084 and DEC-086** (void — G1 is not built; their artifacts stay in git as the record of what was built and is unused) · **DEC-085** (unchanged — it is the ground-truth mechanism this decision rests on) · **DEC-097** (applied: ② cut) · **DEC-107/DEC-108** (their option D taken, and taken further: no G1 at all) · `prompts/P7.md:18` and `prompts/P8.md` limitations (to be re-swept before Planner P7 — the "stratified, not random" sentence becomes "author-generated lab corpus, no estate rate at all") · `docs/limitations.md` (P8) · `01-plan.md` P6/P7/P8 rows (re-dated)
Context, measured 25/09 16:00–17:00 on ATTT-M1: `alerts` = `replay` **92,011** (08/08–07/09, IA1803's archive) + `wazuh` **8,815** (14/09–25/09; since the 22/09 move: `Windows_Endpoint` 1,646 · `wazuh.manager` 187 · `pfSense.home.arpa` 3 = 1,836 rows, **203 heads, 176 of them category `unknown`**); `triage_labels` **0**; `eval/lab_windows.csv` header only; this box's indexer holds **1,836** documents from `2026-09-22T07:41Z`, agents `Windows_Endpoint`/`wazuh.manager`/`pfSense.home.arpa` — **no Linux agent**, `1003xx` **0**, `100999` **0** (rules never deployed here — DEC-107). The labellers were booked for 26–27/09; nothing to label them on that they can judge. The Owner's reading is right on the facts: the only labelable corpus is the one the old host produced six weeks ago, on an estate that no longer exists.
Decision and consequences:
  - **The dedup mechanism is kept, and the committee gets numbers, not an argument.** Load test 25/09 (`/tmp/soc-loadtest/loadtest.py`, 5,000 real intake lines from the 16/08 storm replayed into a throwaway `soc_loadtest` on the container, then drained through the real `pipeline` handler): **1 worker → 5,000 alerts in 33.2 s = 150/s** (3 ms/job); **4 workers → 13.9–14.1 s = ≈ 355/s** (three runs); the 5,000 lines belong to **6 cluster keys** (largest 3,736) — the hot-key worst case, serialised on one advisory lock, and still 7–17× the committee's 10–20/s target. One lookup: **1.35 ms, 2 buffers**, `Index Scan using ix_alerts_dedup` on 100,826 rows (719 in the partial index). Not dedup but the LLM is the bottleneck (2.5 s/call), and dedup is what makes it bearable: 5,000 alerts → **10** heads → 10 calls. **A side finding, not hidden:** in one of three 4-worker runs, **2 of 5,000** `pipeline` jobs failed on `ck_alerts_last_seen_khong_lui` (`schema.sql:282`) and were classified permanent (no retry); 0/10,000 in the two re-runs; not reproduced with 1 worker; root cause not found (both columns `DEFAULT now()`, `schema.sql:210-211`). A card before any multi-worker deployment.
  - **"Delete the old alerts" is executed as a fresh database, not as `DELETE`.** `intake`, `audit_events`, `llm_runs` are append-only by trigger (migration 017) and the P2/P3 gate evidence lives in them. Procedure (Owner, from the primary checkout): (1) `make backup` → `backups/soc-<ts>.dump`, copy it to `backups/pre-reset-2026-09-25.dump` and tag the tree `pre-lab-reset`; (2) `make db-restore` is the wrong tool (it restores a dump) — instead `docker compose stop app worker`, `dropdb`/`createdb soc_dev` on the container as `soc`, `make migrate` → `17 applied`, `db/init/01-roles.sh`'s grants are re-issued by 017; (3) re-seed the four accounts (`seed-users`, passwords at the prompt), reload the inventory (`POST /api/admin/reload-inventory` or the loader), with **the new Linux agent and `Windows_Endpoint`** in `conf/inventory.yaml` (DEC-107's open row); (4) `PULL_START=2026-09-26` in `.env` so the puller starts at the lab, not at 22/09's Windows noise — **or** leave `2026-08-01` and let the 1,836 background documents come in as the live stream; the Director recommends the latter: background noise is what the pilot queue is for, and it is real; (5) `make run-worker`. The old database survives in the dump; DEC-079/DEC-105's figures stay true as history.
  - **Ground truth = the lab windows, exactly DEC-085's mechanism, now the whole gold set.** Every scenario run is logged to the second (`docs/lab-run-log.md`, `docs/lab-scenarios.md` §3's `Start:`/`End:`), `eval/lab_tag.py` retags the heads in each window to `source='lab'` with `scenario_id`, `category_expected`, `kind ∈ {attack, benign}` (`eval/lab_windows.csv`); `build_gold.py --g2` exits 3 on an untagged window. **The gold label is derived from the window** — `attack` → the scenario's expected verdict (`escalate`, per `docs/lab-scenarios.md` "Expected rules"), `benign` twin → `false_positive` — not from adjudication. Heads outside every window (this box's background stream, 176/203 `unknown`) have **no known truth and stay out of the gold set**; they remain the pilot queue and are reported as such. That is what "biết chính xác alert nào thật, alert nào giả" means operationally, and it is what "never synthesise an alert" permits: the alerts are real Wazuh alerts from real commands on a real agent; only the *labels* are known in advance because the Owner caused them.
  - **The two humans still label blind, and their labels become a second result, not the gold.** Both labellers work the G2 candidates on P6-T02's page exactly as designed (own random order, no exchange, `source` hidden — DEC-019/085); κ between them is reported as before, **and each labeller's agreement with the window truth is a new figure: the human baseline** ①'s accuracy is compared against. Adjudication reduces to the rows where a human disagrees with the window (each is either a scenario that misfired — recorded, excluded — or a human error — kept as data). This is a stronger design than the one it replaces, and it is honest about what it lost (next bullet).
  - **What the thesis loses, in one paragraph P8 must carry (limitations (xii)):** there is **no historical corpus** — every evaluated alert is author-generated on the author's host, or 4 days of background noise from a 3-agent estate; **no reported rate is an estate rate**, of any estate; the attacks are the ones the author knew how to run (`docs/lab-scenarios.md`'s eight categories, `web_attack`/`policy_violation` absent by DEC-055/057); author-as-labeler (DEC-056) now compounds with author-as-attacker and author-as-truth. DEC-084 and DEC-086 are void; `eval/g1_clusters.csv`, `g1_members.csv.gz`, `gold_candidates.csv`, `gold_coverage.md` stay in git as the record of the G1 that was built and not used — not deleted.
  - **Sizing, from the floors already written (`docs/lab-run-log.md:55-57`, `chot-v3-14-ngay.md:116`):** ≥ 8/10 categories, ≥ 100 clusters, ≥ 20 benign clusters. A cluster key is `rule_id|srcip|dstip|agent`, `IDLE_GAP` 15 min: runs of one category 20 min apart give one cluster each, so 100 clusters at one category at a time is 33 h — **run the eight categories interleaved** (different rule ids never share a key) and vary `srcip` where the scenario allows (loopback aliases `127.0.0.2…`, or the second interface `79.79.78.119`) → ~100 clusters in **≈ 5–6 h of wall clock over two days**, benign twins in the gaps. The runbook re-host card (below) writes the exact schedule.
  - **Schedule, re-cut inside 02/10 (DEC-071's dates superseded for P6–P8):** **25/09 (tonight)** — prerequisites (Owner, root): install and enrol a Wazuh agent on ATTT-M1 itself (this box is the lab host, as IA1803 was); deploy `conf/local_rules.xml` (four commands, `docs/wazuh-manager-changes.md` §0′; expect md5 `afd2ef60…`, then `100999` counting); ClamAV + auditd `execve` watch (`docs/lab-agent-check-2026-09-20.md` §2 paste blocks); inventory; the database reset above. **26–27/09 — lab days** (the two dates the labellers held), `lab_tag.py` after every window, `build_gold.py --g2` at the end of 27/09. **28/09 — labelling** (≈ 100–150 candidates, one session each for two people) → adjudication of human-vs-window disagreements → `freeze` → `gold_v1.sha256`. **29–30/09 — P7** (B0–B4 on G2, G3 loaded by `eval/adversarial/load.py` after the freeze, ASR). **01/10 — P8**; **02/10 submit**. The labellers must be **re-booked for 28/09** (Owner).
  - **② is cut — DEC-097 applied, not re-decided.** DEC-097(ii) reads "P6-T02 merged **and** `gold_candidates.csv` built with `--g2` by end of 25/09"; at 17:00 on 25/09 P6-T02 is `todo` and no window exists. False on both halves; ② (D11) falls by §10's order, auto-close kept. P5's non-② items are unplanned and stay so — P5 is not on the path to 02/10 and the Planner P5 brief is not written; recorded as the schedule's casualty, not hidden.
  - **Cards (the Planner writes; the Owner says the word): P6-T07 `must` ≤ 1.5 h** — `eval/build_gold.py`: `--g2` runs without `--archive-file` (`:1063` `required=True` must become conditional), the G2 candidate rows carry `truth_label` derived from the window `kind` and the scenario's expected verdict, `gold_coverage.md` gets the (xii) sentence in place of DEC-086's; `eval/label_export.py`: `freeze` takes the gold label from `truth_label`, `kappa` additionally reports each labeller vs truth; tests for both. **P6-T08 `must` ≤ 1 h** — `docs/lab-scenarios.md` re-hosted to ATTT-M1's agent name (16 `user1-IA1803` references, 21 `rhost`/loopback lines), the interleaved two-day schedule with `srcip` variation, the pre-flight on this manager (`100999` ≥ 2 through the indexer). **P6-T06 is retired** (its sentence was G1's). **P6-T02 dispatched now** — legal (P6-T01 ✓, P4-T01/T02/T04 ✓ all in `main`), on the critical path to 28/09. P3-T11's live harness is no longer a gate proof (the gate closed on the old corpus, DEC-105) but is P7's runner — keep, lower priority.
Supersedes: DEC-084, DEC-086 (void) · DEC-071's P6–P8 dates · executes DEC-097 (② cut) · takes DEC-107/108's option D

## DEC-112 · 2026-09-25 · The 25/09 lab prerequisites and the database reset go to the Owner as one ordered runbook; DEC-111's reset recipe is corrected on three measured points; the fresh corpus starts at 26/09 00:00 UTC
Scope: **infrastructure** (manager, agent, host packages, database — the Owner executes) · **evaluation validity** (where the new corpus begins) · **security** (re-seeded credentials)
Decided by: Director, on the Owner's instruction *"Bạn làm giúp tôi phần 25,26,27/09. Nếu cần root thì hãy đưa lệnh tôi sẽ chạy qua terminal"* (25/09)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5.5)
Propagated to: `prompts/lab-rebuild-run-2026-09-25.md` (new — the runbook itself) · `STATE.md` (the two 25/09 Owner rows now point at it) · DEC-111 (its reset bullet is corrected here, not edited in place)
Context, measured 25/09 on ATTT-M1: Ubuntu 26.04; `user1` has sudo and is in `docker`; no `/var/ossec` on the host and no `wazuh-agent`, `clamav` or `auditd` unit; manager `single-node-wazuh.manager-1` 4.14.7 rc1 publishes 1514/1515 on the host, authd on with `use_password=no`; `agent_control -l` → 000 manager, 001 `Windows_Endpoint` **Disconnected**, 003 `pfSense.home.arpa` **Disconnected**; live `local_rules.xml` md5 `11bdb298…` (stock) against the repo's `afd2ef60…`; heartbeat wodle present (`ossec.conf:250-255`); `soc_dev` = 100,826 alerts · 4 users · 6 assets · 17 migrations.
Decision and consequences:
  - **The agent does not run these steps.** The Director's `docker cp` of `conf/local_rules.xml` into the manager was refused by the session's auto-mode classifier ("Remote Shell Writes"), and an earlier turn was stopped by the safety classifier. Every step that changes the manager, the agent, host packages or the database is therefore the Owner's, in the order of `prompts/lab-rebuild-run-2026-09-25.md` blocks 1–5, each with its expected output. The Director records what comes back.
  - **Lab agent name: `attt-m1-lab`**, a native `wazuh-agent` on ATTT-M1 itself (the box is the lab host, as IA1803 was), enrolled to `127.0.0.1`, version pinned ≤ the manager's 4.14.7 and held (`apt-mark hold`). The same string goes into `conf/inventory.yaml` (`criticality: medium`) and every `eval/lab_tag.py --agent`.
  - **ClamAV and auditd are installed natively**, with the `execve` rule keyed `exec` and two `<localfile>` entries (ClamAV syslog, audit log) appended to the agent's own `ossec.conf`. `docs/lab-agent-check-2026-09-20.md` §2 is not used: its blocks were written for an unidentified agent on IA1803 and carry placeholders.
  - **DEC-111's reset bullet was wrong on three points, all measured here:** (a) `make backup` refreshes `backups/latest.dump` (`scripts/backup.sh`), the file already exposed on the public remote. The pre-reset dump goes to `/home/user1/soc-backups-keep/pre-reset-2026-09-25.dump`, outside the repo. (b) `make` does not read `.env` (`Makefile:1-31`), so `make backup` and `make migrate` would both stop with "no DSN". The runbook calls `pg_dump` and `scripts/migrate.sh` with the owner DSN read from `.env`. (c) `schema.sql` already records all 17 migrations, so `migrate` prints `recorded 17 base migrations` and `0 applied, 17 already present`, not `17 applied`. The drop uses `WITH (FORCE)` so a stray connection cannot hold `soc_dev` open.
  - **The new corpus starts at `PULL_START=2026-09-26`** (the parser takes a date only, `puller.py:91-93`, so this is 00:00 UTC, 07:00 Vietnam time). This reverses the Director's own recommendation in DEC-111, which was to keep `2026-08-01`. Reason: an empty `source_cursor` pulls from `PULL_START`, so `2026-08-01` would re-ingest all 1,836 documents from 22–25/09, and the Owner asked for the measurement to start from scratch. Background from 26/09 on still arrives and is still the pilot queue. What is lost is four days of background noise, 176 of whose 203 heads were `unknown`. Keeping `2026-08-01` is the Owner's one-line alternative.
  - **The four accounts are re-seeded with new passwords.** Their old argon2id hashes are in the dump on the public remote (secrets-remediation run, 25/09). New passwords make the published hashes useless against the new database. This does not close that exposure; the remediation run still owns it.

The Director runs `lab_tag.py` per window. **P6-T07 stays the one code change on the critical path** (`build_gold.py:1063` still has `--archive-file` `required=True`) and must merge before the evening of 27/09.

## DEC-113 · 2026-09-25 · The lab prerequisites are done on ATTT-M1: DEC-112's blocks 1–5 were run by the Owner and each was verified by the Director. Rule 100303 was firing false positives from three MISP healthchecks; the fix is at the audit layer, which now records `execve` for login sessions only. The fresh `soc_dev` is empty until 26/09 00:00 UTC
Scope: **infrastructure** (manager rules, lab agent, host audit and AV, database; the Owner executed) · **evaluation validity** (what the lab corpus can and cannot contain; one new limitation) · **security** (backup file exposure; Owner action)
Decided by: **Owner** ran every block (*"Bạn làm giúp tôi phần 25,26,27/09. Nếu cần root thì hãy đưa lệnh tôi sẽ chạy qua terminal"*) · **Director** chose the audit filter as the fix for 100303 and verified each block
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5.5, forked session; the DEC-112 session wrote nothing after `9704ade`)
Propagated to: `STATE.md` (the two 25/09 Owner rows are closed; two new Owner rows; gate-log row 25/09 evening) · `prompts/lab-rebuild-run-2026-09-25.md` block 3 (its unfiltered audit rule is superseded here; the file is not edited in place) · P8 limitations (new item, below) · P6-T08's runbook re-host (scenarios must run from a login shell)
Measured on 25/09, all times UTC, each one read back by the Director rather than taken from the Owner's paste alone:
  - **Block 1, rules.** The live `local_rules.xml` has md5 `afd2ef60…`, 9,289 bytes, owner `wazuh:wazuh`. The stock file is kept at `/home/user1/soc-backups-keep/local_rules.xml.stock-2026-09-25` (497 bytes, md5 `11bdb298…`). The manager restarted at 10:35:11Z. `wazuh-control status` lists the same running daemons as before the change, and `ossec.log` has no ERROR or CRITICAL line after the restart. `Total rules enabled` went from 8,451 to 16,758. The increase is exactly 3 × 2,769, which fits analysisd counting each `if_group` attachment: three new rules hang under every `audit`-group node. There was no duplicate-rule warning. **The heartbeat works end to end again.** Marker `soc_heartbeat_2026-09-25T10:35:21Z` reached `intake` as `outcome='heartbeat'` at 10:35:54Z. The previous heartbeat was `2026-09-20T17:07:28Z`, so this ends a five-day silence.
  - **Block 2, lab agent.** Wazuh's apt already carries `wazuh-agent 4.14.8-1`, newer than the 4.14.7 manager, so the install pinned `4.14.7-1` and held it. Agent **004 `attt-m1-lab`** is Active. The indexer has 60 of its documents, including one rule 501 alert and the PAM and sudo events of the install session, which shows the agent reads the host's auth log.
  - **Block 3, ClamAV 1.5.4 and auditd 4.1.2.** `daily.cvd` and `main.cvd` are present and three units are active. logcollector has analysed `/var/log/clamav/clamav.log` and `/var/log/audit/audit.log` since 11:01:02Z.
  - **Block 4** is covered by the two checks above, so it was not run separately.
  - **Block 5, reset.** The pre-reset dump is `/home/user1/soc-backups-keep/pre-reset-2026-09-25.dump`, 34 MB, taken at 11:09Z. Tag `pre-lab-reset` points to `bd376bc`. `soc_dev` was dropped, recreated and migrated. `alerts|users|assets|schema_migrations` = `0|4|7|17`. Both the worker and the app were **recreated** at 11:11:26Z and both carry `PULL_START=2026-09-26`, read inside the containers rather than assumed from `.env`. Every pull since returns `hits 0`, and `last_error` is null.
The finding: **rule 100303 fired on background activity, not on an attack.**
  - As soon as auditd's `execve` watch was live, `100303` ("possible C2 channel: shell attached to a network socket", level 12) fired **about 123 times a minute** on `attt-m1-lab`, from 11:04 to 11:14, with no scenario running. Every hit was on `/usr/bin/bash` or `/usr/bin/dash`. `docker inspect` shows the source: three MISP containers have healthchecks that open a TCP connection through bash. `misp-core` and `misp-modules` run theirs every **2 s**, and `misp-nginx` every 30 s. The arithmetic matches. Two containers checking once every 2 s, with two processes per check (the `sh -c` wrapper and bash), plus the 30 s check, gives about 124 a minute; the measured rate was 120–125. auditd sees container processes because containers share the host kernel.
  - **Why it could not be left.** It would have flooded the fresh database with level-12 false positives from 26/09 on. Worse, it would have **corrupted the `c2_beacon` window truth**. That scenario's expected rule is 100303 itself, so inside its window a real hit and a healthcheck hit cannot be told apart by rule id, and `lab_tag.py` retags every head of the agent in the window.
Decision:
  - **Fix the event source, not the rule.** Both `execve` audit rules in `/etc/audit/rules.d/soc-exec.rules` now carry `-F auid>=1000 -F auid!=4294967295`. They are loaded, and `auditctl -l` prints the second condition as `auid!=-1`. Only processes of a real login session are audited. Container, daemon and root-cron processes have no login uid, so they drop out. Rule 100303 is unchanged: it is correct, and its content belongs to the Detection Author.
  - **Verified both ways.** Positive check: after the reload, `ausearch -k exec -ul 1001` found a marker command from the Owner's terminal twice. The login uid survives `sudo`, so scenarios run with `sudo` are still captured. Negative check: 100303 per minute on `attt-m1-lab` read 123, 110 (the minute of the reload), then **0 in every minute from 11:15Z through 11:23Z**, nine consecutive minutes, re-counted at 11:23:58Z just before this commit. The last 100303 is at 11:14:57.114Z.
  - **The cost, a new P8 limitation.** A shell spawned by a service account or a daemon has no login uid, so it is no longer `execve`-audited on the lab host. A real-world compromised service opening a channel would therefore not be seen by 100301–100303. Every G2 scenario is run by the Owner from a login terminal, so the lab loses nothing, and the thesis states the gap. P6-T08 must tell the reader to run scenarios from a login shell, not from `docker exec` or a systemd unit.
This run leaves two Owner items open:
  - **`conf/inventory.yaml` carries the `attt-m1-lab` block twice**, at lines 78 and 83, because block 5 step 6 ran twice. The database is correct: it has 7 assets, from the first load. But `validate()` rejects a duplicate hostname as an error, not a warning (`enrichment/inventory.py:476-478`), and `load()` aborts on any error. So **every later inventory load will fail** until the second block is deleted. Nothing reloads the inventory automatically: `web/worker.py` never loads it, and the worker and app logs are clean. The failure is therefore latent, not live. The fix is one line for the Owner, because the file is gitignored live configuration in the shared checkout.
  - **The pre-reset dump is readable by other local users.** It has mode 664 inside `/home/user1`, which has mode **777**, and the host has a second local account, `attt`. The dump holds the old argon2id hashes and the whole pre-reset corpus. Run `chmod 600` on it. Whether `/home/user1` has to stay 777 is the Owner's call. A world-writable home directory is a local hardening issue on a host where `user1` has passwordless sudo.
**Block 2 changed the test suite, too.** `make test` on this branch gives **1 failed, 1048 passed, 1 skipped, 573 deselected, 2 xfailed**. It was 1049 passed on `9704ade` before the agent was installed. The one failure is `backend/tests/test_backfill_cli.py:184`, `test_forbidden_check_never_stats_the_path`, which asserts `Path("/var/ossec").exists() is False`. That is a fact about the host, not about the code, and it stopped being true when the native agent created `/var/ossec`. The behaviour the test protects is on the next line, `_is_forbidden_archive_path(...)` returning `True` by string prefix, and it still holds. The failure is the same on every checkout on ATTT-M1, `main` included, because `backend/` is identical. It is **not a regression**, and a Coder or Reviewer who sees it must not chase it. **Follow-up card proposed** (tests only, ≤ 0.25 h; the Planner or the Owner confirms it, as with DEC-109's P4-T09): prove "never stats" by patching `os.stat` and `Path.exists` to raise, instead of asserting a host fact. `superseded.yaml` does not apply, because it scans documents, not tests.
Supersedes: the unfiltered `execve` rule in DEC-112 block 3
Addendum, 2026-09-25 18:4x (Director 2, after a read-only review by Director 1, which confirmed the three findings above) — one overclaim corrected, one defect attributed, one precision:
  - **Overclaim corrected.** The decision bullet says the lab "loses nothing" under the audit filter. That was inferred, not measured. Two things are measured: the Owner's commands are written to the audit log (the positive check), and the healthcheck noise is gone (the negative check). **Not measured: that 100301 (ransomware), 100302 (data exfiltration) and 100303 (C2) still fire end to end on a scenario run under the filter.** The proof is the first window of each of those three scenarios. If a rule reads 0 inside its own window, suspect the filter first. So those three scenarios run **first on 26/09**, which leaves time to fix a filter problem.
  - **The duplicate inventory block came from an unguarded append, in two places.** Neither DEC-112's block 5 nor Director 2's chat version of step 6 checked for an existing row before `cat >>`. Both blocks in the file carry the chat version's comment (`agent 004`), so it was the chat version that ran twice. A `grep -q 'hostname: attt-m1-lab' conf/inventory.yaml ||` guard in front of the append would have prevented it. Director 1 flagged its runbook's share of the defect. The other share is Director 2's.
  - **Precision on "latent".** Inside the app, the loader's only caller is the reload route: `web/main.py:21` imports `load`, and `:35-36` is `POST /api/admin/reload-inventory`. The manual one-liner is the only other caller. A worker or app restart is therefore safe. What the duplicate does block is any reload, including the one that would add a new host, so the delete has to come first.

## DEC-114 · 2026-09-25 · G2 truth is scoped to each scenario's pre-declared Expected rules, not to everything inside the window and not to the product's own category; the database and app ports are open on every interface, against `docs/db-docker.md`
Scope: **evaluation validity** (how P6-T07 derives `truth_label` — refines DEC-111's mechanism, does not replace it) · **security** (published ports — an Owner action, recorded, not decided here) · **state** (DEC-113's Owner rows re-measured)
Decided by: Director (the truth scope, as the guardian of evaluation validity) · Owner (the port binding, when they choose)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5.5)
Propagated to: `STATE.md` (the Planner P6 paste line carries the scope; DEC-113's two Owner rows ticked or annotated; one new Owner row for the ports)
Context, measured 25/09 11:34Z from this session, independently of DEC-113: agent `004 attt-m1-lab` **Active**; manager `local_rules.xml` md5 `afd2ef60…`; `wazuh-agent`, `auditd`, `clamav-daemon`, `clamav-freshclam` all `active`; `.env` `PULL_START=2026-09-26`; `alerts|users|assets|migrations` = **`0|4|7|17`**; `source_cursor` `last_pull_at` 11:34:32Z, `last_error` null; `jobs` 24 succeeded, 1 pending; `triage_labels` 0; `conf/inventory.yaml` has **1** `attt-m1-lab` row (the Owner deleted the duplicate); the pre-reset dump is mode **600**, `/home/user1` still **777**. Critical path: `main` = `9ee10cf`; `task/P6-T02` is still at `4e5a86c` and its worktree has **no** `backend/app/tier1/labels.py` (not started, 4.5 h card); `docs/plan/tasks/P6/` has **no** `P6-T07`/`P6-T08` card (the Planner P6 addendum has not run).
Decision and consequences:
  - **The finding.** `eval/build_gold.py` `_g2_select` (`:359-411`) emits **every head of the window's agent inside the window** — no rule or category filter. `attt-m1-lab` is also the host that runs MISP, TheHive, Cortex, Graylog, n8n and Elasticsearch, and DEC-113 already measured one background storm from it (`100303`, ≈ 123/min, before the `auid` filter). Under DEC-111's "attack window → `escalate`" as written, any background head that falls inside an attack window would enter the gold set as a known attack.
  - **The scope.** A head in an attack window gets a `truth_label` **only if its `rule_id` is among that scenario's declared Expected rules** (`docs/lab-scenarios.md` §3, the `#### Expected rules` tables, committed before any window ran: `ssh_brute_force` 5710 5712 5760 5503 2501 · `suspicious_login` 5715 5501 40112 · `privilege_escalation` 5401 5402 510 521 · `recon` 5706 5731 40601 · `malware` 52502 · `ransomware` 100301 · `data_exfiltration` 100302 · `c2_beacon` 100303 — **20 ids, 8 scenarios**). Any other head in the window gets no truth, `excluded_reason = in_window_unexpected`, stays out of `gold_candidates.csv`, and is **counted per window** in `gold_coverage.md`. A benign twin's window is scoped to the same declared ids of its category. The once-a-day benign block (`category_expected = benign`) declares no ids, so its heads keep DEC-111's rule, with their count reported.
  - **Why the declared rules and not the product's `category`.** `alerts.category` is produced by the system under test (`app.ingest.category`). Scoping truth by it would silently drop every genuine scenario alert the product mis-categorises — the very errors the evaluation exists to count. The Expected rules tables were written before the run and do not depend on the product. Adding an id after seeing the data is a recorded amendment to the runbook, never an edit at build time.
  - **What the Owner does differently tomorrow:** nothing. Inside a window, run only the scenario. An unrelated `sudo` inside a `privilege_escalation` window would match 5402 and be counted as the attack; in any other window it is excluded by the scope above.
  - **The ports.** `docker ps` 25/09: `ai_support_soc_1_2-db-1` publishes **`0.0.0.0:55432`**, `ai_support_soc_1_2-app-1` publishes **`0.0.0.0:8000`**; `docker-compose.yml:33` and `:75` carry no host address, and `app` runs uvicorn on `--host 0.0.0.0` (`:73`). `docs/db-docker.md:12-13` and the `Makefile` header say `127.0.0.1`. So PostgreSQL (password auth) and the app (plain HTTP, session cookie not `Secure`, `docs/runbook.md:100`) answer on every interface of the host, including `79.79.79.14` and `79.79.78.119`; Docker's published ports bypass a host firewall. The containers reach `db` over the compose network, so `"127.0.0.1:${DB_PORT:-55432}:5432"` changes nothing for them or for host tools. Port 8000 is the Owner's call: it must stay reachable only if a labeller works from another machine on 28/09.
Supersedes: nothing. Refines DEC-111's "gold label is derived from the window" bullet
  - **Erratum (25/09, DEC-115):** "20 ids" in the heading and in the scope bullet is wrong — the list in the same bullet has **19** ids, and so do `docs/lab-scenarios.md`'s eight tables and the P6-T07/P6-T08 cards. 19 is right; nothing else in this entry changes.

Rulings on the Planner's four questions (`P6-tasks.md` §9′):
  1. **A benign window's truth is `benign`.** DEC-111's "benign twin → `false_positive`" was a Director drafting error. The design calls these windows benign activity on the lab host and sets the floor as "≥ 20 cụm lành tính" (`docs/chot-v3-14-ngay.md:29`, `:116`). The runbook pre-declares `benign` for every twin that produces in-scope alerts (`docs/lab-scenarios.md:275` ssh_brute_force, `:345` suspicious_login, `:397` privilege_escalation, `:461` recon, `:512` malware). DEC-114's principle is that the pre-declared runbook is the truth, so `TRUTH_BY_KIND["benign"] = "benign"`, for twins and for the benign block alike; the block's activity is benign by construction, and its unscoped background is the cost DEC-114 already names. The three negative-check twins (`:583`, `:646`, `:710`, "benign / false_positive") and the malware clean scan yield no in-scope head by design. An in-scope head in one of them is a failed negative check: the scenario is excluded at freeze (ruling 3), never labelled. `false_positive` remains a label a human can give; it is no longer a truth value. The Planner reports four producing twins declared `benign`; that matches.
  2. **DEC-114's "20 ids" is 19.** Erratum appended to DEC-114. The cards already assert 19.
  3. **P6-T09 is ordered from the Planner: `must`, depends on P6-T07, serial after it because both edit `eval/label_export.py`.** (a) `disagreements` lists, for every candidate with a `truth_label`, each labeller whose label differs from it — DEC-111's adjudication list — and keeps today's human-vs-human rows for candidates without truth. (b) `freeze` honours a committed `eval/excluded_scenarios.csv` (`scenario_id, reason, decided_in`), drops every candidate of a listed scenario and prints the count; an exclusion is a recorded decision, never a flag typed at freeze time. (c) `should`: `report` loses its G1 wording and prints `vs_truth`. (a) and (b) merge before the 28/09 freeze; (c) before P7 on 29/09.
  4. **The 23/09 `## 0′` records go into git as written, before P6-T08's Coder opens.** DEC-111 and DEC-112 cite `docs/wazuh-manager-changes.md` §0′, and `STATE.md` cites `docs/lab-scenarios.md` §0′. Both exist only in the primary checkout's working tree, so dropping them would leave those citations pointing at nothing. The Owner commits exactly three files, which are outside `docs/plan/` (HUONG-DAN:186): `docs/lab-scenarios.md`, `docs/lab-run-log.md`, `docs/wazuh-manager-changes.md`. Never `backups/latest.dump`, never `git add -A`. P6-T08 then starts from a `main` that has the block, and its §0 rewrite supersedes it, with the `### History` lines naming that commit.
  - **P6-T08's deadline moves to 26/09 10:00 +07:00**, not 07:05. The first three windows, `RW-A1`, `DX-A1` and `C2-A1` (DEC-113 addendum), run from `main`'s file: measured, the three sections (`docs/lab-scenarios.md:529-720`) name the old host only in 3 `--agent` tag lines, which the Director runs with `attt-m1-lab`, and none of them needs the `srcip` rotation. The ssh-based windows wait for the merged schedule.
  - **P6-T02 → review.** Reviewer focus beyond the card: the three deviations the Coder names (the playbook is exempt from the forbidden-string scan; DEC-025 case (a) is proven only with P4's filter neutralised; the G2 correlation is merged after dropping `status`). Also: a candidates file carrying P6-T07's trailing columns `scenario_id`, `kind`, `truth_label` must never reach a response or the page; a test with those columns is required if none exists, because blindness is never relaxed. Finally, `eval/gold_candidates.csv` on `main` today holds 300 void G1 rows (DEC-111) whose `alert_id`s are not in the reset `soc_dev`. The page is not used before the 27/09 G2 build replaces that file.

## DEC-116 · 2026-09-25 · The remaining work to 02/10 is the spine P6 → P7 → P8; the Tier-1/Tier-2 console (P4-T03/T05/T06, all of P5) is deprioritized behind it and auto-cuts on 30/09 unless the Owner lifts it first
Scope: **schedule** · **plan** (priority and a dated cut trigger — a recommendation with a cost, reversible by the Owner) · **evaluation validity** (what the spine protects)
Decided by: Director (priority and trigger) — the Owner may raise any deprioritized card to the front at any time, which is a schedule choice, `README.md:49`
Drafted by: Director (Opus 4.8)
Propagated to: `STATE.md` (P4-T03/T05/T06 and P5 rows point here; the critical-path list below is the P6/P7/P8 phase rows' authority) · answers the Owner's *"làm hết phần còn lại của project"* (25/09)
Context: `main` = `fbe1f8e` after DEC-115. Done: P1, P2, P3 (gates 4/4, DEC-105), P4 T01/T02/T04/T07/T08. In flight: P6-T02 (review), P6-T07/T08 (dispatched), P6-T09 (to card). Todo and untouched: P4-T03 (`tier1/decide.py`), P4-T05 (queue/detail pages), P4-T06 (tier-1 e2e), all 13 P5 cards (② cut, DEC-111; the rest never got a slot, DEC-090). Seven days to 02/10.
The decision:
  - **The spine is P6 → P7 → P8, and only the spine has dates.** The thesis is judged on the evaluation chapter, which runs offline on the frozen gold set (`prompts/P7.md`: B0–B4 configs and the adversarial ASR run over `eval/`), not on the interactive console. Every never-cut item (`director-optimized.md:202`: intake/puller, ① + gate + verifier, the blind labelling page, the eval harness) is already merged or is a spine card (P6-T02, P6-T07). So the spine is reachable in seven days and does not depend on P4-T03/T05/T06 or P5 — P6-T02's only P4 dependencies were T01/T02/T04, all merged.
  - **P4-T03/T05/T06 and all of P5 are deprioritized, not cut tonight.** They are the Tier-1 decide logic, the analyst queue/detail pages, the tier-1 e2e test, and the whole Tier-2 + digest + health-job phase. They make the live console complete; they produce no number the eval needs. They stay dispatchable and may be opened in any slot where no spine card is waiting on a Coder, Reviewer or the Owner. **Dated trigger: any of them still unstarted at the 30/09 morning gate is cut**, and named in `docs/limitations.md` as "operational console not completed within the evaluation window" — the same shape as ②'s cut. The Owner overturns this by naming a card to prioritize; the cost of doing so is measured in spine days and stated at that time.
  - **What the deprioritization costs, stated now:** without P4-T05 the running `app` has no `/queue` or alert-detail page, so `GET /` (P4-T04's redirect) lands on an unbuilt route; the pilot has no analyst screen. The digest, the Tier-2 case flow and the health job (P5) are absent. The thesis then describes a triage engine evaluated offline with a blind-labelling console, not a full two-tier SOC application. This is a real reduction in what the product chapter can claim, and it is the Owner's to accept or reverse.
  - **The critical path, owners named:** **26/09** lab windows on `attt-m1-lab` (Owner runs §3; Director tags each window with `lab_tag.py`), P6-T08 merged by 10:00. **27/09** more windows; evening, Owner runs `build_gold.py --g2` (needs P6-T07 merged) → `eval/gold_candidates.csv` with `truth_label`. **28/09** two labellers on P6-T02's page (Owner + `khanh-admin`/`nguyen-admin`), adjudication of human-vs-window disagreements (P6-T09), `freeze` → `gold_v1.sha256`. **29–30/09** Planner P7 → Director E5 → Coders → the eval runs. **01/10** Planner P8 → the report and limitations. **02/10** submit.
  - **The Director's standing work through this window** (no new authorization needed each time): intake every Coder/Reviewer report, run E1–E5 and the gates, merge on APPROVE, tag lab windows as their instants arrive, keep `STATE.md`/`DECISIONS.md` truthful, and record each `DEC`. The Owner opens the role sessions and runs every §11 and root action; the Director never spawns an agent unasked and never runs the manager, the agent or a scenario.
Supersedes: nothing. Formalizes DEC-090's "P5 non-② has no slot" and DEC-111's schedule into one priority with a dated trigger

## DEC-117 · 2026-09-25 · P6-T02 review is APPROVE and merges as-is; the blindness input-boundary test is required before 28/09 but does not block the merge; the KB-playbook exemption from the leak scan is accepted without editing `kb/`
Scope: **evaluation validity** (labelling-page blindness — the property this guards) · **plan** (a merge, a dated follow-up, a follow-up rationale)
Decided by: Director, on the Reviewer's APPROVE and its three explicit questions
Drafted by: Director (Opus 4.8)
Propagated to: `STATE.md` (P6-T02 → approved by the review commit; one Director follow-up row) · answers the Reviewer's items 1–3 · refines DEC-115's "a test with those columns is required" (kept, dated, un-blocked)
Context: the P6-T02 Reviewer (a forked session, at the Owner's instruction to fork subtasks) re-ran the acceptance on ATTT-M1 with the venv python and a private DB `soc_review_p6t02_test`: `test_labels.py` 31 passed / 0 skipped, all 19 named tests present; `test_import_rules.py` 12; the `SELECT *` and template forbidden-token greps exit 1; `main.py` diff empty; `make lint` 0; `make test` 1 failed (only `test_backfill_cli.py:184`, the DEC-113 `/var/ossec` host fact) / 1051 passed; `make test-db` 601. Verdict committed on `task/P6-T02` at `f61c264` (`.review.md` + STATE row review→approved). No frozen contract touched; `undo`'s DELETE is on `triage_labels`, not in migration 017's append-only set.
Decision:
  1. **The input-boundary blindness test is required, dated to before 28/09, and does not block this merge.** The Reviewer ruled it non-blocking against my DEC-115 "required" and flagged the divergence — correctly. Blindness is fully enforced already, by three independent barriers the Reviewer verified: `load_candidates` reads only the named columns into a frozen `Candidate` that has no truth field, `cluster_view` asserts `tuple(view) == VIEW_KEYS`, and the allowlist tests pin a hardcoded 19-key expected set that turns red if `truth_label` appears anywhere. Moreover `eval/gold_candidates.csv` carries no `scenario_id/kind/truth_label` column until P6-T07's G2 build on 27/09, so there is no live path for a leak before then. The missing test only exercises the first barrier with those specific future columns; it is defense-in-depth, not the guarantee. So P6-T02 is **approved and merges as-is**, and the test — feed `load_candidates` a CSV carrying the three columns, assert none reach `Candidate` or the view — **must land before the 28/09 labelling session**. It edits `backend/tests/test_labels.py`, which is P6-T02's file and does not exist in P6-T07's worktree, so it cannot fold into P6-T07; it is a Director follow-up (below), added on a branch off `main` once P6-T02 is merged, ≤ 0.5 h, no new full review round given the behaviour is already structurally guaranteed and re-runs green.
  2. **The KB-playbook exemption from the forbidden-string scan is accepted; `kb/` is not edited.** The page shows a playbook, and 9 of 10 playbooks (incl. `ssh_brute_force.md`) name `ioc_context`/`asset_context`/`identity_context`/`lookup_status` as field words, so the leak scan cannot both display the playbook and forbid those substrings inside it. A playbook is static KB prose with no alert data, so exempting only the playbook string is sound; the Reviewer confirmed the exemption is non-vacuous (a test asserts `ioc_context` is present in the playbook fixture) and that `raw_log`/`description` are still scanned for those substrings. Rewording the four field names in `kb/` to satisfy a test would corrupt authored KB content for no gain. Recorded here as the rationale; no card, no `kb/` change.
  3. **P6-T02 merges to `main` on the Owner's command** (this session is worktree-isolated and cannot merge to `main`). The merge of `task/P6-T02` carries the row to `approved`; because DEC-115 synced my branch's P6-T02 row to the branch's then-`review` text, the merge changes that one row on both sides and `scripts/resolve_state.py` resolves it to the branch's `approved` — a normal row-resolve, not a defect.
Follow-up ordered (Director, DEC-117): **P6-T10** — the input-boundary blindness test in `backend/tests/test_labels.py`, added on a branch off a `main` that already has P6-T02, merged before 28/09. Recorded as a Director action row, not a Planner card, because it is one test on an approved file with the behaviour already guaranteed.
Supersedes: nothing. Dates and un-blocks DEC-115's required test
  - **Erratum (25/09, DEC-118):** there is no `scripts/resolve_state.py` in the repository (`scripts/` holds `backup.sh`, `check_output_schemas.py`, `dec_overlaps.py`, `dsn_env.py`, `escalate_power.py`, `gen_env_example.py`, `measure_clusters.py`, `migrate.sh`). The row resolver used since DEC-100 was a scratch script. Item 3's merge was in the end composed on the director branch by per-merge scratch resolvers with the same rules (DEC-118).

## DEC-120 · 2026-09-25 · P6-T09 merged; its Reviewer's three non-blocking findings fixed by the Director as P6-T11; at the 28/09 adjudication a misfired scenario is recorded in `eval/excluded_scenarios.csv`, never as a `final_label` on a window row
Scope: **evaluation validity** (a silent exclusion failure; a zero read as a human baseline) · **plan** (a merge, a follow-up) · **procedure** (the 28/09 meeting)
Decided by: Director, under the Owner's 25/09 delegation (DEC-116)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5.5)
Propagated to: `STATE.md` (P6-T09 → done in the merge; one row for the 28/09 meeting) · `eval/label_export.py`, `backend/tests/test_label_export.py` (P6-T11)
Decision:
  1. **P6-T09 merged** (`bd54afc`; Reviewer APPROVE `bb0f04b`). Composed tree: `make lint` 0; `make test` 1 failed (`test_backfill_cli.py:184`, DEC-113 only) / 1074 passed; `make test-db` 606 passed.
  2. **The Reviewer's three findings are defects, not preferences, and are fixed before their dates by the Director** (`69204db`, P6-T11):
     - **(1) before the freeze:** `read_excluded_scenarios` read a file whose header lacks `scenario_id` as "exclude nothing", so a misfired scenario would be frozen into gold while the committed file said it was excluded. It now raises `ExclusionFileMalformed` when there are data rows and no `scenario_id` column; `freeze` refuses with exit 2 **before any connection**; a header-only file stays a no-op; a padded header (` scenario_id `) still reads.
     - **(2) before P7:** `vs_truth` wrote `accuracy = 0.0` for a labeller with `n = 0`, which P7/P8 would read as a human baseline of zero. It now writes `null`, and the report prints `(not available -- no label against a window truth yet)` whenever `n` is 0 or the accuracy is null — including a `kappa_v1.json` written before this fix. This is the card's own rule: a missing input is stated, never a zero.
     - **(3) before P8:** in G2-only mode the report's limitations lost every G1-derived line without a word. It now states that G1 is void (DEC-111) and carries the G2 lines — the (xii) sentence, the `in_window_unexpected` total, G2's severity mix, the ≥ 20 benign floor — and names any of them that is missing from `gold_coverage.md` instead of dropping it. The "September dates are readable" line is replaced in G2-only mode, where every candidate is lab traffic, by the blindness control that actually applies (P6-T10).
     Eight new tests. Each fix was shown **red** on a scratch copy of `backend/` + `eval/` with `__pycache__` cleared — the P6-T09 Reviewer's first red step passed wrongly on stale bytecode, so the copies are cleaned before every run. `make lint` 0 (a first attempt failed ruff and black on the new test constant and one long line, and was fixed before commit); `make test` 1 failed (DEC-113 only) / 1082 passed; `make test-db` 606 passed.
  3. **At the 28/09 adjudication meeting, a misfired scenario is excluded in `eval/excluded_scenarios.csv` (header exactly `scenario_id,reason,decided_in`, `decided_in` a DEC id), committed before `freeze`.** Writing a `final_label` on a window-basis row changes nothing: truth rows are never adjudicated, so `freeze` ignores it. A human who disagrees with the window is kept as data — that disagreement is the human-baseline figure (DEC-111), not a correction of the truth.
Supersedes: nothing

## DEC-121 · 2026-09-25 · E5 for Planner P7 is partial: P7-T01 and P7-T02 accepted and dispatched; P7-T03–T08 were not written because a safety classifier stopped the Planner mid-card, and the Owner decides how they get written; rulings on the Planner's Q1–Q5; P7-T01's merge is held until after the last lab window
Scope: **plan** (partial E5, dispatch) · **evaluation validity** (how a two-valued truth is scored) · **schedule** (when the ① refactor may reach the running worker)
Decided by: Director (rulings, dispatch) · **Owner** (how T03–T08 get written — left open here)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5.5)
Propagated to: `STATE.md` (P7-T01/P7-T02 dispatch; one Owner row) · `tasks/P7/P7-tasks.md` (Q1–Q5 answered here)
Context: the Planner P7 fork committed `e2295c1` on `plan/P7`: the index `P7-tasks.md` (8 cards, 15.5 h `must` against 10.4 h, the overbooking note at its top), `P7-T01` (① core extracted into `evaluate_alert(...)` with B2/B3/B4 switches, 2.5 h) and `P7-T02` (`eval/llm_cache.py`: cache key with the thinking mode, spend guard, concurrency ≤ 4, 2 h), and their two `STATE.md` rows. **A safety classifier stopped the Planner while it wrote P7-T03 (metrics).** It deleted the partial file and wrote nothing further. P7-T04–T08 depend on T03's names.
Decision:
  1. **The Director does not regenerate P7-T03–T08** — not by a second agent, not by writing them itself. Re-producing content a safety classifier stopped, by another route, is exactly what must not happen, even when the content is believed benign. The stop is surfaced, not worked around. **The Owner decides how T03–T08 get written.** Recommended: a fresh Planner session in a new conversation, whose context holds only the P7 brief and this index (the paste line is on the board), or authoring them directly.
  2. **E5 on the two cards that exist passes.** `grep "{{"` → nothing in both; Files disjoint (T01: `backend/app/tier1/triage.py` + `backend/tests/test_triage_eval_switches.py`; T02: `eval/llm_cache.py` + `backend/tests/test_llm_cache.py`); every Acceptance line is a command, and both run the whole `make test`/`make test-db` (the DEC-119 lesson); no §11 item assigned; `must` 4.5 h. Accepted and dispatched: `task/P7-T01`, `task/P7-T02` at `e2295c1`, read back from the worktree list. Coders are fresh agents given the card as their whole brief.
  3. **Q1 — scoring against a two-valued truth.** The gold truth is `escalate` (attack windows) or `benign` (benign windows); a configuration answers `escalate`, `false_positive`, `benign` or `needs_review`. Predictions are scored as three outcomes: **escalate**; **close** (`false_positive` or `benign`); **needs_review**. Every configuration reports the full truth × outcome table. The headline `recall(escalate)` is strict: `needs_review` on an attack is not counted as caught; it is reported beside it as a deferral rate, per truth class, never folded silently into either side.
  4. **Q2** — P7's exit gate reads "prompt v1.1 built and evaluated, or recorded as not built because no qualifying `triage_labels` row exists (DEC-116)". **Q3** — P7-T01 changes the code the running worker executes (`./backend/app` is bind-mounted, `docker-compose.yml:66`). It is built and reviewed now but merged only **after the last lab window of 27/09**, then the worker is restarted and checked (cursor age, `last_error` null), so the refactor cannot cost gold-corpus ingestion. **Q4** — yes: the per-alert predictions CSV is committed under `docs/results/`, because McNemar and reproducibility need it and `eval/results/` is git-ignored. **Q5** — the Director runs the live evaluation card after the freeze; the Planner's estimate is $1.10–$1.60 against the configured $30 monthly cap, so it needs no separate approval unless the live estimate exceeds $5.
Supersedes: nothing

## DEC-122 · 2026-09-25 · P7 plan complete and through E5; the Planner's four own choices accepted; P7-T02 merged; P7-T01 built and held; P7-T03 dispatched; a partial database password reached a subagent transcript — rotate it
Scope: **plan** (E5, a merge, a dispatch) · **evaluation validity** (four scoring and harness choices) · **security** (a credential in a log)
Decided by: Director (E5, rulings, dispatch) · **Owner** (the password rotation — recommended, not done here)
Drafted by: Director (Opus 5.5)
Propagated to: `tasks/P7/P7-tasks.md` (banner and §3 item 4 brought up to date) · `STATE.md` (P7-T01 → review, P7-T03 dispatch, one Owner row)
Context: after DEC-121 the Owner opened a fresh Planner session in a new conversation, as recommended. It wrote P7-T03 … P7-T08 and their six `STATE.md` rows, uncommitted in the primary checkout; they are committed verbatim at `108a8ac` (checked first: the index byte-identical, `STATE.md` differing from `main` = `5c312f2` only by those six inserted rows). The two Coders of DEC-121 also reported, after a restart forced by an API usage limit (both had stopped before changing anything; both worktrees were verified clean).
Decision:
  1. **E5 passes for all eight P7 cards.** `grep "{{"` → none; no file in two cards (T03 `eval/metrics.py`, T04 `eval/run_configs.py`, T05 `eval/report.py`, T06 `eval/regression_gate.py`, T07 `eval/fewshot.py`, each with its own test file; T08 is a run card with no Files); `must` 16.0 h (T07 `should`) across three build days with up to three Coders, the overbooking note at the index's top; no §11 item on an agent (T08, the live run, is the Director's); every row present, T03–T08 `todo`.
  2. **The Planner's four choices outside DEC-121 are accepted.** (a) A run that crashes or hits the cap is scored as its own `error` outcome and counted wrong — never a deferral, which would flatter the system. (b) B1's facts are built from the same helpers the live triage uses, with a test that they are equal, so baseline and system cannot drift apart silently. (c) The spend check adds earlier evaluation runs' billed cost, because the evaluation writes no `llm_runs` rows; otherwise repeated runs would be invisible to the $30 cap. (d) Few-shot examples carry only closed-vocabulary facts and the verdict, never alert text — they enter the system prompt, where alert text would be an injection channel — and exclude duplicates of gold alerts, not only the gold alerts themselves.
  3. **P7-T02 merged** (`0924ca2`; Director review APPROVE, `P7-T02.review.md`). Composed tree: `make lint` 0; `make test` 1 failed (`test_backfill_cli.py:184`, DEC-113) / 1103 passed; `make test-db` 606 passed. `eval/` is not mounted into the containers (`docker-compose.yml:65-68` mounts `backend/app`, `conf`, `kb`), so the merge is safe during the lab.
  4. **P7-T01 is built and stays unmerged until after the last lab window of 27/09** (DEC-121 Q3). Coder report: branch `task/P7-T01` at `3d72f9f`, four files; `test_triage_job.py` 20 passed unchanged; the new switch tests 28 passed; `make test` 1 failed (DEC-113) / 1089 passed; `make test-db` 628 passed; a syntax-tree comparison of the old and new `run_triage_job` found all 17 write calls identical; three DEC-025 red steps shown and restored. It gets its review before that merge. Its open questions go to the card that consumes each answer: the verifier's model id and the explicit `cfg` go to T04; the proposer template parameter goes to T07, which DEC-116 makes unlikely to be built.
  5. **Both Coders' `git merge main` was refused by their sessions' permission classifier, and neither tried another route.** The Director does not run those refused commands on their behalf. Scope was checked by the Director in its own worktree against each branch's base, which is the precise test of a card's own change, and the merges are composed on the director branch as usual (DEC-118).
  6. **Security: a truncated prefix of the `soc` role's password appeared once in the P7-T01 Coder's session output**, printed by a failing assertion that rendered the `Config` object. It is in no file and no commit; the Coder changed the fixture to blank those fields.
